# Magelan backlog

Agreed with the user on 2026-09-28, for the next sessions. Numbers follow the user's own list.

Ship motion (yaw, pitch, roll on Sea's waves) is being built in a separate session (`ShipMotion.cs`, `ShipCamera.cs`). Anything
that walks, climbs or draws in the ship's frame may need a second look once that lands.

**Update 2026-09-28, late:** that landed and works in the client (Magelan 0.2.0, Sea 0.2.0). Sailing by the wind (part 1:
speed, in irons, tacking, heel from the apparent wind, the helm's wind readout) landed too, in `Sailing.cs`. Files that
session changed: `EntityShip.cs`, `ShipMotion.cs`, `Sailing.cs` (new), `ShipDefinition.cs`, `ShipHud.cs`,
`ShipRenderer.cs`, `ShipSeats.cs`, `ShipClientSystem.cs`, `DeckMover.cs` (`ShipFrame`), `Patches.cs`, `MagelanMod.cs`,
`SelfTest.cs`, `lang/en.json`, the tests and the README. Re-read them before editing. Walking stays in the level ship
frame; things drawn in the ship's frame get its pitch and roll from the model matrix (see "Riding the waves" in the
README). Part 2, drawing the sails' trim, is item 17 below.

**Status 2026-09-29:** items 1–15 and 17 and the build item are done (offline tests and the server self-test pass);
none of it has been seen in the client yet (see "To verify in the client" at the end). What was decided on the way:
- **Item 10, why tar and caulking didn't show:** the texture composites load (the atlas code composites `++` paths
  and the log had no warnings), but the first caulk overlay drew its lines on rows 0, 11 and 22 of 32, exactly where
  vanilla oak planks already have their dark seams, so a caulked deck looked like plain oak; and the first tar
  (multiply) only darkened the planks to about 40 %, which reads as dark oak. Both are stronger now
  (`tools/shipgen/textures.py`). The client now logs the atlas colours of plain, tarred and caulked oak once a session
  (`[magelan] Hull textures in the atlas`). Copper only shows once all 48 plates are on (unchanged).
- **Capstan** at x −3.4 with a clear ring (radius 1.3 checked); the cable comes in through the starboard hawse hole,
  takes a turn round the riding bitts, runs aft through the forecastle bulkhead and along the waist, 2½ turns round
  the barrel, and down a hole to a cable tier coiled on the lower deck.
- **Wheel** smaller (rim 0.36, handles 0.56, their top just under the helmsman's eyes), at x −7.1; the wheel ropes and
  blocks run as described; the tiller swings overhead at the aft end of the lower deck and clears the mizzen.
- **Companionway** in the waist, port side of the hatch: a steep ladder (a climbable path like the ratlines), a coaming,
  a hood over its forward part with a rounded roof and the sliding cover run forward. The waist→castle stairs are
  unchanged (still open: ladders instead?).
- **The ship's boat** takes a vanilla **raft** only: a sailed boat is 8 blocks long with an 11-block mast.
- **The hold:** 1560 slots (82 whole-block chest places on a floor at −1.75, two high, 40 % walkways), a scrolling
  dialog. The hold hatch opens the hold; the cargo is stowed up to just under the lower deck, so nobody falls in.
- **Gates:** our own hinged bulwark sections (planked outside and in on the hull's curve, their own piece of rail cap),
  swinging 100° inboard. From the water only the rope ladders lead aboard; right-clicking the hull near one puts you
  on it, anywhere else says where they are. From a dock you still jump aboard.
- **Captain's cabin:** the placeholder bed is a real bunk (a vanilla bed mesh), replacing the bed spot there; the
  table and stools are props.
- **Item 17:** the main yard touches a topmast backstay at 45°, so `sailing.maxBrace` is 44 in the carrack's JSON; the
  lateen meets its lee shrouds past 31°, so the crew sheets it out no further than 30° (`SailingDef.MaxSheet`, in
  `Sailing.Trim`), and its moving part's `limit` of 30 clamps the drawing too. Braces and sheets are "follow" ropes (see `MovingPartDef`), not per-frame rope meshes.

## Next: the carrack's layout and details

Everything is generated by `tools/shipgen/carrack.py`. Vanilla blocks aboard go through `ShipFittings` and
`ShipRenderer`.

1. **Capstan.**
   - Move it forward to just aft of the mainmast (now at x −4.5, too close to the great cabin door). Leave a clear ring
     round it for walking the bars.
   - Add the anchor cable: from the hawse holes aft along the deck, turns round the drum, and down a small hatch to the
     cable tier. Rope or chain.
2. **Wheel.**
   - Keep the wheel; it's better for gameplay than a whipstaff.
   - Make it smaller: it blocks the view and looks too big.
   - Move it forward, closer to the quarterdeck's front rail.
   - Add the steering gear, visual only:
     - wheel ropes from the drum down through the quarterdeck, along the great cabin's walls under the beams
     - blocks at the sides
     - a tiller swinging overhead at the aft end of the lower deck, above head height, no collision
3. **Companionway.**
   - Move it out of the great cabin; the room is needed for other things.
   - Make it a detailed, steep ship's ladder: treads, handrails, a coaming, and a cover or hood.
   - Place it in the waist, clear of the cargo hatch and the capstan ring.
   - Optional: turn the waist→castle stairs into ladders too. Ask first.
4. **Firebox.** A placeholder for now: a brick hearth under the forecastle with a vanilla firepit mesh on it, and a
   stovepipe. Later it's the spot for real heat and cooking.
5. **Anchor.**
   - Hawse holes in the bow.
   - A cathead and an anchor hanging at the bow that goes down and comes up with the anchor state.
   - Bitts, and the cable run to the capstan.
6. **Pump, boat and hatches.**
   - **Pump:** a bilge pump by the mainmast, with pipes down to the bottom.
   - **Boat:** the ship's boat stowed on chocks in the waist, and pickable (take it off, put it back). Probably a
     vanilla boat or raft item on an attachment spot.
   - **Waist hatch:** raised, with glass panes to let light down.
     - Closed, it's weather-tight: the lower deck stays an enclosed room and it can be shut for storms.
     - It opens as the cargo hatch.
   - **The hold is reached from below**, through its own hatch on the lower deck (see 11), not from the waist.
7. **Mizzen.** Decided: take it all the way down to the keel. It now stands on the main deck inside the great cabin.
   It will pass through the aft end of the lower deck and the hold, with collars where it crosses each deck.
8. **Main top.**
   - The main topsail's foot (y 22.7) blocks the view from the main top (floor 21.5, eyes around 23): raise the topsail
     so its foot is above eye height, about 24+.
   - Give the main top a railing all round (confirmed).
9. **Entry ports and boarding.**
   - The gates should follow the hull's curve and be closed planking: a hinged section of bulwark, not a vanilla fence
     gate with gaps.
   - From the water, only by the rope ladders: remove "right-click the hull anywhere to board". Right-click the hull may
     still put you on a nearby rope ladder, or just say where the ladders are.
   - From a dock or a boat, keep jumping aboard (confirmed).
   - Still open: whether the stairs up to the castles become ladders.
10. **Textures not visible in the client.** Tar on the hull, caulking on the deck and copper sheathing didn't show in
    the 2026-09-28 test.
    - The client log that day (build with 55 fixtures) has no texture warnings, so the composite paths
      (`oak1++3~magelan:block/tar`) loaded without errors but either weren't applied or look too much like plain oak.
    - Check in game what the atlas really holds. If compositing through `GetOrInsertTexture` / shape texture paths
      doesn't work, fall back to baking our own tar/caulk textures, or to `CompositeTexture` with `BlendedOverlays`.
    - Copper only shows once all 48 plates are on; check the test had that.
    - Copper stays an upgrade for speed.
11. **The hold.**
    - A solid double-leaf hatch in the lower deck floor (like a two-leaf door lying on the floor). It opens the hold.
    - For now the hold stays one big storage, sized from its volume, not a chest.
      - Measured from the design: a floor over the ballast at y −1.75 gives about 76 blocks of floor and 2.0 blocks
        of headroom under the lower deck. A floor at −2.0 gives 2.25 headroom but only about 42 blocks, because the
        bottom is narrow.
      - Two chests high with walkways is roughly 90–100 chests, about 1500 slots.
      - Needs a scrolling storage dialog.
    - Later, a real walkable hold with its own deck. That needs a "dry hull" below the waterline:
      - hide the world water inside the hull
      - stop swimming, drowning and underwater fog in there
      - The current water clip only holds waves down to the waterline.
      - **Update 2026-09-29:** Sea 0.3.0 does the water side. The Magelan side is item 18.
12. **Heads.** Detailed latrine seats over the bow (beakhead/forecastle tip).
13. **Castle breaks.** The rail and bulwark now step up 90° at the castle fronts. Make the sheer rise in a slope or a
    rounded sweep, outside and inside planking both.
14. **Stern windows.**
    - Higher and more refined, more luxurious, in the style of a ship of the line's stern gallery.
    - Mullioned frames and maybe stained glass, e.g. vanilla coloured glass textures on our own pane mesh.
15. **Captain's cabin placeholders.** A map table and a bed as visual placeholders from vanilla meshes (a "prop"
    fixture: a vanilla block mesh at a fixed spot, decoration only).
16. **Guns** (added by the user, later): cannons on the main deck, and swivel guns maybe at the bow and stern (castle
    rails). **Waist guns built overnight (0.3.5):** three bronze guns a side firing through ports in the waist, fired
    by a click, with their recoil and shot (see "Built overnight", 4). Laid since 0.3.6 (hold left mouse by one: the
    sixth client test, 4). Still to come: loading, a quoin or elevating block, port lids, the swivel guns.
17. **The sails turn with the wind** (part 2 of sailing by the wind; the user asked for it on 2026-09-28). The crew's
    trim is already worked out on every client each frame, eased: `EntityShip.Brace` for the square yards and
    `EntityShip.Sheet` for the mizzen lateen (`Sailing.Trim`). Both are radians about the ship's up axis (+Y), where +
    turns the starboard end forward and the after end to starboard. Square sails are braced from square (0, running)
    to ±`SailingDef.MaxBrace` (45°, close-hauled), with the lee yardarm hauled aft. (Written "windward yardarm aft"
    at first, which was backwards: the user saw the sails pointing the wrong way, and the sign in `Sailing.Trim` was
    fixed on 2026-09-29.) The lateen swings out to leeward by half the apparent wind's angle (5–80°, now at most
    `SailingDef.MaxSheet` = 30°). What's missing is drawing it, and that needs the model:
    - **Generator (`carrack.py`):**
      - Put each yard in its own skin group, with its sail's attachments and the rigging that only touches the yard
        (lifts, footropes, clewlines), e.g. `part:yard-main-course`, `part:yard-main-topsail`,
        `part:yard-fore-course`, `part:yard-mizzen-lateen`. Now they're all in `exterior`.
      - Add a `movingParts` entry for each yard: the axis point on the mast's centreline at the yard's height, axis
        (0, 1, 0), and a new source `brace` (or `sheet` for the lateen), with the value used in radians.
      - The lateen swings about where its yard crosses the mizzen mast.
      - Ropes with one end on a yard and one on the hull (braces, sheets, tacks) can't turn with the yard.
        - Write them as rope endpoints in the ship JSON: sail, the yard end in yard-local coordinates, the hull end,
          and the radius.
        - The renderer draws them each frame between the turned yard end and the fixed hull end.
      - Check that the yards clear the shrouds and the backstays at full brace. Real ships were limited there; lower
        `sailing.maxBrace` in the JSON if needed.
      - The main topsail is being raised anyway (item 8). Its new yard height goes into its axis point.
    - **Renderer (`ShipRenderer`):**
      - Turn `part:yard-*` by `ship.Brace` / `ship.Sheet` (extend `PartMatrix` sources).
      - Draw each sail (its own shape, furled by `FurlMatrix`) as model × yard turn × furl, so it stays under its yard.
      - Draw the per-frame ropes.
    - **Test (offline):** every yard point stays under its yard at any brace, and no yard end passes through a shroud
      at ±MaxBrace.
18. **Rooms below the waterline: a dry hull** (the user asked on 2026-09-29). **Done 2026-09-29** (offline tests and
    the self-test pass, not seen in the client): the cargo deck at −1.75 with frames and the inside planking down to it,
    a ladder up through the hold hatch (a shut hatch stops you at the top), three lantern hooks under the lower deck,
    `ShipDefinition.DryMask`, `ShipDrySpaces`, dry water clips, `"sea": "0.3.0"`. The cargo fill that stood in the
    hatch well is gone.
    **Then (2026-09-29, the user):** the hull's skeleton, visible: frames every 0.75 from the keelson to the rail (the
    user asked about 1 and 2 blocks; 0.75 keeps a beam on every second frame, and real frames stood about a frame's
    width apart), deck beams under every deck with hanging knees and clamps, a raised keelson in the hold (so the
    cargo deck went down to −2.0: 2 blocks clear over the keelson), the stem and the sternpost. Sea 0.3.0 has the water side
    (see "Dry hulls" in `../Sea/README.md`). Magelan tells Sea where its hulls are dry and builds the walkable hold of
    item 11. Nothing here needs new Sea code.

    Why it matters now, not only for the hold: the world's water blocks stay at the still-water level while the ship
    heaves. In a violent storm's trough (heave down to about −2.4) the eyes of someone on the lower deck (floor 0.5)
    drop below the world's water surface. Without dry spaces they get the underwater fog and the breath bar.
    - **Depend on the new Sea:** `modinfo.json` `"sea": "0.3.0"`.
    - **Mark the clips dry** (`ShipRenderer.AddWaterClips`):
      - `Dry = true`
      - `DryInset = wc.Margin` (the cut is then the hull's measured outline, not the footprint around it)
      - `DryDepth` from the waterline to below the keel (−KeelY + 0.5 ≈ 3)
      - `DryHeight` up to the main deck (DeckY ≈ 3.5, plus a little)
      - Dry clips get no calm ring and no clamp. `RidingCalmZone` and the SeaMotion-widened ring stop mattering for
        them; drop them or keep them for non-dry designs.
      - Look at the waterline in the client afterwards: the waves now meet the hull directly.
    - **Say where it's dry** (`IDrySpaces`, registered on both sides with `SeaSystem.AddDrySpaces`, e.g. from
      `MagelanMod.Start`). Sea asks every tick, from the camera check and for each creature in water, so keep it
      quick:
      - For each ship near the point: `WorldToLocal` (tilted on clients, level on the server), then a lookup in a
        precomputed **inside-the-hull mask** of the design.
      - **The mask** is built once per design in `ShipDefinition.Init`, on the collision grid (0.25). It covers the
        band from the keel up to just under the main deck (y < DeckY − 0.25, where the hull has no openings). Flood-fill
        the empty cells from the grid's outside faces within that band. Empty cells the fill can't reach are inside
        the hull, and so are dry.
      - Hatches, the companionway and doors all open above that band, so they don't matter.
      - **Cabins above the main deck** stand above the water even in a trough, so they need no mask.
      - **The hull skin** (solid cells) counts as dry too, so the check at a player's eye never flickers at a wall.
    - **The hold: an empty cargo deck** (the user, 2026-09-29: "empty cargo deck that can be normally inspected").
      - A plain deck over the ballast at about y −1.75, with ~2 blocks of headroom.
      - You get down there through the lower deck's double-leaf hatch and down a ladder. You walk around it and look
        at the inside of the hull, frames and all: no water, fog or breath bar.
      - Nothing in it for now: no chest spots or fittings. The hold's storage stays as it is (the big storage behind
        the hatch).
      - Lantern hooks under the lower deck's beams would help to see.
      - Walking tests: down and up the ladder, and around the deck without falling through it or leaving the hull.
    - **Tests:**
      - offline:
        - the mask holds every enclosed air cell of the hold and lower deck
        - no point 0.3 outside the hull skin is dry (sample the whole hull at several heights)
        - the dry outline (`WaterClipDef` profile minus margin, at the waterline band) covers every mask cell there
      - server self-test: spawn a chicken in the carrack's hold below the pool's water level. It breathes, and one just
        outside the hull at the same depth doesn't. This is Sea's own check, now with a real hull; see
        `Sea/src/SelfTest.cs`.
    - **Later (Sea + Magelan):** flooding. A per-compartment water level: `IsDry` false below it, and Sea drawing water
      inside the outline up to that level (a `FloodY` on the clip), for leaks, pumping and sinking.

## The second client test (2026-09-29, Magelan 0.3.0 / Sea 0.3.0): the user's 33 points

Built 2026-09-29 as Magelan 0.3.1 / Sea 0.3.1; offline tests (252 checks), both server self-tests and Sea's GPU shader
check pass. Nothing of it seen in the client yet. The user's words are in quotes.

1. "No water in the hull, but air bubbles in the hold; the water outside is cut too coarse, especially at the back."
   Two causes. The bubbles are vanilla's splash particles (`EntityAgent.SpawnFloatingSediment`, `Entity.OnCollideWithLiquid`)
   for anyone moving with their feet in water, and aboard a sailing ship everyone always moves: Sea now skips them in
   dry places. The coarse cut was Sea's one outline per hull, measured at the widest point between −0.5 and 1.5: the
   transom stern is much wider up there than at the waterline. Sea 0.3.1 takes a detailed outline (32 stations × 4
   heights, fitted by the mod to the generator's exact hull widths; see the README).
   - Also asked mid-way: "wave edges at high wind speeds at lower deck sides penetrate". The cut stood upright while
     the ship heeled, so the leaning side went outside it: Sea's cut now leans with her pitch and roll.
2. "Rudder small and feeble": a broad blade tapering up from the heel (1.45 → 0.8), thicker at the stock, four
   pintles on gudgeons.
3. "Deck plank corners protruding at the waterline", 5. "a side frame surface penetrating the outer hull at the main
   deck", 6. "elements penetrate outside the castles where they slope in", 7. "at the bow decking edges penetrate the
   hull": voxel decks, beams and walls reached into the hull's thickness, square cells against a curved hull. Now no
   drawn cell reaches past the inside planking (`clip_to_ceiling`); each deck's and wall's edge is closed to the
   planking by a mesh strip (`edge_strips`); the beams are timbers ending in the frames.
4. "Copper should be 1 or 2 planks higher": two strakes higher (to 0.84); the lower wale moved up above it (1.15).
8. "At the bow the boards on top cross each other without trimming": the rail caps and wales stop at the centreline
   (mitred), and a stem head post stands at the forecastle's tip over the seam.
9. "Gates have collisions and protrusions; a frame rib is too high": the entry ports (now 3.75–5.0) fill the space
   between two frames exactly; no frame in the gate.
10. "The keel beam doesn't bend up at the bow": the keelson rises along the bottom to the stem and the sternpost.
11. "Cargo hold floor boards have gaps": the edge strips close them to the planking.
12. "Ribs too shallow; double the distance but beefier": a frame under every beam, every 1.5 blocks, 0.25 wide and
    0.2 deep (were 0.22 × 0.09 every 0.75).
13. "The last frame at bow and stern disappears in the inner boards": each face of a frame now follows the inside
    planking exactly (`ceil_z`, the planking's own offset outline).
14. "Other windows should follow the hull like the aft cabin's and fit between frames": the side windows are our own
    glass (casing, mullion, two transoms, stained top panes) on the hull's curve and lean, between frames; the vanilla
    panes are gone.
15. "Move the capstan in front of the bow cabin" and 24. "one stair to each castle (keep the right ones), the capstan
    off centre where the port stair was, the fireplace to the other side — realistic?": done. Capstans stood on the
    centreline, usually in the waist or under the half deck, so off centre is a liberty (the other way would be the
    capstan on the centreline and the forecastle door moved aside). The anchor, its hawse, bitt and cable are on the
    port bow now; the hearth, its pipe and the forecastle bed swapped sides.
16. "Stairs to castles need refinement; align with the sloping hull": open ship's stairs (stringers, treads with a
    nosing, cleats, a handrail on turned stanchions), their upper steps further inboard where the side leans in;
    starboard only.
17. "Castles need railings facing inside, with poles": balustrades across the quarterdeck's, forecastle's and poop's
    fronts (plinth, turned balusters, handrail, newel posts at the stairs).
18. "Bowsprit attachment needs refinement; a rope penetrates it above the latrines": its heel in a chock between two
    knightheads with a crosspiece, iron bands, a gammoning lashing at the forecastle's tip, raised a little; the heads'
    hand rope is two ropes to eyebolts on the bowsprit.
19. "When sails are lowered ropes hang in the air": the sheets follow their clews up to the yard (`sail` on a follow
    part); the topsail's sheets run between the two yards (`anchorFollow`).
20. "Holes at the bottom of the solid railings for water to run off": six scuppers each side in the waist.
21. "Can floors slope toward the centre?": answered, not built. Real decks have camber (higher on the centreline) and
    sheer (rising toward the ends), so water runs to the sides and aft/forward to the waist, out of the scuppers, and
    not into the cabins. Walking here is on the 0.25 collision grid: a real slope would be a staircase of small steps.
22. "The hold hatch doesn't need to be a chest": it just opens. An older ship's one-big-storage stays reachable through
    it while it still holds something.
23. "Attachments in the hold for chests, double chests, clay vessels, crates": 24 places either side of the keelson,
    in pairs; a trunk lies across a pair.
25. "Companionway where the port stair was at aft, going down aft — realistic?": yes; companion ladders at the break of
    the half deck were usual. It's against the quarterdeck's front, its hood over the after end; the two port beds on
    the lower deck under it moved forward.
26. "Lifeboat racks, realistic options, boats for 8": boats of the period were stowed in the waist (on the hatches,
    one nested in another) or towed astern; skid beams/gallows across the waist came a little later (late 16th–17th
    century); davits are 18th–19th century. Built: gallows across the waist over head height with three places.
27. "Our own boat, raft size, for 2 or 3": "Ship's boat", a double-ended rowing boat for three (`tools/shipgen/boat.py`;
    vanilla `EntityBoat`/`ItemBoat`, our model), 7 planks and a rope. Its model and seats haven't been seen in the game.
28. "Rear sail free edges very edgy": the lateen is a smooth mesh now (skin group `sail:mizzen-lateen`).
29. "Every mast should have a lantern attachment at the main deck": hooks on the mainmast (waist), foremast
    (forecastle) and mizzen (great cabin); the forecastle's beam hook went (it hung in the same place).
30. "Steering wheel animation too coarse": the rudder is synced 5 times a second; the wheel and tiller now follow it
    smoothly at the helm's rate (`EntityShip.RudderShown`).
31. "Figurehead missing — changeable — a gilded mermaid": under the forecastle on the stem; right-click the stem head
    to change it (mermaid / none so far).
32. "Masts should be pine": masts, topmast, yards and bowsprit.
33. "Ship name plate, at the back or the side": a carved board across the top of the transom; right-click it to name
    her (a small dialog; up to 24 letters), painted in gilt.

Also: the hull's collision is the planking's full thickness now, so nobody walks into the inside planking (and the
test walker no longer slipped between a bed and the side).

## The third client test (2026-09-29, Magelan 0.3.1 / Sea 0.3.1): the user's 12 points

Built 2026-09-29 (late evening) as Magelan 0.3.2 / Sea 0.3.2: 287 offline checks, the server self-test (93), Sea's
offline tests and its GPU shader check pass. Nothing of it seen in the client yet. The user's words are in quotes.

1. "Life boat model is rough and jagged. Waves penetrate it and we cannot stand on it. Maybe a bit longer boat. It
   doesn't collide with the ship, I can go inside the ship. No way to pick the boat up at the ship (I can go on the
   ladder but not pick it up)." Asked, the user chose a **walkable boat** (one of our own vessels, like the carrack,
   not vanilla's EntityBoat). Later the same evening they added: "a bigger rowing boat, maybe with a foldable mast,
   like real boats on ships; only two of them with 4 people capacity. Oars deployed when clicking on a rowing seat
   (otherwise stowed); maybe two rowing seats. For 1–2 players on small expeditions on rivers where the carrack
   wouldn't fit. A place for vessels or a chest, like vanilla's (the elk's). Maybe shrink the vanilla model and put
   oars. Think." Built: a ship's launch 5.8 × 1.9 (`tools/shipgen/boat.py`, design `skiff`, item and entity
   `magelan:boat-skiff`), generated like the carrack (smooth planked mesh, walkable voxels, dry inside, rides the
   waves). Two rowing thwarts: right-click one to sit at the oars, which come out while you're there (stowed on the
   thwarts otherwise) and sweep with the stroke; W/S row, A/D pull one side harder; two rowers √2 faster (1.9 / 2.7
   m/s). The tiller: sitting on the stern sheets beside it (W/S sail, A/D, Space the grapnel). A mast that folds down
   forward over the bow (right-click its foot; down on a new boat), up with a lug sail sailing by the wind. A bow
   locker (16 slots) and a place for a chest, vessel or crate aft of the rowers; four aboard at most. She doesn't pass
   through ships (`EntityShip.HitsShip`, both ways), Shift + right-click picks her up (empty, nobody aboard; from the
   water, a dock, a ship's deck or ladder), right-click the water with her to put her in. Get in by swimming into her,
   right-clicking her, jumping in, or coming down a rope ladder with her at its foot; from her, walk into a ship's rope
   ladder to climb it. Two of her go on the carrack's gallows (drawn from her mesh, mast down, oars stowed, rudder
   unshipped). Why vanilla's model wasn't shrunk: vanilla boats are only sat in (the engine has no entity collision),
   and a shape of boxes is what looked jagged; the launch keeps vanilla's look (oak, linen lug sail). The recipe: 12
   planks, 4 sticks, linen, a rope. An old skiff (a vanilla `EntityBoat`) in a saved world may be discarded when it
   loads; the item still works.
2. "Boat stand is a bit too low, I cannot jump into the hold through the open glass doors": the gallows are level with
   the castle decks now (6.5), so you can climb onto the waist hatch's coaming under them and drop through.
3. "Boat stand legs interfere with the gates when open": the entry ports moved a frame space forward (5.25–6.5), and
   the gates hinge at their forward edge, so they swing away from the gallows.
4. "The second stair up to the aft castle ends too close to the first; too little room to get to the wheel or the
   doors. Shorter stairs?": the poop's stair is a steep ship's stair (a quarter block a step, 63°), 1.5 long instead
   of 3, at the aft end of the quarterdeck.
5. "Seaweed penetrates the hull": plants (seaweed, sea grass, corals, water lilies; blocks without collision, in water
   or plants) inside the hull are cleared as she moves (`EntityShip.SweepPlants`); the water stays.
6. "Stuck aground with my side; I couldn't remove the rock and soil, the mod prevented it": the hull protection only
   stops blocks being *put* inside a hull now; breaking a solid block there is allowed (`MagelanMod.MayPlaceOrBreak`).
   And a ship already lying against something may move as long as she touches nothing new
   (`EntityShip.PoseToward`), so she can be worked off with the sweeps or the helm.
7. "Water outside the hull at the stern is still weirdly cropped": Sea's 32 × 4 outline had to jump from nothing to
   the transom's width between two stations a block apart; the fit overshot and cut a triangle of water out up to 2
   blocks beside her quarters, and a wedge a block deep behind the transom. Now (Sea 0.3.2) the detailed outline's
   stern end is a raked plane through the transom (`WaterClipBox.DryRake`), the outline runs on square to it, and its
   rows are at −0.75 (the transom's foot), 0.25, 1.25, 2.25: at most 0.34 wider than her quarters from the waterline
   up, 0.36 behind the transom, 0.32 at the stem. The generator's width grid is an eighth of a block along the hull.
8. "Cannot remove the boat from the stand": the click was hidden by the boat's own collision cells (they reach a
   little past its box on the 0.25 grid). Picking now lets a fixture's own cells be what the ray meets
   (`ShipFittings.Pick`, shared with the offline test).
9. "Frame rib and beam textures are in the wrong direction": vanilla's wood textures have their grain along u. Box
   meshes now run u along each face's longer side (and are drawn in pieces of at most 2 blocks), the frames' faces run
   it up the frame, and wooden poles (masts, yards, stanchions) along their length (`Material.grain`).
10. "Make the ship longer by 2 blocks in the middle": done (`STRETCH` in the generator): 33 on deck, the waist 17. The
    frames stay every 1.5 the whole length; things forward that sit between frames or beams were placed on that
    rhythm (the forecastle window, the foremast at 13.375, the stovepipe). Lower deck: 13 bed spots now.
11. "Is the main mast placement correct?": it stood about amidships, a little aft (0.5 behind the keel's middle, 0.3
    behind the centre of buoyancy), like the wrecks of the period (the Pepper Wreck's mast step is just aft of its
    midship frame). After lengthening it moved to −0.125 to keep that, between two deck beams.
12. "Is the displaced volume about right or too small?": about right. 347 m³ below the waterline (≈ 180 t at real
    scale, 1/1.25³; estimates for the Santa María are 150–230 t), 377 m³ after lengthening; draft/beam 0.27 like the
    Santa María's. She looks high because the decks are 3 blocks apart for players (a real nau's ~2 m, 2.5 blocks at
    scale), and the lowest deck sits just above the waterline, as naus were loaded. Not changed.

## The fourth client test (2026-09-30, Magelan 0.3.2 / Sea 0.3.2): the user's 10 points

Built 2026-09-30 as **Magelan 0.3.3** (Sea stays 0.3.2): the offline checks (for all three carracks) and the server
self-test (ALL OK, with the rowing boat's checks) pass. Nothing of it seen in the client yet.

1. "Can swim inside the hull at the waterline, as a player or in the boat (aft with the boat)": swimmers are pushed out
   of a ship's hull (`ShipClientSystem.KeepOutOfHulls`, on the dry mask as well as the walkable cells), and ship to
   ship contact tests the dry mask too (`EntityShip.HullAt`), so the boat can't slide in under the transom.
3. "Can't pick the boat up from the rope ladder: Shift climbs down": Ctrl + right-click picks her up as well.
4. "Gates: a different texture outside, a different-coloured rectangle inside, part of the outside missing when open":
   the gates' planks are the bulwark's own strakes inside and out, tarred reveals and bottom, black iron.
5. "All stairs the short kind; the stringer flush with the floor; main deck stairs half floating; put them in front of
   the upper castle stairs, straight": all three are steep ship's stairs 1.5 long, straight, in one line at the side
   (z 2–3; the forecastle's 1.75–2.75), stringers cut level at the floor and the deck edge.
6. "Remove the forecastle's side windows": done.

2. The boat. Asked, the user chose (A) vanilla's boat, "a bit shorter than vanilla", two stowed on the ship; storing
   them "belly up" or not to be thought about later (they'd deploy by themselves when a ship sinks, so stowed boats
   need a cover). Built: the **rowing boat** (`magelan:rowboat-<wood>`, `tools/shipgen/rowboat.py`): vanilla's
   sailboat model without mast, rig, rudder, tiller and stern seats, a block shorter (7.5), a thwart in the middle
   (the one seat, which steers) and a pair of oars on thole pins, lying out over the water at rest and stroking while
   she moves ("row" animation); vanilla's places for chests, baskets, vessels, crates and boat seats kept (bar the two
   under the thwart; two added at the stern where the seats were), the anchor, figurehead, plaques, shields.
   `EntityRowBoat : EntityBoat`: stops against ships' hulls, is moved out if a ship comes onto her, and holds the
   waves down under her (a Sea footprint). `ItemRowBoat`: won't put her in inside a ship. Picked up with Shift +
   right-click (vanilla's), from a ship's deck or rope ladder Ctrl or Shift + right-click (ours). Recipe: 10 planks
   of one wood and 2 crude oars. On the gallows the oars are shipped along her sides (the item's model); the gallows
   moved (1.6 and 6.6) and the places grew (7.6 x 2.9) for her. The walkable launch (`skiff`) is parked: loadable, no
   recipe, not in the creative inventory. Not known till it's seen: which way the rower faces on the thwart, the
   oars' stroke, speed (1.35 x a raft's).
7–9. **Built for trying (the user: "build v2 (13 block main deck) and v3 (extended deck by 2) and current,
   temporarily 3 ships"):** Charter of a carrack (as built), of a carrack (deep, for trying) = `carrack2`, and (deep,
   long waist, for trying) = `carrack3` (`carrack.py --variant=2|3`). As the proposal below, except: the foremast
   stays where it was (moved 0.9 it would have stood on a forecastle beam and outside the hull at its foot); the stern
   run and the transom's foot are the first carrack's measured from the new waterline (the transom starts 0.75 under
   it), so Sea's dry outline fits her stern as closely; the poop's overhang stands on knees; the forecastle's
   underside slopes from 4.75 at the stem (1.25 over the waist rail) to 6.0 at the tip, the figurehead four fifths
   the size under it; the wales and the copper line are the waterline's; the capstan 2 in front of the forecastle
   door. Waist: 13 and 15. Draught 4.5, beam 10.5.

   The proposal (2026-09-30): Hull proportions, castles and mast placement: `tools/shipgen/proportions.py` draws the carrack as built against a
   proposal (`docs/proportions.png`, with a table of the numbers). The proposal, from the period's rules (the
   *as-dos-tres* 1 : 2 : 3; Palacio 1587; Cano 1611; Fernandez's *Livro de Traças* 1616 as transcribed by Domingues)
   and the Pepper Wreck, Mary Rose and replicas: she floats 1.5 deeper (draught / beam 0.33 → 0.43; the wrecks and
   replicas 0.35–0.44), beam 9.2 → 10.5 with a flat floor a third of it (hold floor 3.5 → 5.0 wide), the quarterdeck
   3 forward to just abaft the mainmast (the tolda ran "from the mast aft"; the main chainplates ran aft from abreast
   the main hatch), so the main shrouds come down on its side; the poop 3 longer (half forward, 1.2 more transom rake,
   1.25 overhanging the transom); the forecastle 1 longer aft and 1.25 further out, its underside sloping up from
   lower on the stem; the castle sweeps 1.75 → 2.75; the foremast 0.9 nearer the stem. The mainmast stays at the
   middle of the keel (Fernandez). The cost: the waist 17 → 13 (the capstan could go up on the quarterdeck, where naus
   had it), unless the midbody is lengthened again.
10. After the shape: the companionway, capstan, glazed hatch and the rest placed again.

## The fifth client test (2026-09-30, Magelan 0.3.3 / Sea 0.3.2): the user's 8 points

"Both ships look very good and the aft cabins are very spacious now. But the longer version looks better for place
management and later cannon placement." **The carrack is now the long-waist one** (variant 3; `carrack.py` builds it
by default; 1 and 2 still build as `carrack-v1`, `carrack-v2` for comparing, not shipped; the entity codes `carrack2`
and `carrack3` are kept as aliases of `carrack`, so trial ships in a saved world load). Built as **Magelan 0.3.4** (Sea
unchanged): the offline checks and the server self-test (with new checks) pass; not seen in the client.

1. "The rowing boat can't be boarded; standing on it I walk through it; Ctrl was used to place vessels and chests on
   it." Vanilla makes a seat's click box from the size of the model element its attachment point is on
   (`EntityBehaviorSelectionBoxes`), and ours was a speck: now it's vanilla's seat size over the thwart. Vanilla's
   boats have nothing to stand on, so stepping, jumping or swimming into her now seats you (on the thwart, else a
   free seat; not straight back after getting out). Our pickup took Ctrl + right-click from vanilla (which puts chests
   and vessels in her): ours now works only from a ship's deck or rope ladder, with Shift or Ctrl and an empty hand.
   The model's textures pointed into our own domain (warnings in the log): now the game's.
2. "Check and calculate realistic roll at different wind speeds up to 12, and the rocking from side wind and waves;
   at full speed it rocks too much; at the strongest wind the waves reach almost the main deck and the quarterdeck
   cabin's windows." A table now comes from the offline tests (`dotnet run -c Release -- motion`): for each Beaufort
   force Sea's sea, her speed and heel, and the roll and pitch from the waves. Sea's strongest wind is 1.5 (37.5 m/s,
   force 12; 1 = 25 m/s, force 10), its sea there 5.5 blocks significant height (real seas run twice that in force 10
   already). Heel under full sail on a beam reach: F5 2.8°, F6 4.6°, F7 7°, F8 10°, F10 15.5°, F12 19°; the waves roll
   her at rest (beam sea) F8 5°, F10 14°, F12 19° (ships lying ahull in a storm roll 20–30°). What was missing is that
   sails steady a ship: now the roll damping grows with sail set and wind (`MotionDef.SailRollDamping` 0.3,
   `ShipMotion.SailSteadiness`): under way under full sail she rolls about half as much (F10 10° → 5°). And the water is
   kept out of her hull (Sea's dry cut) up to the waist's rail, not just her deck: a crest breaking higher than the
   deck outside isn't drawn inside the bulwarks. Green water comes over the lee rail only in force 11–12 under full
   sail (her lean plus the roll).
3. "Does copper change the speed? What could the top speed be?" Answered: copper adds 8 % to her top speed (7.0 →
   7.56 m/s). Her hull speed (1.34 √LWL in feet, in knots) is about 7.2 m/s (14 kn), so 7.0 is at it; under full sail
   in a good wind she makes 5.3–5.5 m/s (10–11 kn) on her best points, a carrack's best days. Historically copper
   (from the 1760s) didn't make a clean hull faster: it kept it clean. A fouling bottom that slows her over the
   months, which copper prevents (and careening clears), would be the realistic model; not built.
4. "Poop cabin could be 1 longer (a lot of room on the quarterdeck). Where would the compass be, near the wheel?"
   The poop's front 1 further forward (−8.5). The compass is in a binnacle in front of the wheel (a small cupboard,
   its after face glazed, a lamp beside it); the wheel 0.75 further aft for it. Look at it: her heading, to 16 points.
5. "The quarterdeck's two side windows are too close to the water now": the great cabin has no side windows; the poop
   cabin two.
6. "The companionway at the glass hatch's place, going down toward the bow; the glass hatch after it; the cargo hatch
   below with stairs the same way. Realistic, or at the side of the hatches?" Built on the centreline: the mainmast,
   the companionway (down toward the bow, the hood over its forward part), a landing, the glazed waist hatch (7–9.5)
   and the hold hatch under it with its ladder at its after end going down the same way. Realistic: the main (cargo)
   hatch was kept clear for hoisting, the ladders went down through their own smaller hatches, and in the hold a
   ladder stood at one end of the main hatchway so cargo still passed. The capstan moved aft of the mainmast, port side
   (where naus had it; the companionway was there), its cable led aft along the deck from the forecastle: forward its
   bars would have swept over the hatch.
7. "Some bubbles from the water visible inside the hull again." The deep carrack's main deck is 2 above her waterline:
   heaving and rolling in a big sea she dips it under the level of the world's (still) water blocks, and on deck you
   were in water to the game (splashes, bubbles, and the underwater view and breath). The dry places now reach over
   the hull's inside from the main deck up through the castles (`ShipDrySpaces`).
8. "Move the pump behind the glass hatch, on the bow side": forward of it, a little to starboard (10.25), clear of
   the forecastle's door and stair.

## The sixth client test (2026-10-01, Magelan 0.3.5 / Sea 0.3.3): built as Magelan 0.3.6

"Good news! Ships are sinking and making wreck." What the user found, and what was done:

1. **"They are sinking on their own when trying to sail"**, "only while a player is on it", "water mostly when the
   sails are down and she's tilted". Unmanned, she anchors and furls, so she doesn't heel. Two bugs in the sea
   coming over her side (`DamageLayout.DownfloodPoints`, `ShipFlood.Downflooding`):
   - **The waist's rail was taken as 0.5 over the deck,** not 1.0. The side leans in above the deck, and the scan went
     up the outermost column at deck height, which stops at once. Now every column of the waist is scanned across a
     block of the side: its lowest edge is the bulwark's top (1.0), a gun port's sill (0.25), or a shut gate's top.
   - **Half of what came over the side went into the hold.** Now it lands on the open deck and mostly runs off:
     `DeckShare` 5 % gets below (gratings, the companionway, seams), `OpenHatchShare` 15 % more for each open hatch in
     the main deck, and the sea over an open hatch itself goes straight down.
   - With both, a heel of 10–15° and a rolling sea pushed the lee rail under. A little water then made her list more
     (free surface), and she went down in minutes.
   - **"Foundering"** was announced for any splash. Now it's water getting below more than 0.3 t/s faster than it's
     pumped out (eased over seconds), or a hold 80 % full, and it's said at most once a minute.
   - **A new report,** `dotnet run -c Release -- sailflood`: a sound carrack for 10 minutes in each wind, sails furled
     or full sail on a beam reach, with the gates open or shut; how much water she takes and where.
     - Before the fix she flooded from force 10 under sail (and took some water from force 9).
     - Now she takes none up to force 9, 0.3 t at force 10, and 51 t at force 12 under full sail.
     - Offline checks: the waist's side heights, and no water in 3 minutes in force 8 and 9, furled and under sail.
2. **"One ship sunk and then appeared again after game restart, but I saw the wreck on the floor."** From the logs,
   not a bug:
   - Carrack #4113 sank at 22:22 and became a wreck.
   - A second carrack, #6844, started going down at 22:25:59, and the game was quit at 22:26:24 while it was still
     sinking.
   - On loading, #6844 went on sinking, came to rest on top of the first wreck (y 139 against 130), and became a
     second wreck at 22:27.
3. **"Pump speed is too slow."**
   - 80 litres a stroke instead of 10 (`DamageDef.PumpRate`): one pumper 80 l/s holds a few leaking or cracked
     panels, and two at a brake (160 l/s) hold one fresh hole below the waterline (156 l/s).
   - New config factor `Pumps` (1).
4. **"Cannons: we need to be able to aim a bit left to right and a bit up and down."** Then: "holding the left mouse
   button to aim and right to shoot", and "port hole can be enlarged". Built (not seen in the client):
   - **Laying a gun:** stand by it (its breech within 2.5 blocks, or it in the crosshair) and hold left mouse: its crew
     lays it toward where you look, 10°/s trained and 6°/s elevated, as far as it goes; let go and it stays. A
     left-click there swings at nothing. Right-click fires the gun you're laying (wherever you look), the one in the
     crosshair, or the one you laid in the last 4 s and are still by. The HUD while laying: "At the bronze gun, port
     side: trained 5° to the bow, elevation 3° · ready", "As far as it goes (...)" when you look beyond its limits, the
     keys. The look-at box and the hint on a gun: ready or reloading, and its lay.
   - **The gun captain's place** (an extra, from the first plan): Ctrl + right-click a gun to stand just behind its
     breech, facing out along it (you look along the gun as it's laid); the same keys there, Shift leaves (you stand where
     you were). You move with the gun as it's trained and step back as it runs back at you. The seat's facing stays
     square to her side: turning it with the gun would turn your view and lay the gun further.
   - **The limits** (in each gun's fixture, defaults in C#: `trainLimits` ±12, `elevationLimits` -5..15, `trainRate` 10,
     `elevationRate` 6), as the generator found them clear:
     - trained 12° either way, except toward a gun 1.5 away (7.25°: both trained toward each other and both run back,
       they'd meet), the forward guns toward the bow (3.75°: the boat gallows' forward post) and, while the entry port's
       gate beside them stands open, 0.25° (`trainLimitsGateOpen`; the gate won't open while the gun is trained further:
       "The gun beside it is trained into the gateway");
     - elevated 5° down to 15° up. The port is now a notch from 0.25 over the deck up through the rail and its cap (cut
       square, its ends closed, lined with oak), the sill where it was (the sea over her side unchanged: the full
       offline suite's flooding checks still pass). With the old 0.5-high port the muzzle (it comes back in through the
       port as the gun runs back) allowed only -3..+3.5°.
   - **The gun model, split** (`gun.py`): the carriage (`part:<gun>`, it runs back), the barrel (`part:<gun>-barrel`, it
     elevates about the trunnions), the trucks, the breeching (it follows the barrel); a new part `<gun>-train` (the turn
     about an upright in the middle of the side's thickness, at the port) that the carriage rides on. Run out, the
     carriage's fore end is close to the side (the trunnions 0.5 in from the inside planking; the muzzle stands 0.8 out
     of her side, was 0.25). The barrel's axis 0.06 higher (0.55: the muzzle clears the sill laid 5° down as it runs back
     in); the cheeks' after steps lower and shorter, the transom lower, a stool bed on the deck under the breech (it
     comes down onto it at 15°). **No quoin any more:** a wedge under the breech can't follow it from -5° to +15°
     without the cascabel coming down into it; the breech hangs free over the stool bed (0.31 over it when level). The
     gun block's shape follows.
   - **Server** (`src/Ship/GunLaying.cs` pure, `ShipGuns.cs`): the lay toward where the player looks, from their
     `Pos.Yaw`/`Pos.Pitch` (their client sends them 15 times a second, mounted or not: engine notes) turned into her
     frame as she really lies; laid at the crew's rates every tick; each gun's lay saved with her and sent to clients in
     WatchedAttributes `magelanGuns` (4 bytes a gun), five times a second while one moves and once when it stops; the
     player laying it has to stay by it (3.5 blocks) and their client says so twice a second. Clients draw each gun
     easing toward the sent lay; the one laying it lays their own drawing at once from their own view and is pulled back
     only when it's further off than the lag explains.
   - **The shot** leaves the laid barrel's muzzle along it, through her true pose (waves, heel, list, trim) as before.
     `/magelan fire [gun|port|starboard|all] [elevation] [train]` lays them first, at once, as far as they go (`-` leaves
     one as it is).
   - **Tests:** offline 439 → 447 (`LayTests.cs`: limits and pace, the lay from a view as she lies tilted, the muzzle at
     the corners tilted, the moving parts composed as the renderer composes them putting the barrel's tip from its mesh
     where the shot leaves, run out and run back, the clearances at every corner run out and run back a quarter at a time
     with the gate shut and open, the neighbours at every pair of corners; and the earlier cannon checks updated); the
     server self-test: laid through the API it fires along its lay, as far as it goes, toward a view, at the crew's
     pace, the lays saved and a carrack loaded with them laid so, `/magelan fire` with a train, a stand-in at the gun
     captain's place. Views: `docs/views/carrack-gun-lay-deck.png`, `-gun-lay-out.png` (the four corners, run out and
     run back), `-gun-captain.png`.
   - **Choices for the user to confirm:** hold left to lay, right-click to fire (and the 4 s "the gun you just laid"),
     Ctrl + right-click for the gun captain's place; the reach (2.5); the rates; the limits (and that the forward guns
     barely train forward); the taller notched ports; the gun run out further (muzzle 0.8 out); no quoin for now; the
     gun captain stepping back with the recoil; the gun's solid box doesn't turn with it (walkers can brush the trail of
     a gun trained 12°); the lay is where you look in the world (parallax from your eye over the barrel ignored).
5. **"Boats have sticky water around them: the water is depressed or elevated around them, like an imaginary wave at
   a different height."**
   - The rowing boat doesn't ride Sea's waves; vanilla's physics floats her at still-water level.
   - Her water clip held the waves down to her level in a 1.2-block ring round her, so a passing crest or trough showed
     as a ring of water standing too low or too high.
   - Now her clip is a dry cut like the ships' (`Dry`, from 0.8 under her waterline to 1.2 over it, inside her outline
     less 0.15): no water inside her, and the waves run on round her as they are.
   - **Later, maybe:** making her ride the waves (heave, pitch and roll). Her passengers' seats and the rower's camera
     would have to follow, and vanilla's boat physics floats her on still water.
6. **"Three ships afloat and two wrecks; the 3rd ship reappeared when I was going near it."** The save (copied and read)
   holds carracks #6743, #7048 and #7074 afloat; the sunk #4113 and #6844 are only wrecks now.
   - #6844, #7048 and #7074 were all launched by the charter. Any right-click with it in hand that nothing aboard took
     launched a carrack in front of you, and in creative a charter isn't used up. The user kept it in the hotbar.
   - Now (`ItemShipDeed`):
     - it's held for 1.5 s to launch
     - not from aboard a ship (seated, on deck or on a ladder), nor with a ship in the crosshair
     - every launch is said in chat ("Launched: Carrack (#7101)") and written to the audit log
   - `/magelan remove` takes the nearest ship within 48 blocks away (her cargo dropped).
   - Not done: keeping parked ships visible from further away. The world unloads far from players and they go with it,
     like animals. Offered, not decided.
7. **"Check if the ship rolls too much under sail."**
   - Her rolling from the waves under sail is small: 1–3° to force 8 on a beam reach (her sails steady her).
   - What rocked her was her lean in the wind. Sea's gusts change the wind ±45 % over 7–15 s, and her lean followed in
     0.7 s, so a breeze leaned her anywhere from 1.4° to 7.9° and back.
   - Now (`MotionDef`):
     - `HeelResponse` 4 s: her lean follows slowly, eased the same on the server (`EntityShip.ServerHeel`, used for
       the sea over her side and for laying the guns) and on clients
     - `MaxHeel` 12° (from 20°): past it the crew spills wind and shortens sail
   - The same gusts now lean her 3.4–5.5°.
   - Heel under full sail on a beam reach: force 6 4.5°, force 8 8.6°, force 10 11.3°, force 12 12° (it was 15.5° at
     force 10 and 19.3° at force 12).
   - Offline checks for both.
8. **The layout in the waist and the bow.** Built (not seen in the client). Positions are her own x (forward) and z
   (starboard), the same in the game; heights over the main deck.
   - **The hatches right after the companionway** ("1-2 blocks only" between the ladders):
     - The glazed hatch and the hold hatch under it moved aft from 7.0–9.5 to 5.25–7.75 (`HATCH`, from the
       companionway's end). The companionway is unchanged (1.5–3.75).
     - On the lower deck: the companion ladder's foot at 3.37, the hold hatch's coaming at 5.0 (1.6 of floor between),
       the hold ladder's head at 5.33 (1.96). Its foot on the keelson at 6.75.
     - On the main deck a block's walk across the waist between the hood (to 4.0) and the coaming (from 5.0).
     - The hatch lies between the deck beams at 5.0 and 8.0, partly under the gallows: the forward gallows beam
       crosses it 2.7 over the deck (the user accepted that).
     - Walking: off the companionway, keep walking forward with the hold hatch open and you go on down into the hold;
       up out of the hold, keep walking aft and you go on up the companionway (an offline check, both ways).
   - **The pump on the centreline** at (8.5, 0), 0.5 forward of the hatch's coaming. Its brake reaches forward (the
     handle at 9.55), its spout to starboard, away from the capstan.
     - Its pipe goes straight down through the lower deck to a strainer box on the keelson, solid all the way. The
       pump itself is solid to 1.5, so nobody steps up onto it from the coaming.
     - No cargo places at x 8 and 9 in the hold, so you can walk round the pipe (the hold ladder kept them clear
       before). The ladder now comes down where the places at x 5 and 6 were: 34 places (38 before). On a saved ship,
       a chest, vessel, crate or trunk on a place that's gone is moved into the hold's old storage with what was in it
       (`EntityShip.RescueOrphanedCargo`, which did that only for the contents before).
   - **The capstan in the bow** at (10.25, −2.0): 1.75 aft of the forecastle bulkhead, port side.
     - `capstan_clearance` in the generator checks its bars as the crew walk them (and stops the build if they'd sweep
       into anything). The bars are 0.72 clear of the pump's brake, 0.89 of the forward port gun laid anywhere and run
       back, 0.91 of the forecastle door, 1.29 of the hatch's coaming, 2.6 of the port gate, 3.0 of the forecastle
       stair. The crew walking them (1.3 out, a player wide) is 0.29 clear of the brake: pumpers and capstan crew can
       work at once.
     - The offline ring check passes (0 of 32 places blocked).
   - **The hawse holes further out:** `HAWSE_Z` 0.9 → 1.6 (the port hawse at x 18.85, 0.8 over the deck). The riding
     bitts' posts went out from ±0.875 to ±1.25.
     - The cable comes in through the port hawse along the outboard side of the port bitt, takes a round turn on it, and
       runs straight aft along the deck (z −1.45 → −1.5). It goes through the bulkhead at z −1.5 (was −1.25) onto the
       capstan barrel's inboard side (z −1.53): one straight line from the bitt to the barrel.
     - 2.5 turns round the barrel, then down a hole outboard of it to the cable tier on the lower deck at (10.6, −1.05),
       clear of the spindle and the berths.
     - The anchor's cable outside (the moving part `anchorcable`) follows from the new hawse.
     - Before, the turn round the bitt went through the post; now it's a helix round it.
   - **The gallows' posts as frames:** they stand on the frames at 2.125 and 6.625 (`GALLOWS_X` 2.0, 6.5; they were
     at 1.6 and 6.6, inboard of the frames).
     - Above the rail cap each post is its frame carried on up: its inner face where the frame's is, out to the outside
       planking's line, leaning in with the tumblehome (the side 4.98 out at the rail, 4.67 at the beam).
     - The beam lies across the posts' heads, 0.05 proud of them, with a hanging knee inboard under each end. The old
       knee to the bulwark is gone.
     - The forward post is on the frame the gates hang on.
     - The boats' places moved 0.15 forward (centre 4.375, 0.575–8.175); they are still clear of the mainmast. Their
       stowing is unchanged (`boat-1`, `boat-2`, `GALLOWS_Y`).
     - A side effect: the gallows no longer limit the forward guns, which now train 12° toward the bow (3.75° before).
       Toward the gun beside them and the open gate the limits are as before.
   - **The firehearth** in the forecastle's starboard after corner: x 12.3–13.9, its open front at z 2.15 facing port,
     its back against the frames (3.6–3.9).
     - A brick base 0.5 high out to the frames, with a firebed (1.1 × 1.0) sunk 0.25 into it.
     - Raised brick sides at both ends (1.0 high at the back, falling to 0.85 at the front) and a fireback 1.5 high
       along the side, with an iron plate behind the fire.
     - The front is open, with an iron fender along the lip. It all stands on an iron tray that keeps embers off the
       deck.
     - The vanilla firepit (`game:firepit-cold`) stands in the firebed. The bricks are vanilla's fire bricks.
     - The smoke hood is one tapered copper hood, open below. It runs from a hem round the hearth on the fireback's top
       (1.5) to a square neck round the pipe (2.35). Every face is flat and seen from both sides (the back follows the
       side, as two triangles).
     - The old "broken pyramid" was a prism whose top corners were paired with the wrong bottom ones, so its faces
       twisted.
     - The stovepipe (13.125, 3.375) goes up through the forecastle deck before its first beam, 1.5 over the deck, with
       a cone rain cap on legs. On the deck it stands by the bulwark, 0.9 forward of the stair's head and just outboard
       of its line (the stair is 2.25–3.25 across, the pipe solid from 3.25 out), aft of the fore shrouds' rail.
     - The hearth is solid to 1.0 (the fireback to 1.5), out to the side, so nobody steps into the fire. The pipe is
       solid on the forecastle deck.
     - The forecastle's chests moved: `chest-forecastle-1` from starboard 14.0 to 15.25 (forward of the hearth), and
       `chest-forecastle-2` from port 17.0 to the port after corner, 13.25 (out of the cable's way).
   - **What the hearth leaves for the working fire** (the next job: fuel, cooking, flames, warmth). One fixture, type
     `hearth`, code `hearth` (game heights):
     - `pos` (13.1, 2.25, 2.81): the firepit block's bottom centre on the firebed.
     - `yaw` 90: its open front faces port.
     - `block` `game:firepit-cold`: drawn like a prop (`ShipRenderer`, `case "hearth"`).
     - `sel`: the firebed and the fire over it (12.55–13.65, 2.25–3.0, 2.3–3.32).
     - `box`: the bricks (12.3–13.9, 2.0–3.5, 2.15–3.91).
     - `fire` (13.1, 2.45, 2.81): the fire's middle (light, flames, heat).
     - `cook` (13.1, 2.375, 2.81): where a pot's bottom stands, as vanilla puts one in a firepit (1/8 over the block's
       bottom: engine notes).
     - `chimney` (13.125, 6.5, 3.375): the stovepipe's mouth under its cap (smoke).
     - `room` `forecastle`.
     - In C#, `FixtureDef.Fire`, `Cook` and `Chimney`. Its look-at name is "firehearth". (As first built right-click did
       nothing and its hint said "The ship's firehearth (cold)": the working fire is below.) It isn't solid as a fixture:
       the bricks are in the design's collision.
     - The older `stove` type (lit by a right-click, warming its room through `ShipShelter.StoveHeat`) is still in the
       mod, unused by the carrack.
   - **The hearth now works** (built 2026-10-02, after the layout; not seen in the client). The user: "Fireplace model can
     take vanilla fireplace, mechanics and animations." Vanilla's firepit only works as a block in the world and her deck
     is an entity, so its pieces are reused and its behaviour rebuilt aboard with our own logic, as with the other fittings
     (no hidden world block). Config `Hearth` (on).
     - **Inventory:** vanilla's own `InventorySmelting` (fuel, input, output, the four cooking slots a clay pot opens) on
       the ship (`HearthInventory`; it needs no block entity: engine notes). Saved with her and sent to clients in
       WatchedAttributes `magelanHearth` with the fire's state (by vanilla's keys).
     - **The dialog** (`HearthDialog`): vanilla's firepit dialog rebuilt for a ship: the same layout, flame, arrow and
       readouts, what it will make; its clicks go to the ship. Right-click the hearth opens it.
     - **Lighting:** as vanilla: fuel in it, then a firestarter or a lit torch held to it with held right-click: after 3 s
       a torch lights it, a firestarter one try in four (the HUD counts; it goes on trying while held; "The tinder didn't
       catch" when it doesn't). Shift + right-click puts one fuel or food in; a clay pot goes on with a right-click, a bowl
       is served from a cooked pot.
     - **The fire** (`HearthFire`, server, every 100 ms): vanilla's burn tick ported number for number and run against
       the inventory through vanilla's own methods (melting points and durations, CanSmelt, DoSmelt, the pot's recipes,
       item temperatures). So food cooks aboard as ashore: raw red meat on firewood 43 s from lighting, porridge in a
       pot 47 s. It burns its fuel, goes out when it's gone, its embers relight new fuel for 2 game hours.
     - **Looks and sound** (clients): the firepit's own shape as it burns (lit, ashes, cold with logs) and for what's
       cooking (normal, spit, wide); a pot and its rattling lid, a cooked pot's meal, a joint on the spit, as vanilla draws
       them on a firepit; the lit firepit's own flames, embers and smoke at the fire, moving with her (`HearthClient`);
       smoke out of the stovepipe's mouth drifting with Sea's wind; a flickering light (the lit firepit's, on the
       lanterns' scale) and a glow of embers; vanilla's looped crackle and a pot's bubbling.
     - **Warmth:** vanilla's firepit heat (10, embers 0.25) by vanilla's falloff, for whoever is in the forecastle (a
       closed room) or within 3 blocks of it.
     - **Sinking and wrecks:** going down, the sea puts it out; its contents go into her wreck's chests with her loose
       things (`ShipWrecks.LootOf`), and drop if she's removed.
     - **Tests:** offline 462 → 486 (`HearthTests.cs`, `-- hearth`): the fire's sums, cooking times, warmth, how it's
       drawn, its state and vanilla's inventory saved and loaded. The self-test 185 → 203: lighting it (not without fuel,
       nor with a stick, nor held a second; a firestarter missing and catching), the meat cooked in the time the sum gives
       (42.6 s against 42.5), the forecastle warm and the waist not, going out with embers, a pot of porridge on new fuel
       relit by the embers (32.6 s against 32.5), saved and loaded burning, switched off; a sinking carrack's hearth put
       out; a target's hearth contents in her wreck.
     - **Views:** `docs/views/carrack-hearth-pot.png`, `-hearth-pot-side.png`: the lit firepit with a clay pot and its
       lid, placed as the mod places them (`python tools/shipgen/view.py hearth-pot hearth-pot-side`).
     - The fixture's `cook` point isn't needed after all: vanilla draws a pot from the firepit block (1/16 up, its own
       renderer, not the 1/8 of its `inFirePitProps`), and so does the mod.
     - **Choices for the user to confirm:**
       - A firestarter catches one try in four, as vanilla's does (the HUD goes on counting while you hold it; a line
         says when it didn't catch, which vanilla doesn't).
       - The smoke under the hood lives 3.5 s (vanilla's firepit smoke 18 s would rise through the forecastle deck); the
         stovepipe's smoke is vanilla's smoke quads at vanilla's rate, drifting with 0.7 of Sea's wind (as vanilla's smoke
         takes 0.7 of the wind), so it trails off astern as she sails.
       - A pot taken out with ingredients in it gives them to the one who took it out (vanilla drops them at the
         firepit, which aboard would fall through her deck).
       - Its warmth reaches through the bulkhead and the deck above within 3 blocks (vanilla's heat goes through walls).
       - Hearth off: it goes out and can't be lit, but opens so nothing is stuck in it.
       - The light: the lit firepit's own ([7, 7, 16] × 0.55, like the lanterns), flickering 0.75–1; embers 3/16 of it.
     - **Seen but not changed:** vanilla's pot cooking puts the cooked pot in the output whatever is there (a cooked joint
       left in the output is lost): as vanilla's firepit does.
   - **Tests:**
     - Offline (462): the hold hatch 2.08 forward of the companionway's foot, up from the hold and on up the
       companionway and back down the same way; the hearth (its corner, its firepit on the sunken firebed, the fire, the
       pot and the chimney through the forecastle deck); the capstan, ring and walking checks as before; the guns' lay
       clearances, flooding and cargo checks pass with the new positions.
     - Changed: the round-the-hold walk starts where the hold ladder sets you down (it began inside the pump's pipe).
       The ladders' walk-on-at-the-top check tries only that ladder (the hold's now takes you on up the companionway, as
       it should).
     - The self-test (185): the firepit block is in the game, the hearth's bricks are solid round the fire, in the
       forecastle; a chest on a place the design no longer has ends up in the hold.
   - **Views:** in `docs/views/`, rendered with the rowing boats on the gallows and the firepit from the game's own
     models:
     - the waist: `carrack-waist-hatches(-open)`, `-waist-long`, `-hatch-pump`, `-pump(-stroke)`
     - the ladders: `-ladders-cutaway`, `-lowerdeck-landing`, `-lowerdeck-ladders`, `-companion-below`
     - the bow and the cable: `-bow-port`, `-bow-deck`, `-forecastle-in`, `-capstan`, `-capstan-aft`, `-capstan-cable`
     - the hearth: `-hearth`, `-hearth-close`, `-hearth-deck`, `-tex-hearth`
     - the gallows: `-gallows-post`, `-gallows-out`, `-tex-gallows-post`, `-gallows`, `-helm-gallows`
     - the plans: `docs/carrack-*.png`
   - **Choices for the user to confirm:**
     - The hatch at 5.25: a 1.6 landing below and a block's walk above. 5.0 would make them 1.4 and 0.75.
     - The pump at the hatch's forward end with its brake reaching forward and its spout to starboard.
     - The capstan at (10.25, −2.0) with the cable onto its inboard side.
     - The hawse at 1.6 out, and the bitts at ±1.25.
     - Walking off one ladder straight onto the other.
     - The chests' new places in the forecastle.
     - The hearth's size (1.6 × 1.75) and heights. The pipe on the forecastle deck beside the stair's head.
     - Cream fire bricks, and a copper hood.
     - The posts' full frame section above the rail (0.45 deep). The forward guns training 12° toward the bow.
   - **Seen but not changed:**
     - Catted, the anchor's cable runs from the hawse straight up to its ring and passes through the forecastle's raked
       underside. It did before, more so; it would need a sagging cable.
     - The gates, swung open past square (100°), reach a little into the frame at their hinge (the gallows' forward
       post's frame). They did before; the frame is drawn only, not solid.
     - The deck plans `carrack-plan-lowerdeck` and `-maindeck` are cut at the first carrack's heights, so they show the
       main deck and the castles of this deeper one.
9. **A surveyor's chalk for reporting:** "Could we build an item to hold as a player to mark locations on the ship that
   could be commented, and would make reporting issues easier to you?" Built (not seen in the client):
   - **The item** `magelan:surveyorschalk`, "Surveyor's chalk": creative inventory only, no recipe; it looks like
     vanilla's lump of chalk (`game:item/stone`, `game:block/stone/rock/chalk1`). Its class `ItemSurveyorsChalk` only
     keeps vanilla from swinging, breaking, placing or using anything with it.
   - **Right-click** with it (whatever is in the crosshair, before the guns, fixtures and boats get the click): everything
     is captured at once and a window shows it (read-only, scrolls) with a box for the comment, Save and Cancel (Ctrl +
     Enter saves).
   - **What a note holds** (`src/Client/SurveyNotes.cs`): the versions (Magelan, Sea, the game), the real time and the
     game's calendar, the world; the target: on a ship her design, name and number, the part as the look-at box names it
     and its index, the hit point in her own frame (x forward, y up, z starboard, as in `carrack.json`) and in the world,
     the face and its normal in her frame, the fixture there, the part's damage (at a mast its rigging and sails, at the
     helm the rudder) and the whole look-at box; a block's code, name, position, face, hit point and look-at text; a
     creature's code, number and position; nothing: the look direction. The player: feet and eye in the world, the
     view's heading and elevation, and aboard their seat (deck, helm, gun:...), deck, room, climbing, and their place and
     view in her frame (degrees off the bow). The ship aboard and the one looked at: position, heading, speed, sails,
     rudder, anchor, aground; Sea's wind there (strength, m/s, force, from) and waves (significant height × the share
     that reaches her, period, the drawn surface there); her drawn heave, pitch, roll and heel (with the player's Sea
     motion setting) and as she really lies; the water in her hold and its rate, list, trim, sinkage, stage, pumpers,
     fouling, on the bottom, sheathed; her parts that aren't sound (the first 40).
   - **The screenshot:** yes, from code. A quarter second after the window closes the game's own F12 handler is called
     (`GetHotKeyByCode("screenshot").Handler`): it saves that frame with the HUD but without the window to
     `Pictures\Vintagestory\yyyy-MM-dd_HH-mm-ss.png`. The newest PNG there since is found and its name and path go into
     the note (if none turns up in 6 s the note says so and to match one by its time).
   - **The files:** `VintagestoryData/ModData/magelan-notes.json` (a JSON array of every note, every number; camelCase,
     points on one line) and `magelan-notes.md` (rewritten each time: number, time, target, the comment, the
     screenshot, the key numbers). Notes are numbered on from the highest kept.
   - **The marks:** while the chalk is in hand each note's spot shows a small white chalk X (0.3 across, flat on the
     surface, a faint dark edge) with its number above it, turned to you. On a ship it's at her ship-local point,
     moving and tilting with her (by her entity number; not shown while she isn't loaded); on a block or a creature it
     stays where it was. A note about nothing is marked where the player stood. Within 64 blocks, this world only.
   - **Left-click a mark:** its note in chat; **Shift + left-click:** rubbed out (chat says so). The line above the hotbar
     says which mark is in the crosshair, or what a right-click would write about.
   - **`.magelan notes`:** the list (number, time, target, first words; the last 25); `show <n>`, `delete <n>`, `clear`
     (asks for `clear yes`).
   - **For Claude:** `python tools/shipgen/view.py notes [path]` (the game's file by default) renders the carrack with
     each note's spot marked (a magenta X and pin) and numbered, seen from the player's eye when they were aboard (else
     their eye brought into her frame through her heading and tilt, else from in front of the spot), and an overview
     from both sides, into `docs/views/notes/` (a number in grey "(hidden)" when something of her is in front of it).
     Checked with the hand-made `tools/shipgen/notes-sample.json` (five notes: her side from the water, the deck by the
     pump, a gun's cheek, the wheel, a block).
   - **Tests:** offline 448 → 463 (`NoteTests.cs`, `-- notes`): a point at a tilted pose where the renderer draws it and
     back, a ray at her side kept as the same ship-local point, normal and face, the mark lifted off it and in the
     crosshair, the view in her frame; the JSON round trip, the file (delete, numbering, an unreadable file), the
     readable list and chat lines, the sample. The self-test: the item is registered, in the creative inventory, no
     recipe. The window, the capture in the game, the marks, the clicks and the screenshot are client-only: unverified.
   - **Choices for the user to confirm:** the screenshot after Save (so it shows the place a minute later if you took
     long to write, with the HUD and the chat line in it) rather than at the click; Cancel takes no screenshot and keeps
     nothing; left-click with the chalk never breaks a block or opens a door, even with no mark there ("No chalk mark in
     the crosshair"); numbers aren't reused unless the last ones were deleted, and `clear` starts again at 1; a mark on a
     creature or a rowing boat stays in the world where it was hit; a note about nothing is marked at your feet; the
     marks are lit full (they show in the dark hold); the notes stay on the player's own disk (multiplayer: each
     player's own); the X is our own small texture, not a vanilla one.
10. Minor, from the log: `Missing mapping for texture code #writingsurface-leftplaque-<id>` once a session for a
    rowing boat with a plaque. Vanilla's plaque behaviour adds its text texture after the boat's first tesselation. Not
    fixed.

## The seventh client test (2026-10-02, Magelan 0.3.6): the user's 31 chalk notes

The user walked carrack #290 with the surveyor's chalk (`magelan-notes.json`, a screenshot each). Notes 1–3 were taken
with her already damaged by the user's own gunfire (that damage isn't a bug). Built as Magelan 0.3.7, not seen in the
client. Positions below are the game's ship-local ones (x forward, y up from her waterline, z starboard); the generator
draws in heights 1.5 higher. Views: `docs/views/notes/note-<n>.png` (flat) and `tex-note-<n>.png` (textured, lit as the
game lights her), and the new `docs/views/carrack-tex-*.png` named below.

1. **"Cannot interact - place pot, firewood and food"** (the look-at box said "Woodwork (the forecastle)"). The
   firebed is sunk inside the hearth's bricks, which are solid in her collision, so the crosshair's ray met the bricks
   before the hearth fixture's selection box and `ShipFittings.Pick` never picked it: no right-click reached the hearth.
   Now a fixture's own body in her collision (`ShipFittings.BodyOf`: the hearth's and the old stove's `Box`, their
   bricks) is the fixture: the ray meeting it picks the fixture there. So right-click opens the hearth's window, Shift +
   right-click puts fuel or food in, a pot goes on, a firestarter or torch lights it (all as built in 0.3.6). Checks:
   offline, from where note 1 was written, from over the firebed and on its forward end it's picked, not the side over
   its hood nor the deck by it; the self-test, the same through her own ray on the server.
2. **"Texture is glitching - like changing color"** and 3. **"Texture on hood glitches - like changing darkness"**
   (both the copper smoke hood; the crosshair passed through it to the planking). Each face of the hood was drawn
   twice in the same place, once facing out and once in. The game draws her mesh with culling off, so the two fought
   for every pixel, lit by different normals: light and dark flickering. Now the hood is a sheet with a thickness, its
   inside its own face 0.015 in (`firebox`, `sheet`). Not its light: the fire's light only flickers while it burns
   (`HearthGlow`). Engine notes: "Two quads in the same place flicker".
4. **"Rope knot is not anymore going around"** (the cable's round turn on the port riding bitt). `helix` made its
   points from the turns' count, and a turn the other way (port: -1) had two points: a straight rope through the post.
   Now it's sized by the turns' size; and the turn is 0.25 round the post (clear of its corners, 0.12 + the cable's
   0.075), so the cable runs on aft in one straight line through the bulkhead to the capstan. View `carrack-tex-bitt-turn`.
5. **"There is hole in bow where both hull sides meet. Should be visible some keel or similar beam"**. The inside
   planking of both sides stopped just short of the centreline where they meet at the stem, a slit with the stem's tar
   (and the figurehead) behind it. Now there's an **apron** up the inside of the stem, from the hold to under the
   forecastle's deck, 0.34 wide, its after face where the planking is that far out, and a **knighthead** inside the
   forecastle's tip from its deck to its rail (`centreline_timbers`). View `carrack-tex-bow-apron`.
6. **"Hole in top of side board where curvature ends"** (the rail cap where the sheer sweeps up to the forecastle).
   The cap along the sweep was offset straight out and in from the hull a little under its top, the waist's cap square to
   the hull at its top: their ends didn't meet, a sliver of a gap on top. Now the sweep's cap starts and ends exactly on
   the edges of the caps either side (`raised_strip` returns its top's edges, `sweep_cap` takes them), its edges square to
   the hull's line at the bulwark's top, its faces as deep as theirs. All three castle breaks. View
   `carrack-tex-fc-break-cap`.
7. **"Interruption in side rib and support pole for boat stand"** (the boat gallows' post at x 2.0). The waist's rail
   cap ran across the post, which stood on the cap, and the frame below stopped under it. Now the cap stops against each
   post (cut out from x+0.01 to x+0.24, its ends closed) and the frame runs up to the bulwark's top, where the post goes
   on: one timber from the deck through the rail to the beam. View `carrack-tex-gallows-post`.
8. **"Stair railings have too small quantity of vertical poles and too thin diameter"** and 9. **"Railing poles should
   be larger diameter"**. The turned balusters are about two thirds stouter (0.09 across at the neck, 0.18 at the
   belly, square-ish blocks at foot and head), spaced 0.32 on the castle fronts' balustrades (0.26 before, with thinner
   ones). Each stair's handrail now stands on a square newel post at its foot and head and a turned baluster on every
   tread (six; it was two turned posts and one thin stanchion), and the rail is squared (0.1 × 0.08, was a 0.035
   pole). Views `carrack-tex-stair-balusters`, `tex-note-9`.
10. **"Aft wall texture is dark like tar covered on inside"**, 14. **"Texture here is also too dark"**, 15. **"Here and in
    other corner texture is different for rest of the wall"**. Two causes:
    - The flat transom's quads (`planking.transom`) were wound the wrong way, inside and out: the inside's normal
      pointed aft, out of her. The game lights a ship's mesh by its normal (engine notes), so the inside of the stern
      was lit as a face turned from the light: dark, like tarred wood, though its texture is plain oak. Fixed in
