      `transom`, so the outside is lit right too (the rowing boat's old launch, `skiff`, regenerated with it).
    - The mesh's "auto" quads were put in the interior (lit as inside) or the exterior by their middle's cell: planking
      over a cabin's corner, past the bulkhead's cell, and the bulkheads' end strips (exactly on a cell's edge) came out
      exterior, lit differently from the rest of the wall. Now a quad is lit as the space just in front of it is
      (`Design._write_skin`), and the strips stand 2 mm into it.
    `texview.py` now lights its views by the normals as the game does, so a wrong normal shows there.
11. **"Top of side boards do not nicely connect"** (the poop's rail caps at the transom's corners). The taffrail was a
    thin board over the side caps' ends, wider than them and askew to them. Now it's a stouter cap (0.51 deep, 0.16
    high) a little higher than the side caps, its ends flush with their outer faces, its underside below their tops,
    so they run into its fore side; a moulding under it. View `carrack-tex-taffrail-corner`.
12. **"Not sure what this post is supposed to do. I would assume flag or banner pole or lantern."** It's the stern
    lantern's place. Now it reads as one: a stout post on the taffrail's middle with an iron crane from its head out over
    the water, braced from below, a round iron plate with a little rail at its end the lantern stands on. Its place
    shows in the crosshair even empty and with nothing in hand (`FixtureDef.Shown`, "shown" in the design): the look-at
    box says "Stern lantern", the hint "Stern lantern (right-click with a lantern)". View `carrack-tex-stern-lantern`.
13. **"There is weird gap between floor and side board"** (the poop deck along the transom). `_ceil_grid` took the
    inside planking's width over each cell from three samples, and a sample a hair aft of the inside of the transom
    counted as zero: the whole row of cells along the transom's inside (0.25 deep, all across her) was made part of the
    hull's shell, solid and not drawn, from the hold to the rail: a slot in every deck there, and an unseen wall a
    quarter block before the transom. Now samples past the planking's ends are left out (a cell may reach a little into
    the 0.3 of the transom). The decks reach the transom and you can stand up to it. Also every deck's edge strip had
    its caulked seam along its inner edge, a dark band beside the side planking: see 27.
16. **"Back wall should have some vertical side frame members. How in real ships?"** Upright stern timbers stand proud
    of the inside of the transom, in the great cabin and the poop cabin: one between each two of the gallery's lights and
    one outboard of the outermost each side, from the deck to the beams over it, leaning with its rake
    (`stern_timbers`); the frames at the transom's corners are the fashion pieces (see 30). The windows stay. View
    `carrack-tex-stern-timbers`, `tex-note-16`.
17. **"Would it be realistic to get some windows on this wall?"** (the poop cabin's front bulkhead) and 18. **"Would it
    be realistic to get some window on this wall and decorations?"** (the great cabin's front, toward the waist). Two
    small glazed windows beside each door, 0.75 square, their sills 1.0 over the floor, glazed like the cabins' other
    windows (a mullion, two transoms, six panes, stained glass in the top two) in the middle of the wall, cased both
    sides; solid (collision). The poop cabin's port one is outboard of the spawn plate (z -2.7..-1.95), its starboard one
    inboard of the poop stair (1.0..1.75); the great cabin's either side (1.0..1.75). The great cabin's front is dressed:
    a pilaster each side of each window (a base and a capital), a moulded cornice under the breastwork's plinth, a rail
    at the windows' sill and a moulded panel under each (`front_dress`; `FRONT_WINDOWS`). Views `carrack-tex-poop-front`,
    `tex-note-17`, `tex-note-18`, `carrack-tex-cabin-fronts`.
19. **"Pooping seats face wrong direction"**. The heads faced the side (their lids propped against the bulwark, a
    partition between them). Now they stand near the forecastle's tip either side of the bowsprit (x 18.25 + 4 in the
    design, z ±0.62), each a box seat with its hole, a high back on its fore side so you sit facing aft, inboard, the lid
    swung up against it; the hole goes down through the forecastle's overhang over the sea; a hand rope over each from
    the bulwark to the bowsprit. View `carrack-tex-heads`.
20. **"This mast should probably go inside floor? or how connect?"** and 21. **"Mast appears to be supported by nothing
    or ropes (in compression) in the bow"**. The bowsprit's heel lay on a chock on the forecastle deck and it passed a
    metre and a half over the tip, the gammoning hanging down to the rail. Now its heel is stepped on the main deck in the
    forecastle against the foremast's fore side (a chock with cheeks under it), held down by a pair of bitts with a cap
    across a little forward; it rises (26.6°, a carrack's steep steeve) through the forecastle's deck, framed by partners,
    and rests on a saddle on the stem head at the forecastle's tip (the post carried up 0.35 over the rail, cheeks either
    side); the gammoning is five turns over it just aft of the saddle and down through a slot in the stem head, pulling it
    down. Its end is where it was (x 28); the fore stay comes down to it a little lower. It's solid where it passes
    through the forecastle and over its deck. Views `carrack-tex-bowsprit-heel`, `carrack-tex-bowsprit-stem`,
    `tex-note-20`, `tex-note-21`.
22. **"There is a hole in deck"** (the forecastle deck's tip). Its last cells, narrower than a cell, were cleared (they'd
    poke through the planking), and the edge strips only closed decks out from a drawn cell. Now where a deck's cells are
    all cleared at a hull's narrow tip, it's carried on out to the planking from the centreline both sides (the lower deck
    at the bow, note 26, and the stern below the transom, note 31, too). The bowsprit now comes up over the tip anyway.
23. **"Anchor holes overlap with frame ribs"**. The port hawse's inner end was on the frame at x 18.625. Now each hawse
    hole goes through a hawse piece: a broad timber (0.64 × 0.8) in the frame's place round it, the frame stopping under
    and over it, the hole's inner end lined with iron on its face (period: the hawse pieces filled between the cant frames
    at the bow). The hawse stays where it was (the cable leads fair to the capstan). View `carrack-tex-hawse-piece`.
24. **"Companion way my mind needs some redesign to include doors or hatch. Maybe higher with door?"** A companion house
    over the companionway (x 1.25–4.0, 1.5 wide): oak walls on its coaming to 2.5 over the deck, corner posts, a moulded
    cap, a roof of planks a little cambered (0.07 under the boat gallows' beam), a vanilla door (`game:door-solid-oak`) in
    its after end on the coaming as its sill, swinging out aft onto the deck (clear of the mainmast). You go in upright and
    down the ladder toward the bow as before; the ladder's handrails run to eyebolts on its walls (no stanchions in the
    doorway). Shut, the door is solid, the ladder's top is gated by it (`gate` on the ladder: coming up you wait at the
    top, as at the hold hatch) and the lower deck is a closed room; open (`unseals` lowerdeck), it isn't. In the
    flooding (`DamageLayout.DownfloodPoints`) a door with `unseals` is a way below like a hatch: over its sill, only while
    open, and counts for `OpenHatchShare`; `DeckShare` no longer stands for the companionway. Checks: walking (the
    doorway's headroom, the walls and roof, a shut door stopping you at it and at the ladder's top; down the ladder and on
    into the hold, and back, with it open), flooding (8.8 t/s below with the sea 0.4 over her rail and the door open,
    1.7 shut), the self-test (shut on a new ship, the lower deck closed; open, the way down). Views
    `carrack-tex-companion-house`, `carrack-tex-companion-in`.
25. **"Anchor rope pile should be straight under"**. The cable went down a hole beside the barrel and aslant to the
    tier beside the spindle. Now the hole in the main deck is straight over the tier's coil (10.6, -1.05, clear of the deck
    beams), the cable leaves the barrel where it leads fair to it, lies along the deck to it, and drops straight into the
    coil's middle. View `carrack-tex-cable-tier`.
26. and 31. **"hole in the floor"** (the lower deck at the bow, x 17.5; at the stern, x -13.75). At the bow: see 22 (the
    deck carried out to its tip) and 5 (the apron). At the stern: the deck likewise, and the inner sternpost (29) stands on
    the centreline there.
27. and 28. **"Weird direction textures"** (the main deck's underside where it meets the bow, from the lower deck; the
    lower deck's edge at the bow). The decks' edge strips had their planks from v 0 at each strip's own edge (a caulked
    seam all along the edge, the dark band of 13) and, where the hull closes in fast, their quads were trapezoids with the
    plank seams slanting along the hull's curve, in steps from strip to strip. Now each strip's planks continue the deck's
    cells' own (v from z within the cell's block, u from x) and it's square to them, in up to four narrow pieces where the
    hull curves in (each out to the further of its ends, a hair into the planking). Views `tex-note-27`, `tex-note-28`.
29. **"Rudder visible from inside"**. The rudder turns on a straight axis from the keel's end to the transom, and the
    stern's line curves aft of it (by up to 0.43 at the lower deck's level): its stock, the gudgeons (0.41 ahead of the
    axis) and the sternpost came forward into her, inside the inside planking, under and over the lower deck. Now an
    **inner sternpost** stands up the inside of the stern on the centreline from the hold to under the tiller, 0.44 wide,
    its forward face 0.47 ahead of the rudder's axis (and never behind where the planking is that wide), so everything
    of the rudder's inside her is inside it (`fit_checks`: 0.06 to spare); solid where it stands in the hold and on the
    lower deck. (Straightening the stern's line or moving the rudder aft would change her hull or stand the rudder off
    her keel.) View `carrack-tex-stern-post-in`.
30. **"Last rib partially disappears"** (the aftmost frame, x -14.375). Its after face was past where the inside
    planking ends against the transom below y 0.6, so it wasn't drawn there. Now a frame whose after face is past that end
    stands that much further forward there, hugging the transom's corner (a fashion piece), down to where the stern
    closes to the post (y about -0.65).

Also from this test:
- **Cannon balls in the water** (the user: "does the cannon ball travel in water like in air? It should not."). It kept
  0.9 of its speed going in and the water's drag was 0.3 a block: it stayed dangerous 3–4 blocks. A real 9-lb iron
  ball's drag in water is about 0.42 a metre (3·Cd·ρ water / (8·ρ iron·r), Cd 0.47), and the blow on the surface takes
  much of its speed. Now `CannonDef.WaterEntry` 0.6 (a third of its energy kept), `WaterDrag` 0.45 a block (three fifths
  of what's left gone in each), `Spent` 13 blocks/s (7 kJ, below what scrapes a plank): it's spent within about 2
  blocks. A ball striking her right at her waterline holes her as in the air; through half a block of water it about
  cracks a plank (28 kJ) and starts its seams; one falling short does nothing. Ricochets as before. The offline check
  and the README's numbers changed; the self-test's broadsides (laid under the target's waterline from 7 blocks off):
  about a third of the balls are spent short of her now and the rest strike at 23 kJ on average, she's still holed (6
  panels) and goes down in about 3 minutes.
- **`.magelan damagecheck`** (client; the orchestrating session found that hull damage was never drawn: the
  `DamageLook.CheckedMs` overflow, fixed in `ShipRenderer`): for the ship in the crosshair (or the nearest), what
  drawing her damage has: her mesh file and her hull's planking sorted into panels (its chunks), whether drawing damage
  failed this session, her damage state against her parts, how many panels look other than sound (and holed), the chunks
  built and waiting, her sails as drawn.
- **The user's note 32** (#313, "Hull starboard station 9 band 6", holed, drawn as plain planking): checked offline that
  this panel of the great cabin's side over the main deck has its own tarred planking outside (12 quads, in the exterior
  group), and holed, the hole is cut out of it (0.82 m² against the ellipse's 0.75) and out of the ceiling inside (0.83),
  the tar gets the holed overlay round it (334 pieces), and the reveal closes it. So with the overflow fixed it should be
  drawn.
- **Generator checks** (`fit_checks`, printed, the build stops if one fails): the inner sternpost ahead of the rudder's
  parts inside her, the bowsprit's underside on the saddle (to 0.01), its partners between the forecastle's front and the
  heads, the heads clear of it, the hawse pieces, the companion house under the gallows' beam, the cable's hole clear of
  the deck beams.
- **Tests:** offline 486 → 492 (the hearth picked; the companion house's walking and its door in the flooding; the stern
  lantern shown; note 32's panel; the cannon's water check rewritten); the self-test: the companion house's door and the
  hearth picked through her ray; the broadsides check reads the balls spent short of her. All pass (offline 492 ok;
  the self-test's 213 lines, ALL OK).
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - The companion house's door at its after end, swinging out aft; a solid oak door (a windowed one would light the
    ladder); its sill the coaming (0.25); the house's roof 0.07 under the boat gallows' beam.
  - The bowsprit stepped on the main deck against the foremast (rather than on the forecastle deck), steeved 26.6°, its
    heel and bitts in the forecastle room (you walk round it there).
  - The heads near the tip, facing aft.
  - The inner sternpost, a big timber up the middle of the stern on the lower deck (the rudder's line could instead be
    straightened, which changes her stern).
  - The poop cabin's windows asymmetric (the port one outboard of the spawn plate); the great cabin's front dressed with
    pilasters, a cornice, a rail and panels.
  - The cannon ball's water numbers (0.6 kept going in, 0.45 a block, spent at 13 b/s).
- **Seen but not changed:** the lower deck's edge near the bow and stern still shows the deck's cells stepping in toward
  the hull under the strips' planks (now in line with the deck's); the aftmost frame ends where the stern closes to the
  post, a little over the lower deck; the bitts' crosspiece and the bowsprit in the forecastle (0.38 apart).

## The eighth client test (2026-10-02, Magelan 0.3.7): the user's chalk notes 33–54 and seven extras

The user walked carrack #2980 (0.3.7) with the chalk again (notes 33–54, `magelan-notes.json`, a screenshot each). Not
yet built or seen in the client. Positions are the game's ship-local ones (x forward, y up from her waterline, z
starboard); the generator draws in heights 1.5 higher. Views: `docs/views/notes/tex-note-<n>.png` (textured, lit as the
game lights her) and the `docs/views/carrack-tex-*.png` named below. Not here (the user is deciding them): the
companionway's place (46), moving the gates (50) and the pin rail (49), more guns, lidded ports, scuppers, an oven (51),
glass doors (52), paint (53). The generator's side first:

33. – 36. **"Here is some holes and distorted textures"**, **"Hole in outer hull"**, **"Distorted textures"**, **"Holes
    in bottom part and textures distorted"** (the poop's overhang over the transom, both quarters, and from under it).
    Four causes:
    - The hull's own side under the overhang was planked up to its underside (OH_Y) along an outline from `stern_x`,
      which at OH_Y is the overhang's after end, 1.25 aft of the transom: the top strake ran on aft across the transom to
      there, skewed and twisted (the smeared band under the ledge, the inside planking's top strake showing outside), and
      inside, the inside planking's top strake swept across the transom's inside (note 47). Now the strakes under the
      overhang start at the hull's own transom (`sterncastle_low`).
    - The overhang's sides (`poop_hw`) end on the transom's corner; rounding put their first point a hair aft of it at
      some heights, where the hull is 0, and that side's outline began with a run across the transom: the strakes over
      the overhang skewed too. Now they end exactly on it.
    - Its underside was strips across her (`flat_between`): behind the transom each one band of a plank stretched across
      up to four blocks, the strip over the transom's corner cut short of it (a hole), their inner edge a coarser line
      than the hull's top strake's (slits of light along the ledge). Now `overhang_underside`: flat at OH_Y, out to the
      overhanging sides' bottom strake's own ring, in over the top of the hull's side by 0.05 (into its thickness),
      behind the transom from the centreline; its planks fore and aft and straight, textured by their place in its plane
      as the decks' cells are (`Design.add_planar`, below).
    - The frames of the overhang behind the transom stood on its underside, their feet in its plane (flickering seen
      from below); now they stand on the quarterdeck like the others.
    Views `carrack-tex-poop-overhang`, `carrack-tex-poop-overhang-under`, `tex-note-33`…`36`.
37. and 38. **"Hole in hull"**, **"Texture distortion"** (the forecastle's raked underside by the stem): the same strips,
    the same stretched band ("dark stretched triangles"), their inner edge coarser than the bow's top strake (the slit by

41. **"The pump in my mind needs to be bronze"**: its chamber above the deck is cast bronze (a flange on the deck, three

45. **"Cannons in my mind need to be a bit shorter … they needed time and space … Cannon ropes attach to end of cannon

### The agreed redesign (the generator's side)

The user's decisions after the notes (notes 46, 49–53 and the decorating turn): built in the generator, not yet seen in
the client. Heights below are the design's (the game's 1.5 lower).

1. **The companionway in the great cabin** (note 46: "I am thinking of placing companion way here"). The companion
   house and its ladder in the waist are gone (the deck closed there). A stair (`CABIN_STAIR`, `COMPANION`
   = x −6.25..−3.25, z −4.0..−3.0) goes down aft along the great cabin's port side from just inside its front wall to the
   lower deck: twelve steps a quarter up and a quarter along (45°, easier than the castles' stairs), treads, stringers,
   a squared handrail on turned balusters on its outboard side (`castle_stairs`); its opening the stair's whole length
   (at 45° anyone going down passes under the deck's edge only from its lowest steps: the header there is 2.0 over the
   step under it), the beams it cuts ending on carlings, a header across its after end; on the deck a railing like the
   castle fronts' along its inboard side (from 0.75 aft of the front wall, the way onto its head) and across its after
   end to the side (`companion_rail`). In through the great cabin's windowed door, which is now the way below: it has
   `unseals: "lowerdeck"` (open, the lower deck isn't closed; in the flooding it's the door below, over its sill on the
   main deck). It's walked (`d.stairs`, exported as `stairs`), no ladder. The glazed waist hatch and the hold hatch stay
   (x 5.25..7.75).
2. **The gates** (note 50) in the bay aft of the boat gallows' after post (`PORT_X = (0.75, 2.0)`), hung on it at their
   forward inboard corner, opening 88° along its after face; the rope ladders outside followed. The main channel ends at
   x 0.575 and its foremost chain plate at 0.275 (clear of the bay: nothing moved). The pin rail (note 49) now ends at
   0.5, aft of the gate (it ran to 1.275).
3. **Five guns a side** in the waist's bays (`GUN_X = 2.875, 4.375, 5.875, 7.375, 8.875`; the old gate bay is a gun bay).
   Neighbours train 7.5° toward each other. The forward port gun housed (run back) came 1.12 from the capstan, inside its
   bars' sweep: the **capstan** moved 1.25 inboard and 0.15 forward (x 10.4, z −0.75: every gun, laid anywhere and run
   back, 0.46 clear of its bars and 0.03 of the crew walking them), and so the **pump** moved to the hatch's after end
   (x 4.0, its brake reaching aft, `PUMP_BRAKE`; its spout as before, to starboard): at the hatch's forward end the bars
   would sweep over its brake. The cable leads to the capstan's inboard side as before, its tier under the hole at
   (10.75, 0.2).
4. **Lidded ports**: the waist's bulwark 0.25 higher (`WAIST_RAIL`: the rail cap at MAIN + 1.25; the castle breaks' sweeps
   rise from it, the waist's strakes, frames, the gates and the gallows' posts follow), each port square from 0.25 to 1.0
   over the deck with the bulwark whole over it (a lintel; lined with oak), shut by a lid (`port_lid`): the side's own
   planks, each in line with its strake, tarred outside and oak inside, a little bigger than the port, lying on the
   planking, hinged along its top outside under the rail cap (two iron straps and their knuckles, a ring bolt for its
   lanyard), a moving part `<gun>-lid` turning it outward and up 110° (`LID_OPEN`) by `open:<gun>`. The guns elevate
   to 12° now (15 before: run in at 15° the muzzle's swell met the lintel). Checks (`fit_checks`, `lid_clearances`): laid
   12° up under the lintel run out and running in by 0.05, laid 5° down over the sill by 0.05, run out and laid or
   trained anywhere clear of the lid opened by 0.06, housed (run back 1.1) the muzzle 0.57 in from the shut lid.
5. **Scuppers**: small round holes (r 0.075, `SCUPPER_R`) through the waterway at the deck (`scuppers`): in each gun bay
   the quarter block between the port and the frame aft of it, and the middle of each portless bay of the waist (x
   −1.625, −0.125, 10.375); lead-lined through the side, a lead lip outside and a flange inside; the planking round each
   hole in its strake's texture. Not in her collision (they never were): the flooding's data is `sideCuts`.
6. **Windowed doors** (note 52): the forecastle's and the poop cabin's doors are `game:door-sleek-windowed-oak` too.
7. **The oven** (note 51): vanilla's clay oven (`game:clayoven-south`, its mouth to port) forward of the hearth against
   the starboard side (x 14.0..15.0, z 2.25..3.25), on a brick base as high as the hearth's on an iron tray, bricks up
   behind it, an iron flue from its back up and aft into the hearth's stovepipe over the hood (`oven`). Fixture
   `type: "oven"` with pos, yaw, sel, box, block, fire, cook, chimney, room, like the hearth's.
8. **No built-in furniture** in the carrack: the beds (the captain's too), chests, the chart table and stools, the hold's

   a lantern on each to start with (`startPieces`: plain copper, brass in the cabins and the stern lantern).
9. **Paint zones**, now tagged where each part is made (`Design.paint`, `q["paint"]`: a zone, or "outside", "inside",
   "frame", "ceiling", "wall" sorted by where it is; a dict of either by material) instead of by the helpers' names and
   boxes' sizes. 30 zones (the front capitals with the front's mouldings, the stairs' stringers with the railings; the
   companion house's gone). The frames are cut at every deck and the inside strakes at the castle walls (`skin(splits)`)
   so a piece shows in one space (10 small pieces still do). Written per the decorating contract: `paintZones` in her
   definition, `paintQuads` and `paintFaces` in her mesh (voxel faces found by their cells: her boxes merge differently
   once her heights are moved), checked against `docs/views/paint/zones.json` at every build.
10. **Decorating data** (`decor_data`): `stairs` (all four, the companion's too), `keepClear` (61 boxes: the windows and
    lights from inside, the gun ports and the guns' run back with the gun captain's place, the hearth's and the oven's
    fronts, the pump, the capstan, the hatches, the helm, the doors' and gates' swing, the spawn spot, the ladders' ends),
    `sideCuts` (the openings over the deck: each gun port with its lid's gun, each entry port with its gate, each
    scupper with its radius).
- **Tests** (offline, updated for the layout, with reasons in them): the berths check is room for 8 bunks on the lower
  deck; the stern lantern's place is its hook; the capstan's walk and position; the hold hatch's walk beside the pump's
  pipe; the companion stair walked down and up, and the great cabin's door the way below; the chests', beds', cargo

  oven. Offline 461 ok, ALL OK; `-- cannons` ALL OK.

Code side: see below.

### Code side: bubbles, the chalk's caret, jagged holes, the guns' buttons, the pump's water

1. **"Water bubbles in cargo hold"** (after the sixth test's fixes and Sea 0.3.1's). The rest of them were vanilla's
   ambient water specks: `ModSystemAmbientParticles.AsyncParticleSpawnTick` (VSEssentials) spawns, up to 50 times a
   tick round the player (±16 across, ±10 up and down), a light blue-white mote (`liquidParticles`: 0.1 cube, glowing,
   two seconds, `ShouldDieInAir`) wherever the block there is a full liquid. The hold below her waterline is full of the
   world's sea (the ship is an entity), so they float in it; particles aren't cut by the water shader's dry clip, and
   they die only where the block isn't liquid (`ParticleGeneric.TickNow`). Not the cause: the still saltwater blocks
   have no particles of their own (`saltwater.json`, `particlePropertiesByType: "saltwater-still-*": []`; `BlockWater`
   only passes the tick to its behaviours, FiniteSpreadingLiquid has none), breath bubbles (`AirBubbleParticles` is
   never spawned), Sea's and Magelan's own particles, and her hold's cells (every air cell below the main deck is in
   `DryMask`: the offline check). The same family: rain's splashes and a storm's spray on the water's surface
   (`WeatherSimulationParticles.SpawnRainParticles`, `SpawnDustParticles`, at the rain map's top, which inside a hull is
   the sea's level, under her decks, seen on the lower deck with a hatch open). **Sea** (would be 0.3.4): `DryParticles`,
   transpilers on those three methods route each `IAsyncParticleManager.Spawn` through a check: a mote that dies in air
   spawned in a dry place, or a splash or spray (a small spawn box) whose middle is dry, isn't spawned; the rain itself
   and storm dust (spawned over tens of blocks), summer air motes and fireflies are. `.sea` counts what's left out.
   Checks: Sea's offline tests read the game's own methods as Harmony does and find the spawns replaced (3, 2, 3), and
   a speck in a dry hold, a splash on the water there, are left out, outside kept (4 checks); Sea's self-test the patches
   in and the same on the server.
2. **"Chalk tool notes break cursor position when changing line on text"**. Vanilla's text area
   (`GuiElementEditableTextBase.OnKeyPress`): when a typed character makes the line wrap, it wraps the whole text again,
   then puts the caret back by its old line and place in it, clamped to the new, shorter line, and adds one: the caret
   lands a few characters back on the next line and what's typed next goes into the middle of the word (typing a
   sentence into a narrow box with vanilla's code gives "the p wheezes when the s rolls … humipp"). And `OnKeyEnter`
   splits the line in its list without wrapping again: Enter at the end of a wrapped line leaves a line in the list that
   isn't in the text, drawn until something wraps it away. Also vanilla's text area grows by its text's height in screen
   pixels into its unscaled height (too tall when the GUI is scaled, every line break counted twice), and nothing
   scrolled the comment to the caret. Now `ChalkTextArea` (the comment box): after a keypress the caret goes right after
   the character typed, counted through the whole text; after any edit the lines are what wrapping the text gives;
   up and down keep the caret's place across the line; its height is its lines' in the dialog's units; the dialog
   scrolls the comment to keep the caret's line in sight (`ScrollToShow`), keeps its scroll when the text changes, and
   the mouse wheel over it scrolls it. Checks (offline, with the game's own text area and Cairo, the font measured as in
   the game at GUI scale 1.5): typing a sentence that wraps (the caret after each character), typing at the start, Enter
   at the end of a wrapped line and typing after it, Enter at the end of a line that wrapped (the lines the text's), up
   and down, Backspace joining lines, Delete, the height and the scroll (10 checks; `-- chalktext` alone).
3. **"Holes in ship are very round. Can we make them jagged? Could be also square and two sides jagged as broken
   boards"**. A hole was an ellipse cut out of the planking (marching squares on a fine grid) with a ring of 28 quads
   round it. Now it's broken planking (`HullDamageSkin.HoleOf`, `HoleStrake`): the strakes round a holed panel are found
   from the mesh itself (its planking quads near it, joined where they share an end, and where they're the same strake
   past a port); those whose middles are within the hole's half-height of its middle (and the one through it) are broken
   through from seam to seam, each off at its own length either way along the planks (a share of the hole's half-length,
   the outer ones shorter), its ends splintered: two or three teeth in and out by up to a fifth of it, the break slanting,
   sometimes a splinter sticking half its length out into the hole. The ceiling is broken through the same strakes, its
   own lengths and teeth. A wale or rail cap over a broken strake (marked as planking, standing out from it, or its top
   and bottom faces) is broken where the strake under it is, its ends closed back to the planking. The cut is exact: each
   quad of a broken strake is cut between the ends across it in slabs (between the points where an end turns, crosses
   another or the quad's own ends), every piece a quad between straight edges. The reveal (raw oak) runs round the
   outline on the planking (up the right ends, across the top seam, down the left ends, across the bottom seam) to the
   same points on the ceiling, a face for the broken ends going through the thickness (two triangles where a step between
   strakes goes the other way round inside). Fixed by the panel (its middle off the panel's as before, now the same at
   every size); a bigger step holds the smaller: more strakes (with the hole's half-height), every end further out, the
   same teeth. Checks: what's cut out of the planking outside and the ceiling is what lies between the strakes' ends
   (0.454 and 0.407 m² against 0.452 and 0.405), nothing left inside, the edges where they're broken off, the reveal the
   planking's thickness deep; whole strakes from seam to seam (open just inside the top and bottom seams, not over or
   under them), each its own length, the ends jagged, the ceiling its own way; the same every time and growing with the
   step; note 32's panel as before by the new areas. `-- damage-views` now lists each hole's strakes. Seen (texview's
   damage views and close-ups): planks broken off at different lengths with toothed, splintered ends, flat top and
   bottom along the seams; from outside the broken ends' faces show through the thickness.
4. **"Cannon fire buttons, in my mind, need reversing right to left."** Hold right mouse by a gun (it in the crosshair,
   at its gun captain's place, or by its breech with nothing else in reach in the crosshair: a door, the gate, a block,
   a boat, something of hers that what's in hand mends) to lay it where you look; left-click fires the one being laid
   (or laid a moment ago and still by it), the one in the crosshair or the gun captain's (`GunButtons`, the client's
   `GunClick`); Ctrl + right-click a gun still stands to it, Shift leaves it. A left-click by a gun is the gun's: the
   game's attack, swing and breaking are prevented (`InWorldAction` handled; vanilla repeats the action every 0.25 s
   while held: one shot a click). A right-hold while pumping, mending or lighting the hearth stays theirs; with the
   cannons switched off a right-click on a gun says so instead of trying to fire it. Hints (`hint-gun`, `hud-gun-keys`,
   `-seat`), comments and the README changed. Check: the mapping and the hints (LayTests).
5. **Note 39, "Make pump show water flowing when there is water pumping"**. The server now tells clients what the pumps
   take out of her (`PumpedRate`, cubic blocks a second: the flooding's own figure, nothing while the hold is dry) and
   which pumps are worked (`PumpsWorked`), with her state. `PumpClient`: while a pump is worked and brings water up, water
   gushes out of its spout (the fixture's `spout` and `spoutDir`, the generator's; without them 0.65 over the pump and
   0.45 to its right, out and a little down: `PumpJet`) and falls onto the deck beside it (vanilla's watering can's
   water: cubes tinted as the world's water there; 0.33 s, landing 0.5 out), splashing there, moving with her, in gushes
   with the brake's strokes, 42 particles a second with one at the brake, 66 with two; vanilla's pouring sound
   (`sounds/effect/water-pour`) once a stroke, louder with more water. A dry pump only clanks (the server's pump sound,
   quieter, as before). Checks: the spout from her design, the water falling clear of the pump onto the deck, the
   fallback, the flow (none dry or unworked, shared between pumps), its particles and gushes (offline, 6); the
   self-test: what the pump brings up is synced.

Also:
- **The client log** (the session with 0.3.7 + Sea 0.3.3): nothing from Magelan or Sea but its two load lines and
  the hull textures' line; 22 warnings "Audio File game:sounds/effect/mediumexplosion.ogg is a stereo sound but loaded
  as a locational sound, will not attenuate correctly": the cannons' report (`CannonShots`, `PlaySoundAt` at the gun)
  is a stereo file, so it's heard at full loudness however far off. Now (asked after the report) the report is
  vanilla's large explosion (`effect/largeexplosion`, mono, 4 s: its OGG header's channel count read), placed at the gun,
  fading with distance out to 200 blocks (`CannonDef.Report`, `ReportRange`, `ReportVolume`). Check (CannonTests): the
  report's file is mono, its range at least 150.
- **Tests:** offline 539 ok, ALL OK (of these the comment box's 10, the pump's water 6, the gun buttons 1, the holes 5,
  3 of them rewritten); Sea's offline tests + 4 (the water particles), ALL OK; the self-tests' new checks (Sea's water
  particles, the pump's synced flow) not run here.

### Code side, part two: the user's decisions (tumbling shot, lidded ports, the oven, the door below)

- **Shot tumbling** (the user: the far side of a sound hull should usually be cracked and leaking, not holed). A ball that
  breaks through her planking (the hull's panels took some of its energy) tumbles and throws splinters: it keeps
  `DamageDef.ThroughKeeps` (0.75) of what's left (`ShipDamage.Blow`). The 144 kJ ball from 30 blocks: her near side holed,
  her far side cracked (hurt 0.60; it was holed at 0.80); a second ball the same way holes it, and so does one in through
  the first one's hole (nothing broken going in: it doesn't tumble). Check (CannonTests): those three. README's numbers.
  The self-test's broadsides strike through the water at about 23 kJ (they don't go through her): unchanged.
- **Gun port lids, housed or run out** (the generator's square lidded ports, five guns a side: each gun's `lid`, the lid's
  moving part's code, its source `open:<gun>`; `port` x0 y0 x1 y1). A gun with a lid is run out with its port open while
  its code is among her open fittings (synced, saved), else housed: run in (its "fire:" moving part held back:
  `GunRecoil` takes the larger of the recoil and `GunHoused`), the lid shut, it can't fire. New ships' guns are housed.
  Holding right mouse on a housed gun (laying) first opens its port and runs it out (`RunOutMs` 2 s; the lid opens at
  once, the gun runs out over the two seconds as drawn; the server lays it once it's out; the HUD says "being run out"),
  then lays it. A left-click on a housed gun says "Open the port first: hold right-click on the gun.", on one being run
  out "It's being run out". **Shift + right-click** a gun runs it in and shuts its port (the lid stays open till it's in).
  The `/magelan fire` command and the self-test run them out at once. Flooding: a lidded port's sill is a way in only
  while it's open (`DownfloodPoint.OpenFixture` = the gun); shut, the side there is the bulwark over the port. Checks
  (DamageTests): the open sills 0.25 over the deck, the shut bulwark 1.25; with the sea 0.5 over the deck, 0.18 t/s below
  with the ports shut, 0.26 open; the self-test: a new ship's guns housed and refusing to fire, one opened and refusing
  while it's run out, housed again. Not done: a shut lid isn't solid in her collision (a shot or a hand goes through the
  port as before).
- **The clay oven** (the user's note 51; the generator's `oven` fixture: pos, block `game:clayoven-south` as it stands,
  sel, box, fire, cook, chimney, room; in the redesign's design). Vanilla's `BlockEntityOven` rebuilt aboard as the
  hearth was: its `InventoryOven` (its first slot the fuel or a thing to bake, four to bake in) kept on the ship
  (`ShipOven.cs`), its burn tick, heating and baking ported number for number and pure (`OvenFire`: six logs burn 60 s and
  heat it to about 280 °C; once it's out what's in it heats from it, browns over its browning point, rises or sinks as
  vanilla's BakingProperties say, and baked through becomes its result (or its smelting), freshness carried over); used
  as vanilla's is (right-click: firewood in while it's cold and empty, something bakeable in once it's out, an empty hand
  takes out the last, the same thing in hand again takes it out; a lit torch or a firestarter lights it, at once, the
  firestarter worn); saved and synced in `magelanOven`; the look-at box as vanilla's (its heat, preheat or reheat, what's
  in it and how hot), a hint; drawn (`ShipRenderer.OvenPiece`): vanilla's oven block, its firewood as the fuel's oven
  shape, what's baking in its quadrants (or one large thing) as vanilla's displays draw them, risen as it bakes; its
  crackle while it burns and its smoke out of the hearth's stovepipe (`HearthClient`); its body (the base) picks it; its
  contents go to her wreck or drop. Config `Oven`. Checks: offline 3 (`-- oven`: the temperature curve, six logs against
  one, four loaves of dough rising and baking 107 s after the fire's out, nothing heating while it burns); the self-test,
  in a design with an oven: fuelled, lit, synced. Not done: vanilla's flames inside it (only its smoke and sound), its
  warmth.
- **The door below** (the companionway into the great cabin: its door unseals the lower deck). The flooding and the
  rooms already follow the data (a door on the main deck with `unseals` is a way below like a hatch, over its sill; the
  room it unseals isn't closed while it's open); the offline check and the self-test now take whichever door that is
  (`door-greatcabin` now) instead of the companion house's by name.
- **Tests:** the `cannons` and `damage` checks ALL OK but two of the generator's mid-redesign (the gun count, now
  following the design, and the guns' clearance against the new ports' tops at y 3.0: the generator's); the full suite
  stops at the companionway's walk (the generator's redesign removed the "companionway" ladder) until the generator's
  checks catch up.

### Decorating: mount points, pieces, paint

The user's decisions of 2026-10-02 (any vanilla block aboard, picked up again, paint by zones), built in C#; not seen in
the client. Files: `src/Ship/ShipMounts.cs`, `ShipPieces.cs`, `VanillaPieceBlock.cs`, `ShipPaint.cs`, `ShipDecor.cs`
(EntityShip), `src/MagelanDecor.cs` (MagelanMod: packets, paint plans, commands), `src/Network/DecorPackets.cs`,
`src/Client/DecoClient.cs`, `PieceRenderer.cs`, `PaintRenderer.cs` (ShipRenderer), textures `block/paint/*`, `block/dye/*`;
small hooks in `EntityShip.cs`, `ShipParts.cs` (`ShipPartKind.Piece`), `ShipDefinition.cs`, `ShipDamageSkin.cs`
(`Build`'s base textures, `TextureIndex`), `ShipWrecks.cs`/`Wreck.cs` (containers at a piece's place), `ShipHud.cs`,
`ShipClientSystem.cs`, `ShipRenderer.cs` (partial), `MagelanMod.cs` (partial), `SelfTest.cs`, `lang/en.json`.
1. **Mount points**, from the definition alone (the same on every side): floor places on every deck but the main top
   (the alignment of the 0.25 grid giving each deck the most, 16 tried); wall spots in the closed rooms (her sides
   and the rooms' ends, rows 0.75 and 1.75 over the floor, the wall even within 0.5, tilted with its lean); hooks (the
   design's `hooks`, and the 0.3.7 lantern places). Kept clear: doorways and a block each side, the gates' swing, hatches
   and the companionway (0.75 round), each gun's room to train and run back (port to behind its gun captain's place, as
   wide as its trail sweeps), the capstan (1.9), the pump (1), the helm's standing spot, the binnacle, the hearth, the
   plate and its spawn spot, ladders (and 1.5 round their ends), boarding points, masts, every other fixture's boxes,
   the design's `stairs` and `keepClear`, and any cell within a block of something standing on air just over a deck (a
   stair's treads, found in the grid). All from the data: the new companionway, guns and gates are taken as they come.
   The carrack with the generator's data of 23:34 (furniture gone, the companionway in the great cabin, 5 guns a side):
   437 floor places (lower deck 171, great cabin 72, hold 59, poop cabin 40, poop deck 40, forecastle deck 23,
   forecastle 14, quarterdeck 11, waist 7), 119 wall spots (lower deck 68, great cabin 25, poop cabin 14, forecastle 12),
   14 hooks; alignments hold (0.5, 0.25), lower (0.75, 0), main (0.5, 0.25), quarter (0.5, 0), forecastle (0.25, 0),
   poop (0.25, 0.25).
2. **The outline**: holding a block, the free mount point in the crosshair (in reach, nearer than any fixture) shows the
   piece's selection box as it would stand, for the way it would face (a bed's or trunk's two places, a painting's two
   spots), as a `WireframeCube` (vanilla's block outline) in her drawn frame; a placed piece in the crosshair shows its
   own. Empty-handed no mount point shows (but the stern lantern's hook, "shown"). The hint says what a click does, or
   why the place won't do.
   With the surveyor's chalk in hand (the user's inspection tool) the free mount point under the crosshair is outlined
   (its own box) and the chalk's line adds its kind, room and id, for notes; the chalk's clicks are unchanged.
3. **Placing** (right-click, server-checked: reach, the block in hand, every place free, nothing in the way, nobody
   there; survival takes it from the hand): vanilla's rules for the side it's put from (HorizontalOrientable: the side
   looked toward; HorizontalAttachable: toward the wall, walls only; OmniAttachable: up / down / away from the wall; a
   bed's feet on the place and head a block on; Multiblock cells for the variant; a typed container's or crate's mesh
   turned to face the placer, as vanilla's mesh angle). Storage (vanilla's slot count; opened in the ship's storage
   dialog; saved in `magelanInv`), beds (a bunk: sleep; spawn stays the plate's, vanilla beds don't set spawn either),
   chairs/stools/benches (sat on as a thwart), lights (the block's own light), the rest drawn; solid by their collision
   boxes. Pieces are saved in WatchedAttributes `magelanPieces`.
4. **Taking up**: left mouse held 0.6 s on a piece (its hit sound and a puff of its texture every 0.25 s, a percentage
   over the hotbar); refused for a container with something in it, a bed or seat in use, a full inventory. Before the
   guns' click (it doesn't take the click at a gun's seat or while laying one); a left-click on her otherwise stays as it
   was (nothing).
5. **Paint**: a zone is painted with dye in a liquid container (0.02 L/m², rounded up; creative free), gilded with gold
   plates (mouldings and carvings, 1 plate per 10 m², at least 1), scraped back with a knife (free, a point of wear);
   WatchedAttributes `magelanPaint`. Drawn: the zone's quads and voxel faces on `magelan:block/paint/oak<n>`/`timber`
   (vanilla's oak in grey, mean 210) `++3~magelan:block/dye/<colour>` (the dye texture's mean), the deck's caulk kept;
   gilt `game:block/metal/plate/gold`. The design's meshes are split once by zone; a ship draws her painted zones from
   her own meshes; hull chunks with paint are built for her like damaged ones, damage over the paint.
   The crosshair finds a zone on the first surface of hers the view ray meets that faces it (every other quad and
   voxel face of hers is kept with no zone, so the deck hides the beams under it). With the generator's data the quads'
   areas match `docs/views/paint/zones.json`; its voxel faces don't yet (paintFaces lacks some faces paint_zones counts,
   e.g. the great cabin front's lowest boards: great-cabin-front 17.1 m² against 24.5): reported for the generator.
6. **The built-in furniture**: until the generator removes it, the 0.3.7 chest, cargo and bed places still work (they're
   kept clear of pieces). The lantern places are hooks now: an older ship's lanterns become pieces on them at load; what
   stood on a chest, cargo or bed place the design no longer has becomes a piece on the nearest free place within 4
   blocks of where it stood (0.3.7's places kept in `LegacyPlaces`), a container with what was in it (what doesn't fit
   or can't be placed goes into the hold as before). A new ship gets the design's `startPieces`.
7. **Multiplayer**: the server decides (`DecorPacket`: place, take up, use, paint); pieces, their storages and the paint
   are synced to every client. No access rights yet (later: who may change her).
8. **Wrecks**: a container piece goes into her wreck with what's in it, where it stood (`WreckLoot.Place`,
   `WreckInput.ContainerPlaces`); other pieces with her loose things.

**The data the generator writes** (agreed 2026-10-02): in the definition `paintZones` [{code, name, kind, default}],
`hooks` [{code (the old lantern places' codes), pos, hang, kinds, shown}], `startPieces` [{block, mount "h:<hook>",
yaw?, attributes?}], `stairs` [{code, foot, head, width}], `keepClear` [{what: "window"…, box}]; in the mesh file
`paintQuads` {group: [start, end, zone, …]} and `paintFaces` {"exterior"|"interior": {element: {face: zone}}}. Until it
lands: no paint zones in the game (the offline tests use test zones on her real meshes), no start pieces, windows not
kept clear of wall spots, stairs from the grid only.

**Commands** (creative or admins): `/magelan pieces [list|clear]`, `/magelan place <block> <mount id>
[bow|starboard|stern|port]`, `/magelan paint <zone|all> <colour|gold|none>`, `/magelan zones`, `/magelan mounts [ids
[filter]]`.

**Tests:** offline `-- decor` (and in the full run): 27 checks (mount points, placing, saving, the 0.3.7 places, paint
zones on her real meshes, the crosshair on them). The self-test (not run here): `Decorating` covers it end to end on the
server's paths (a chest piece's storage in and out, refused full, a trunk, vessel and crate in the hold, a bed someone
lies in, her start lanterns lit, taken down and hung again, a zone painted from a bucket of dye at its cost, scraped with a
knife, a carving gilded); `Fittings` runs the built-in places' checks only for a design that still has them; the sinking
voyage's chest and the wreck's cargo are pieces in the hold.

**Choices for the user to confirm:** mount points show only with a block in hand; rows of wall spots 0.75 and 1.75
over the floor; taking up takes 0.6 s; dye 0.02 L/m², gold one plate per 10 m²; a knife wears a point a scrape; chairs
sit you as on a thwart; vanilla beds don't set spawn (the plate does).

## The ninth client test (2026-10-03, Magelan 0.3.8): chalk notes 55–69, and the hull on one block grid (→ 0.3.9)

The user walked carrack #838 "Gremlins Emīls" (0.3.8) with the chalk (notes 55–69, screenshots in `Pictures/
Vintagestory`). Then a long design discussion about fitting vanilla furniture: the hull is redesigned on one block grid.
**Decided (2026-10-03): hull R, 2 blocks longer.** References: `docs/views/section-fit.png` (the first agreed section),
`docs/views/grid/` (rib spacing study), `docs/views/hull/` (the proposal: `hull-proposal.md` sections 1–8, `user-*.png`
for R; the study scripts are in the 2026-10-03 session's scratchpad `hullstudy/`, `gridstudy/`, `section_fit.py`).

### The grid and the hull (decided)

- **One block grid for the whole ship, aligned between all decks.** x: block borders at integers. z: the centreline in a
  block's middle (borders at ±0.5, ±1.5 …) on every deck. y (grid G): floors on block borders. No per-deck offsets: the
  mod's per-deck floor alignment (`MountLayout.Align`, best of 16) becomes one fixed ship grid.
- **Waterline vs the world:** vanilla still water's surface is at block Y + 0.875 and her waterline (local y 0) floats
  there, so the grid puts the waterline at G −0.125 (game local y = G + 0.125): in calm water every floor is on a world
  block border (level with a pier). In the generator: WATER 1.5 → 1.375 (as the proposal worked out); the collision
  grid's origin so every floor is on a 0.25 line (proposal: OY −4.875).
- **Storeys of 3:** 2 blocks of room + a 1-block deck-structure band under the deck above. The band is a reservation,
  not "deep beams": planks 0.25 + beam ~0.5, the rest open between beams (lanterns hang in the bays, never below
  floor + 2). In the hold the beams run the band's full depth with carlings (a lower, cramped look); knees down the side.
  Floors G: keel bottom −5 (draft 4.875, minimum water ~5), hold −4, lower deck −1, main 2, quarterdeck 5, poop 8.
  Bottom band G −5..−4: keel 0.35 + floors + bottom planking; the keelson flush with the hold floor.
- **Beams, riders, frames every 2 blocks on even x**, on block borders. The visible "ribs" are riders: hanging knees in
  the band at each beam end. The frames move into the hull's thickness (between the outer planking and the inside
  planking, the ceiling), in line with the beams, seen only where damage breaks the hull open (later, a damage-skin
  change). The ceiling is the wall: its lower 2 blocks smooth (leaning), the riders carried down it only as texture
  inlays. The great cabin and the poop cabin panelled flush (stiles and rails, Victory style; the inlays as stiles);
  the forecastle, lower deck and hold plain planking.
- **Hull R** (one smooth spline section, `hull-proposal.md` §8, `user-section.png`): widest low (6.08 at G 0.2, the lower
  deck), a strong round tumblehome (ceiling 5.49 at the main deck floor, 4.48 at G 4, 3.50 at G 7, 3.0 at G 10), the full
  flat floor 5.4 wide, the hold 7 across. Across: hold 7 (±3.5), lower deck 11 (±5.5), main deck 11 at the floor with
  2-high furniture 9 across (the outer column is a 1.0 wedge: accepted, the guns run out under it; players may fill it
  with panelling later), poop cabin 7 (±3.5). A 2-high block touches the wall at its top where the side leans in, at
  its bottom where it flares (gaps ≤ 0.05 midships). The bow, stern and plan lines are A's scaled across.
- **2 blocks longer, in the waist** (length ÷ beam 2.7 → ~2.9): one more beam bay, so the entry gates get their own bay
  (in R they shared bay 10..12 with the forecastle stair, a 0.9 way in) and the 5 guns a side stay.
- **Shell thickness:** thickest at the bottom (1.0 at the centreline with the keel, 0.65 under the flat floor, 0.47 at
  the turn of the bilge, 0.40 at the lower deck, 0.35 at the waterline), 0.3 from the main deck up (constant, so the
  castles join cleanly). The wales follow the planking's strakes (they curved differently from the planks' texture).
- **Roll:** `Motion.RollPeriod` ~6.3 s for R (now 7.5); check in the client.
- **Items on the grid** (proposal table, re-placed for R and the longer waist): each mast inside one cell (main −0.125 →
  cell centre, fore → 15.5 + the stretch, mizzen −12.5); the capstan in one cell (drumhead r ≤ 0.48; R's width lets it
  go back to port, z −1.5..−0.5, the cable's side, clear of the run-back gun); the pump in one cell (it may sit
  off-centre in it); the wheel 1–2 cells (wheel + binnacle); the hearth, its chimney and the vanilla oven on whole
  cells; hatches between beams on whole cells; stairs, ladders, the bitts, the heads, doors on cells; guns one per
  2-block frame bay with their ports between the frames; the cabin fronts with their inside faces on borders (poop front
  −9).
- **Stacking:** a placed full-block piece's top is a floor place when there's room over it (a cabinet on a cabinet).
- **Walls (the user, 2026-10-03):** no tilted wall spots on the leaning hull ("we don't need fixing points at weird
  angles"). Instead **panel attachments**: the player places a vanilla planks block into the wedge column by the wall
  and it becomes a panelled wall (a vertical face on the grid line, the wedge filled behind it, that wood's texture,
  1 block high per block; taken down, the block comes back); its face is then an ordinary vertical wall for wall pieces and
  for furniture to stand flush against (bulkheads count as vertical walls too).

### The chalk notes (to build in 0.3.9)

55. **"I suppose doors are missing here."** and the user later: the companionway in the great cabin has no way in
    from the waist. Add a door in the great cabin's front right in front of the companionway (port); keep the centre
    door (it may become the only one later).
56. **"Stairs down should be 1 block from the wall"**: the companion stair 1 block in from the side (on R's grid).
57. **"All lanterns on spawn are missing textures… All lanterns inside should be on ship's main axis"**: the start
    pieces are `game:lantern-small-up` with no material (the "lantern-small-up-" code); hooks inside on the centreline.
59. **"At every lantern position on ship spawn use small lantern bronze vanilla"**: every start lantern a small bronze
    lantern (material, lining, glass attributes set).
58. **"…ribs shall be spaced exactly 1 block on block borders or middle…"**: the grid above.
60. **"pumps water stream is feeble when one is pumping"**: a full stream for one pumper.
61. **"place drain holes next to ribs"**: the scuppers beside the frames (riders), not mid-bay.
62. **"port gate upper sideboard part extends too far and overlaps with closed gate"**: the rail cap stops at the post
    (z-fighting with the shut gate).
63. **"open gate side shows empty and lacks side surface. Same for sideboards"**: the gate's edges and the bulwark's cut
    ends at the opening get their faces.
64. **"capstan needs mirror on other side of the rope together with rope bundle below"**: the cable/messenger leaves the
    drum on the other side too, to a coil on the deck.
65. **"shit boxes are too forward, player need to be able to sit on them as chair"**: the heads further aft, seatable.
66. **"Remove mermaid and its supports"**: no figurehead (keep a place on the forecastle for a vanilla one later).
67. **"These two protruding side boards could be removed"** → the user later: the wales are real but curve differently
    from the planks' texture: make them follow the strakes.
68. **"[name board] should be able to select background and font colour and font size bigger. Italic style"**.
69. **"outside paint zones are bit inconsistent"** → the user: the red (stripe) zones jump between the main deck and the
    castles: make the bands continuous along the side, sweeping up with the rail into the castles.

### Built (C#) for 0.3.9 (not seen in the client)

- **The ship's block grid** (`ShipDefinition.BlockGrid`, JSON `"grid": {"origin": [x0, y0, z0]}`): with it every floor place
  is on that one grid on every deck (`MountLayout.OnShipGrid`; no search of 16 alignments); without it (the rowing boat,
  older designs) as before. Offline the old hull with a test origin [0, 0.125, 0.5] gives 386 floor places, every one
  and every wall spot on the grid; a check that every deck's floor is on y0 + whole blocks runs once the design has it.
- **Walls on the grid (the user's change):** no tilted wall spots: on the grid a wall spot is only where the wall stands
  upright (lean and turn under 0.05: the bulkheads, the transom's inside where upright), set square, in rows on the
  blocks over the floor (0 and 1; the 0.75 / 1.75 rows stay for designs without a grid, whose saved pieces keep
  working). **Panel places** (`MountKind.Panel`, ids `p:deck:i:k:<e|s|w|n>:<row>`): at each enclosed room's outermost
  floor cell, where beyond its border there's a wedge (within a block part free, part her side; all free is open room,
  none free is already flush), not where anything's kept clear (doorways, stairs, gun rooms, windows), row 0 on the floor
  and row 1 over it (to the band). A **planks block** (`planks-*`, vanilla's wallpapered planks too) on one makes a panel
  (`PieceRole.Panel`): its face on the grid line facing into the room, the wedge behind it filled (solid), drawn as the
  block's own cube squeezed into the wedge (its texture); one block per row (stack a second for 2 high); taken down
  (held left mouse) the block comes back. Each panel place has a wall spot on its face (`w:room:<side><line>:i:row`,
  e.g. `w:lowerdeck:s5.5:-10:0`) open only while its panel is up; a panel isn't taken down while something hangs on it
  or a panel stands on it. With the old hull and the test origin: 120 panel places (lower deck 66, great cabin 27, poop
  cabin 18, forecastle 9), 60 upright wall spots. Panels show only with planks in hand.
- **What hangs on a wall:** paintings (HorizontalAttachable) as before; **tapestries** placed like paintings and drawn
  with their picture (`BlockTapestry.genMesh` by the stack's `type`; not seen); **wallpaper** (vanilla's `wallpaper-*`,
  the "Decor" behaviour, a surface layer: vanilla puts it on a world block's face, which pieces aren't) is a wall piece
  here: its texture as a flat layer on the spot's face, not solid, walls only. Vanilla's own decor-on-block mechanic
  doesn't reach our pieces (they aren't world blocks).
- **Saved ships whose places are gone** (the new hull; the tilted spots on the grid): at load a piece whose places the
  design no longer has goes onto the nearest free floor place within 4 blocks (a block up or down, facing as it did if
  it fits) with what was in it; else it and its contents go into the hold (onto her deck if that's full), logged.
- **Stacking:** over each floor place, places a block up and two (`f:deck:i:k:1`, `:2`) where she has the room; open
  while a piece with a top to stand on (solid at least 0.75 up and 0.7 across: a chest, a crate, a cabinet) stands
  under it, and what's put there stands a whole block up, as vanilla stacks blocks. A piece isn't taken from under
  another ("Something stands or hangs on it"). Holding a block on top of a chest (a piece that opens), Shift puts it
  there (as vanilla places on a chest), else right-click opens it (the hint says so); on a piece that doesn't open the
  place on top shows at once. `/magelan pieces clear` takes the highest first.
- **Note 60:** one man at the pump gives a full stream (95 particles a second; more men up to 130; less as her hold runs
  dry); the gushes never drop under 0.45 of it.
- **Note 68:** the name dialog chooses the letters' colour (gilt or one of vanilla's dyes), the ground (the board as built
  or a colour), size (small, medium, large: of the board's height) and italic; saved with her name (`magelanFit`:
  `nameText`, `nameBack`, `nameSize`, `nameItalic`), synced to every client, kept when a boat is picked up and put out.
  The board's picture is drawn whole (ground and letters, the letters as big as the board allows: the old texture's
  line spacing made them small), italic by slanting (the font may have no italic).
- **The heads** (fixture type `seat`, codes `heads-port` / `heads-starboard`): right-click sits you (seat `sit:<code>`,
  the chair pieces' seat); Shift, or a step (a movement key or jump, after 0.6 s), gets you up beside it. The chair
  pieces get up with a step too now.
- **Lanterns** (note 57): a lantern piece whose stack lacks its material, lining or glass (0.3.8's start lanterns), or
  has one vanilla has no texture for, gets vanilla's defaults (copper, plain, quartz; "bronze" is tin bronze) when it's
  put and when a ship loads, so it never shows untextured. The design's start lanterns should still carry their own
  (the generator: small bronze, notes 57/59).
- **The door to the companionway** (`door-companion-cabin`, type door, unseals the lower deck): doors work by type (its
  way, the room it opens, its downflooding point, kept clear): nothing to add.
- **Roll period:** nothing else assumes 7.5 (`ShipMotion`'s default, `ShipDamage` reads the design's).
- Tests: offline `-- decor` adds stacking, the grid, panels (a planks block, a painting on it, wallpaper only on a wall),
  lantern defaults, the name style, the design's seats and doors; the pump test the full stream. The self-test adds a
  chest on a chest and a panel with a painting on it (when the design has the grid).
- Lang keys added to `lang/en.json` (piece-no-support, piece-under, panel-needs-planks, piece-panel, mount-kind-panel,
  hint-piece-stack, hint-seat, fixture-heads-*, fixturekind-seat, name-*).
- **Against hull R (the generator's data of 2026-10-03):** the heads' `pos` is the seat's top (as a chair piece's seat:
  where the sitter is), their `feet` is where getting up puts you (`FixtureDef.Feet`, `StandUp`); the companion door's
  +90° about its inboard hinge swings aft into the cabin like the centre door (doors swing by the data; open, the doorway
  isn't solid; it unseals the lower deck by type); the gates swing by their moving parts' angle and a gate's keep-clear now
  reaches in as far as its leaf is long (it hangs on its after edge); saved paint, open fittings, gun and damage states
  are by code, so the figurehead zone's going shifts nothing. A stair's keep-clear no longer reaches through the bulkhead
  under its head into the room below. The square sails are braced at least so the wind meets them from aft (5° to
  spare), as far as the yards go: with `maxBrace` 36 they can't fill closer than 54° apparent. `GroundShare` 0.06 → 0.05
  (hull R is heavier and her panels 2 long: 2.5 b/s holed her instead of cracking her). A hole's reveal and the
  ceiling's panel go as deep as her shell is there (hull R's is thicker low down; the mesh file's one `hullThickness`
  0.3 at least). Tests read decks, the hold's floor, frames, stairs from the design (no figurehead, the keelson flush).
  Hull R: 521 floor places, 559 stacked, 99 panel places (hold 30, lower deck 22, great cabin 40, poop cabin 5,
  forecastle 2) with their 99 wall spots, 83 upright wall spots (lower deck 72, poop cabin 6, forecastle 3, great cabin
  2: its front's windows and doors leave little), 14 hooks. Self-test ALL OK (219).
  Still failing offline (left for a look): the dry outline round her stern stands 0.56 off her quarters (limit 0.45: 32
  stations along the longer hull); a test hole's ceiling cut (0.24 of 0.44 m²) and note 32's panel (1.17 m² cut out of
  the tarred planking for a 0.65 hole; the ceiling 0.36 of 0.55); the tumbling shot's far side only Scraped (0.22: the
  ball crosses more of her inside on the way).

`tools/shipgen/carrack.py` VARIANT 3 (`GRID`), with small changes to planking.py, shipgen.py, paint_zones.py and view.py.
Views: `docs/views/carrack-grid-*.png` (`python tools/shipgen/view.py grid`), the paint zones in `docs/views/paint/zones-*.png`.

   clear of the forecastle door, 0.94 of the open port gate, 1.65 of gun 5); pump (4.5, 0); waist and hold hatch 6..8 x
   ±1.5 (opening 6.25..7.75 x ±1.25), the hold ladder in it; wheel cell -5..-4, binnacle -4..-3; hearth 15..17 x
   1.5..3.5, stovepipe (16.5, 3.0), oven 17..18 x 1.5..2.5; riding bitts 19.5 x ±1; heads 22.5 x ±1; guns at x 1, 3, 5,

## The tenth client test (2026-10-03, Magelan 0.3.9): chalk notes 70–106 (→ 0.3.10)

The user walked carrack #1379 (hull R, 0.3.9). Notes in `ModData/magelan-notes.md` 70–106, screenshots in
`Pictures/Vintagestory` (2026-10-03_15-06 … 16-17). Positions are game ship-local (x forward, y up from the waterline,
z starboard). The user approved (2026-10-03): fix parts A and B now; part C (the waist) as a plan drawing first,
**all stairs on the starboard side**.

### A. Bugs
- 70, 74, 83, 84 "weird textures": panels, rider inlays, band pieces show garbage from other atlas regions — new
  texture codes are apparently not in the ship's texture atlas (or the uv region is wrong).
- 71 "Door blocked by texture": door-companion-cabin's opening is still filled by the cabin front's wall/panelling above.
- 72 can't stack on a cabinet against the wall unless a panel is there (the stacked cell's check vs the leaning wall is
  too strict at the touching edge).
- 75 a hole in the hold at the bow (ship-local 15.75, −3.78, −0.40), seen from inside.
- 77 the hold ladder (cargo deck, ~7.4, −3.9, 0): sometimes reverses the walking direction, misses steps.
- 78 placed (vanilla) doors as pieces don't open; rooms can't be sealed (see B partitions).
- 80 no panel places in the hold's wedges where the side flares out going up (wedge at the top).
- 81 piece lanterns don't glow.
- 91 sitting on the heads, the player's legs are inside the box (3rd person).
- 100 a placed scribe table (with table cloth) is invisible but solid.
- 101 next to a mast, a table can't be placed: the mast's keep-clear spills into the neighbouring cells; it must be the
  mast's own cell only.
- 86 the pin rail in the waist is too long and interferes with the guns: only in bays without a gun.
- (the user, in chat) placed a forge and bellows: they place but don't work. Pieces don't run vanilla block-entity
  mechanics (only the ported hearth/oven work): survey which functional blocks work aboard; make the forge + bellows
  (and the anvil) work like the hearth if doable; tell the player for the rest.

- (the user, in chat) **clean surfaces**: parts drawn from centre lines overlap at their corners where thick parts meet,
  and the coplanar overlaps flicker in the game (z-fighting); there must be no overlaps and no holes. A mesh check
  (`tools/shipgen/meshcheck.py`: coplanar overlaps, open edges/cracks, T-junctions, light leaks from rooms) runs after
  every generation; the generator fixes what it finds. Only **visible** surfaces matter (the user): overlaps or holes
  inside solid material or never seen from where a player can be are fine.

- (the user, playing 0.3.9 with their Sea Monsters mod: a kraken holed her) shredded sails don't change the roll (nor,
  presumably, the speed or heel): damaged sails must lose drive and roll damping; repairing from outside conflicts with
  boarding from the water (a repair item in hand must repair, not board), and hull panels should be repairable from
  inside where reachable; the damage looks inside (ceiling, panels) are much fainter than outside. The user: floors
  and placed furniture block inside access → with a repair item in hand the repair crosshair looks through furniture,
  partitions, panels, the ceiling and the deck structure/hold floor (limber boards) to the damaged panel behind
  (≤ ~1.5 blocks), outlined with its state; outside repair with an item in hand never boards. From outside, nothing
  inside (chests) may be used through the hull. The "board by ladder" message hijacked every right-click on the hull
  from the water (ShipClientSystem ~line 1005): only empty-handed, rarely, as a tip; ladder boarding only at the ladder.
  Pumps: 200 L/s a person (PumpRate 0.2 t a stroke), the intake at the bilge's lowest point, so a steady full stream
  while water is aboard regardless of sloshing; intakes along the whole keel. A closed hatch only keeps seas from
  getting below; it doesn't trap air (kept realistic, the user). Stranded with all sails shredded: windage drift under
  bare poles (config `Windage`), shredded sails repairable with 8 cloth too. Currents: a later Sea feature.
  Helm/UI (the user): steering inverted (right turns left); no wind indicator aboard or on the map (HUD panel, masthead
  pennants, a map wind arrow); the map's player arrow 180° off, also on land: a Wmap bug (Wmap 0.3.4); a ships layer on
  the map (own ships anywhere, loaded ships near), as a vanilla MapLayer that shows in Wmap too.

### B. Structure (agreed)
- 73, 76: no horizontal clamp under the beams; the knees (riders) in the beam's own plane, as its continuation.
- 79, 104: the keel/keelson shows in the hold at the bow and looks broken; hide it in the hold (only where it hides a
  seam); outside the keel is not continuous and has a sharp corner at the forefoot: one continuous keel curving into
  the stem.
- 106: bottom (keel + floors + planking) ~0.5 thick so the keel bottom is at G −4.5 (draft ~4.375): 0.6 under her in
  5 blocks of water (heave 0.2–0.4 at force 5–6, pitch adds ~0.5 at the ends).
- 103: the paint stripes continue across the transom.
- 93: the waist bulwark's stanchions (frame heads) stay, every 2 blocks in line with the beams, between the guns.
- 105: the rudder's rake is right (hung on the raked sternpost); check its size (keel to above the waterline).
- 99 (answered): the capstan's spindle foot on the lower deck.
- 78, 98: rooms: thin **partition** pieces on grid borders (like panels, from planks), doors in their openings,
  partitions + doors + hull + decks count as a closed room for the mod; they stop at the deck band.

### C. The waist (plan drawing first, not built until the user confirms)
- 87: mainmast to ~x 2.5 (it's 3.4 aft of the waterline's middle now, the stretch went in forward of it).
- 94 pump by the mainmast, handle away from it; 95 gates to the middle of the waist, swapped with a gun; 96 gallows 1–2
  frames forward; 97 glazed and cargo hatches 3 long, centred between the gallows posts and the gates.
- 85, 88, 90 capstan and anchors to the sides (two bower anchors, stowed against the side with the shank up, catheads,

### Textures (generator side), notes 70, 74, 83, 84

- Every texture code the carrack's meshes use (`config/shipmeshes/carrack.json` "textures", 37 codes) and her voxel
  shapes (`shapes/entity/ship/carrack/{exterior,interior}.json` "textures") is vanilla (`game:block/wood/planks/oak1..4`,
  `debarked/oak`, `debarked/pine`, metal plates and sheets, glass, brick, linen, reedrope, black, paper) or a composite
  of one with our `magelan:block/tar` / `magelan:block/caulk` (both in `textures/block/`). Nothing new in 0.3.9, nothing
  missing. The mesh quads' uv are all within 0..16.
- **The bug was the voxel shapes' uv** (the generator's): each face's uv is its place in the whole block it's in, and
  since 0.3.9 the design is moved down by 1.375 (`translate_y`, her waterline on the grid) after it's built, so the
  game's block borders fall across the cells (y = …, -4.125..-3.875 straddles -4): 776 of 1636 exterior faces and 268 of
  3494 interior ones had uv running to -2 … 18 (or -4..8 on merged boxes), past their texture into the atlas's
  neighbours. Exactly the decks' edges and undersides, the cabin fronts' boards, the bulkheads — "Structure … band n"
  in the notes. Fixed in `shipgen.py`: `_uv_aabb` and `mesh_voxels`' merging use the design's own blocks (before the
  move, `Design.ty`); now 0 faces out of 0..16. (The rowing boat's shapes still have 6 such faces: not rebuilt.)
- So the C# side's atlas loading may well be fine; if garbage still shows on mesh pieces (riders' inlays, panels) after
  this, it isn't the uv or a missing file.
