### Sails (0.3.10, C#; offline checks, not seen in the client)

The user sailed #1379 "Lisbon" → "Azores" (logs: `/sea setwind N 1.5`, i.e. Sea's most, force 12, a hurricane):
1. **"Sails get damaged very fast"** (10–20 % a passage): the old wear was 0.3 an hour × (wind − 0.5)², 30 %/h at full
   sail in force 12 and already 0.5 %/h in a strong breeze; every `/time set` also counted an hour. Now (`SailWear`,
   `DamageDef.SailChafe*`, `SailGale*`, `SailLoadExponent`, `SailAback*`): chafe 0.2 %/day under full sail in a working
   wind; gale tearing from a load of set² × (apparent wind / 0.69)² over 1 (full sail from force 8, reefed from force
   11), 2.2 %/h × (load − 1)²; the square sails taken aback (apparent wind inside `NoGo`) tear from set² × (wind/0.6)²
   over 1. Each sail at its own rate (0.7–1.3). Calendar jumps over a quarter hour are left out (sleeping still counts).
   A game day (% of a sail; torn at 30): force 3–7 full 0.1–0.2, reefed ≤ 0.11; force 8 full 2.6; force 9 full 28;
   force 10 full 98 (torn in ~7 h); force 11 full 244; force 12 full 733 (31 %/h: torn in an hour), reefed 10
   (0.4 %/h); furled 0. Aback an hour, full sail: force 7 0.1, 8 7, 9 33, 10 90, 12 551; reefed ≤ 16. So in Sea's
   1.5 she has to be reefed (half sail) or her sails go in an hour.
2. **"The mizzen sails I cannot repair", "cannot fix the main sails either":** sails couldn't be looked at at all (the
   crosshair met only the masts: you had to look at the mast, which nothing said), and the lateen's damage box was a
   square sail's box round its yard's lower end, mostly forward of the mizzen and under the poop. Now each sail has a
   frame and outline from its yard and clews (`SailFrame`, `DamageLayout.SailFrames`; the lateen a triangle aft of the
   mizzen), and the crosshair meets a sail as it's drawn (braced or sheeted, furled up on its yard;
   `DamageLayout.SailAt`, `EntityShip.SailRay`, nearer than what's behind it, only with damage on): its name, state and
   what mends it, mended there (or at its mast, as before).
3. **"Says sails but actually needs cloth":** the hint named only the first kit ("1 sails"), and `game:sail` is the
   windmill's sail, not what a player calls a sail. Now: torn, 1 sail (`game:sail` or a boat's `game:sail-large-*`) or 4
   cloth (`game:cloth-*` any colours, or `game:linen-*`, which does exist: the decor block); shredded, 2 sails or 1 sail
   + 4 cloth. The hint and "you need" list every way ("1 sail, or 4 cloth or linen"); what's in hand is used first.
4. **"The mizzen sail looks to be the wrong direction to the wind":** it swung to leeward right, but its mesh is bellied
   to starboard always, into the wind on the starboard tack. The renderer mirrors it through its own plane by the
   sheet (`PartMotion.LateenBelly`/`BellyMatrix`; flat for a moment as it comes over), so it bellies to leeward on both
   tacks. Sheeted out at most 25° (the shrouds stop it at 26°).
5. **Looking through things:** decorating's piece target (DecoClient) wasn't stopped by her decks, sides or masts ("a
   reading of items below deck or through the mast"); now it is. The ship's own ray already took the nearest of cells,
   masts, fixtures and pieces.
6. **Server config (the user):** `SailWear`, `SailDamage`, `SeamWear`, `Fouling`, `HullDamage`, `CannonDamage` (factors,
   1, 0 = off), on top of `Wear` (all time wear) and `GroundingDamage` (blows from grounding). `HullDamage` is every blow
   to the hull, decks, rudder, masts and rigging; `SailDamage` gales, aback and shot on sails; `CannonDamage` shot on
   top. Missing keys get their defaults and are written back on load.
Offline: `dotnet run -c Release -- sails` (the wear table, looking at and mending each sail, the kits and that their
items exist, a deck over the hold, a mast before what's behind it, the lateen's belly on both tacks).

### Built (C#) for 0.3.10 (not seen in the client)

- **70, 74, 83, 84 (garbage textures):** the cause was the shapes' uv (the generator's fix above); the mod's atlas
  loading was fine (every code found). Safety net (`src/Client/ShapeUvs.cs`): before a ship's shape is tesselated (hull,
  paint zones, torn sails) every face's uv is brought inside its texture (moved by whole textures, else as little as
  fits; longer than the texture: squashed), the mesh file's quads likewise; a warning names the shape and the count when
  that happens, and every texture code a face or quad uses that isn't defined or whose image isn't in the assets
  ("base++n~overlay": each part) is logged ("drawn as unknown"). The regenerated data: 0 faces moved.
- **72:** a piece on a floor place is checked against her collision only within its places' blocks (the layout found
  those free): a cabinet's top board overhanging its block no longer clashes with her leaning side when stacked.
- **77 (the hold ladder):** on a ladder the way you walk says up or down: toward it as you'd step onto it from its foot
  is up, away from it down, whichever key; across it, W/S as before; jump/sneak still climb/descend. (Going down the hold
  ladder facing forward, W meant "up" once the first key was let go: the reversal.) "Missed steps" not found in the code
  (the climb is continuous along the ladder's line).
- **80:** panel places where her side flares out going up: the wedge may run up to 2.5 blocks out (`MaxPanelDepth`) when
  the panel's foot meets her side within a block (row 0), and over a panel (row 1). Panels: 182 (hold 72, lower deck 62,
  great cabin 41, forecastle 2, poop cabin 5; 0.3.9: 99).
- **81 (lanterns don't glow):** the game draws only its first "max dynamic lights" (graphics setting, 10 by default)
  point lights in the order added, after the lit entities near the player; the ship added all her design lights,
  stoves (dark ones too) and hooks first and the pieces' lanterns last, so they were never drawn. Now only her lit
  lights nearest the player (a deck between counts double) are given to the game, as many as it has room for after the
  lit entities, chosen again every frame; every piece whose block gives light (lanterns, candles, torch holders) lights.
  **Limits:** at most "max dynamic lights" lights at once round the player (raise the setting for more); point lights
  aren't shadowed (they light through a deck a little); other mods' point lights added before ours come first.
- **91 / chairs:** sitting on the heads or a chair is vanilla's sitting on a block's edge (`sitflooredge`, animation
  `sitidle`: legs over the front), not the boat pose (legs out along the bottom, into the box); the heads at the
  generator's new `pos` (the seat's front edge); a chair's sitter 0.25 forward of its middle (chairs put from now on).
- **100 (scribe table invisible):** vanilla's clutter blocks (`BlockShapeFromAttributes`: the scribe tables with cloth,
  clutter of every kind) hand out their cached mesh itself; the piece renderer turned and moved it in place, wrecking it
  for every later draw. The mesh is copied first (the displays' item meshes too); a mesh source that throws is logged.
- **101:** on the ship's grid a mast keeps only its own cell(s) clear (0.3.9: its radius + 0.35 round it, into the
  neighbours). The offline check now checks the mast's own circle.
- **78, 98, B (rooms: partitions and doors):**
  - **Border places** (`MountKind.Border`, ids `b:deck:i:k:<e|s|w|n>:<row>`): every closed room's floor cell sides,
    rows 0 and 1 (to the band at floor + 2), once per border between two floor cells (from the west / north cell), by a
    non-floor cell (a stair, a hatch surround, a fixture) the cell's own side; not where a panel place is, not where the
    cell's layer along it isn't free of her, not into a doorway's keep-clear. 1996 on the carrack (hold 476, lower deck
    1028, great cabin 318, poop cabin 144, forecastle 30). Shown with planks or a door in hand (and the chalk).
  - **Partition** (`PieceRole.Partition`): a planks block on a border place, a block high, drawn 1/8 thick on the grid
    line (the planks' own texture), picked as a sheet on the line (so a chest or cabinet stands right up to it on either
    side), solid as the 0.25 voxel layer on the anchor cell's side; row 1 needs a partition under it; not taken down
    from under one.
  - **Doors** (`PieceRole.Door`): a vanilla door (the "Door" behaviour, 1 wide, at most 2 high) only on a row-0 border
    place with its row 1 free: it takes both, hung on the border's lower end, drawn from its block's mesh fitted onto the
    line; right-click opens/shuts it (its block's sounds; not shut on someone in the doorway); open it isn't solid and is
    drawn swung a quarter into the anchor cell; the state is saved (`open`). A door aimed at a floor place: "a door goes
    on a cell's side". Doors put as pieces in 0.3.9 (on floor places) stay decorations: take them up and put them again.
  - Placing checks against her collision **without** partitions and shut doors (`EntityShip.PlaceGrid`), so pieces go
    right next to them.
  - **What "sealed" means now** (`EntityShip.SealedAt`, used for shelter: rain, body temperature, stove and hearth
    heat): a design room whose unsealing fittings are shut, as before; and, inside a design room that's open (the hatch
    or door that unseals it open), the space round the player closed by her sides, decks and bulkheads, shut design
    doors, partitions and shut door pieces, from the floor up 2 blocks (the band above taken as closed), worked on her
    collision voxels (`ShipCompartments.Sealed`): open if it reaches a block beyond the room's bounds (a doorway out) or
    under an open unsealing fitting (the hatch's opening). A partition line ending at her side's wedge (no panel there)
    is carried through the wedge to her side. The hold: no fitting unseals it in the design, so it was and is always a
    closed room (the user's note 78 attempt was there). Cached per block until her pieces or fittings change. Open decks
    (no room) are never sealed.
- **Workstations aboard (the user: "bellows and forge ... did not work"):** pieces aren't world blocks, so vanilla's
  block entities don't run for them (the hearth and the oven work because their workings were ported). A block with
  workings of its own placed as a piece now says so: the place hint and the piece's hint add "works only ashore for
  now", a right-click on it says "<block> works only ashore for now: aboard it's only standing there". Survey:

  | Block | Works aboard? | What's needed |
  |---|---|---|
  | Chests, trunks, labeled chests, storage vessels, crates, other containers with slots | yes (storage) | - |
  | Beds | yes (sleep) | - |
  | Chairs, stools, benches; the heads | yes (sit) | - |
  | Lanterns, candles, torch holders | yes (light, the nearest N) | see 81's limits |
  | Doors (1 x 2) | yes (new: on cell borders) | wider gates: not yet |
  | Paintings, tapestries, wallpaper | yes (on walls and panels) | - |
  | Scribe tables, tables with cloth, clutter | yes (decoration) | - |
  | Forge + bellows | no (says so) | port BlockEntityForge (fuel, heating the item on it, the bellows' blowing) as HearthFire was; of little use without the anvil |
  | Anvil | no (says so) | smithing is vanilla's voxel work item on a world block (per-voxel selection, the recipe dialog, hammer hits): not portable to an entity piece; needs a real block (a minidimension) |
  | Quern | no (says so) | port the grinding (an inventory and a timer, hand-turned) as the oven was: feasible |
  | Barrel (sealing, mixing) | no (says so) | port its liquid and item slots and the sealed-recipe timer: feasible, medium |
  | Crock | no (says so) | sealing meals: a small port |
  | Firepit, clay oven, pit kiln as pieces | no (says so) | the design's hearth and oven are the ported ones; a pit kiln: niche |
  | Shelves, tool racks, display cases, scroll racks, mold racks | no (says so) | an inventory and drawing the items on it (as the hearth's pot): medium each |
  | Signs | no text | the sign's text dialog and drawing |

  Not built: the forge port (without the anvil it only heats things that cool on the way ashore).
- Lang keys: piece-partition, partition-needs-planks, door-needs-border, door-needs-floor, door-no-room,
  hint-door-open, hint-door-shut, hint-piece-ashore, piece-ashore-only, mount-kind-border.
- Tests: offline `-- decor` adds border places (on the lines, once, row 1 over row 0), a partition (thin, only planks, a
  chest right up to it), a door (both rows, its hinge, not on a floor place, open saved), and a closed room on the lower
  deck with the waist hatch open between two partition lines (sealed; by the hatch not; one partition away: not). The
  self-test adds a partition and a vanilla door (solid shut, free open, saved open, shut again). Two data-sensitive
  checks loosened (a blow's energy summed in floats: 1e-4; the wreck's plants: 90 % of the columns, the decay draws
  differ with rocks in her way). Still failing offline (as before): the dry outline round her stern, the hole's ceiling
  cut, note 32's panel, the tumbling shot's far side.
  On the generator's 16:58 data (Debug build): **self-test ALL OK (223)**.

### Built (C#) 0.3.10 — play fixes (the user's 0.3.9 play: the kraken, stranded, foundering; not seen in the client)

- **Torn and shredded sails draw less** ("my sails are shredded but that doesn't seem to affect the roll"): a sail's
  cloth that still draws is 1 (down to 0.9 just short of torn), torn `TornArea` 0.6, shredded `ShreddedArea` 0.15
  (DamageDef), times what its mast and rigging leave (`SailArea.Drive`, cached by the damage version). Her speed, her
  heel in the wind and how much her sails steady her roll all go by the sail set times that (`EntityShip.SailsDrawing`),
  on the server and every client alike (from the synced parts). Full sail, force 1 beam wind: heel 10.9° whole, 2.7°
  shredded; roll steadying 1.00 → 0.15.
- **Windage** ("without sails I am stranded"): the wind on her hull, castles and rig (`Sailing.Windage`,
  `SailingDef.Windage` 0.045 of the wind's speed, config `Windage` factor, default 1). Under bare poles (sail drawing
  under 0.05) she drifts downwind: dead downwind 0.045 × the wind (force 7, 15.5 m/s: 0.70 b/s; force 12: about 1.7);
  her headway eases off to none 40° off downwind (`BarePolesSteer`), so the helm steers her up to about ±35° off it once
  she has way on; beam-on she makes leeway (0.8 of it sideways: force 7 0.56 b/s), applied to her position on the server
  and the client's drawn position (synced `leeway`). With sail set it adds its along-heading part (helps running, holds
  her back on the wind) and no leeway (the sails' polar is her drive, her keel holds her). Not at anchor, aground or
  rowing.
- **"Set sails: speed drops to zero; furl: I get speed"** (Beaufort 12 from N, patched sails, foundering): reproduced
  offline (`DriveTests.cs`): with 0.3.9's logic the sails give their full speed (5.5–8.3 b/s) at every heading abaft the
  no-go angle (67°), sound, torn or shredded, dry or nine tenths full of water — set sails never give less than furled.
  They give ~0 (or a little sternway) only heading within 67° of the wind: in irons / taken aback; and in 0.3.9 "furled"
  could only give way as **sweeps (astern)** (S pressed once more: a fixed 0.6 b/s whatever the wind) — almost surely
  what happened (head to wind, then furled → sweeps). Not a bug in the physics, so the helm's HUD now says why her set
  sails give little way: "taken aback (the wind right ahead)", "in irons (too close to the wind)", "her rig is too hurt
  to draw", "heavy with water" (`EntityShip.WhySlow`, synced). Furled she now drifts with the windage (so no stranding).
- **Repairs from outside and inside:**
  - From the water: a right-click aimed at a rope ladder itself (within 1 block of it, standing within 5 of its foot)
    climbs it whatever's in hand (the user: "we should board also with something in hands"); empty-handed within 2
    blocks. A right-click on her plain side with anything in hand is the item's (mending her: held right-click), never
    boarding and no message; mending wins only there. Swimming into a ladder climbs it as before. Empty-handed elsewhere a hint, at most every 20 s, as chat, not an error: "To climb aboard, swim to a rope ladder at the entry
    ports, amidships (port and starboard), and right-click it with an empty hand." (`Boarding.OutsideClick`, tested.) The
    copper-plate cladding click stays.
  - **The repair crosshair** (`ShipRepairs.Pick`, `EntityShip.RepairPick`): holding something that mends a hull or deck
    panel, her ray looks through everything put aboard (pieces, chests, partitions, panels: her design's own collision
    only) and up to 1.5 blocks past the first of her surfaces (her ceiling, a deck's edge or band, the hold's floor over
    her bottom) to the nearest panel that item mends; from outside her only 0.6 (her planking: never through her side to
    what's inside). The first surface must be in reach. The picked panel is outlined (magenta wireframe) and the look-at
    box shows its state and what mends it; held right-click mends it (the server checks reach as before).
  - **Not through her hull** ("trying to open chests placed inside"): from outside her (not aboard) a piece or a place
    behind her design's woodwork isn't targeted (client), and the server refuses a piece's use, taking up or placing, and
    a fixture's use, when no line from the player's eye reaches its box past her hull, decks and bulkheads
    (`ShipPieceSet.Seen`, `EntityShip.SeenBy`).
  - Reach: **every hull panel below her waterline (140 of 140) can be mended from inside** (from a floor place, within
    the picking range, through her ceiling and the hold's floor). None needs diving or a port.
- **Damage inside** ("not visible, at least not as well as outside"): her ceiling quads do carry it (all 348 panels by
  a room have them; 28 of 6141 sit behind the interior voxel shape's elements), but the inside overlays were the outside's
  on a dimly lit ceiling. Inside now has its own stronger ones (`textures/block/dmg-*-in-a|b.png`: cracks 2.2× as opaque
  and darker, the wet stain 1.6×, the hole's splinters 1.6×), and worn seams weep inside (damp); a scrape stays
  outside only. The two failing hole-cut checks are not this: the reveal's depth (40 of 56 quads within 0.15 of the
  shell's thickness at the hole) and note 32's outside cut (0.97 m² cut for a 0.73 hole) — the strake cutting on hull
  R's thicker, curved shell (left for a look).
- **Pumps** (the user: "about 200 l/s", "output still variable"): `PumpRate` 0.08 → **0.2 t a stroke** (200 l/s a man,
  400 at a brake; config Pumps scales it; one man now holds a fresh hole below the waterline, 131 l/s). The intakes
  are along the keel (the user): her water is one body for the pumps, so any water aboard is reached at the full rate
  whatever her heel, trim or sloshing (as it already was: the rate never depended on where the water lies) until less
  than `PumpResidual` 0.05 t is left; then the pumps suck air until 0.15 t has come in again (no flicker). The variation
  was the old rule capping the stream at what came in each tick once the hold was nearly dry, and the 0.45–1 gushes:
  the stream is now steady, swelling 0.8–1 with the brake. Server `PumpedRate` and the client's stream use the same
  synced value.
- **Shredded sails** can be mended with **8 cloth** alone (any colour, or linen) besides 2 sails or a sail and 4 cloth;
  torn stays a sail or 4 cloth. The hint lists the kits.
- Lang: why-aback, why-irons, why-rig, why-water; board-by-ladder reworded.
- Tests: offline: the sails' area (torn 0.60, shredded 0.15; heel and steadying), the drive table (force 12 from the
  north by heading, sound / torn / shredded, dry / half / nine tenths full: set never worse than furled abaft no-go),
  bare poles in force 7, cloth-only shredded repair, outside-vs-inside picking (a chest just inside her side: the ray
  meets her hull, the chest is hidden from outside; planks pick the cracked panel there from outside and through the
  ceiling from inside), every panel below the waterline mendable from inside, the ladder click logic, the inside
  overlays, the pumps' balance; the sealed-room test now looks for whole partition lines (the generator's waist changes
  moved the pump and hatch). **Self-test ALL OK (223)** on the generator's 17:36 data (Debug build).
- **Part C (the waist re-plan, the generator's 18:01 data):** the layout tests read the design now: the capstan on the
  centreline before the forecastle (walking up the middle it stops you), the rail jumped where no gun, gate, stair or
  ladder is, the hearth's smoke from the forecastle deck's grating (`chimney` in the `grating` keep-clear; HearthClient
  draws it there), the guns either side of the main channel (it's at the rail, over the ports: only what reaches
  above the shrouds' foot less 0.2 hits it), looking down at a free floor place of the waist, her topsides from abeam
  clear of the channels, the sealed-room test on whole partition lines, walking the hold's sides (the tighter bilge: a
  step up to 0.6 at the side), and the new **hold stair** both ways (passes). The companion door (hinge [-2.875, 3.5],
  -90°) unseals the lower deck by type, as the great cabin's. Offline: the 4 old failures (stern dry outline, the hole's
  reveal, note 32's cut; the tumbling shot passes now) **and the companion stair both ways: the generator's** — in
  0.3.9 the main deck's opening over it ran from x -7.25 with the beam at x -6 cut; now the opening starts at -6.0 and
  that beam (x -6.25..-5.75, y 1.375..1.875) is left across the stairwell: no headroom at its foot. **Self-test ALL OK
  (223)** on the 18:01 data.

### Helm and wind UI (0.3.10, C#; offline checks, not seen in the client)
- **Steering ("right turns left").** Read through every step (keys → `HelmSeat.Controls` → `Rudder` → `YawRate` →
  `Pos.Yaw`, the client's dead reckoning, the seat's yaw and vanilla's `PushYaw` camera push, `ShipFrame` and the
  renderer's `ModelYaw`, vanilla `EntityBoat` for comparison): D already puts the helm to starboard and swings her bow to
  starboard with way on, the same sign as vanilla's boats, and the helmsman faces the bow (helm yaw 0). Not found in the
  code; the one case it really reverses is sternway (sweeps, taken aback; `Sailing.Steer` < 0, as physics says). Now:
  `HelmMath.RudderInput`/`YawRate` (the server's formula, unchanged) with offline checks pinning the sign at the wheel, the
  tiller and the oars, both ways and astern; the wind panel says "Going astern: the helm works the other way"; the key
  hints say "A/D turn her bow to port/starboard" (they said "rudder"/"tiller"/"pull one side harder"). **To ask the
  user:** where it happened (was she making sternway or under sweeps? at the wheel, the tiller or the oars? first or
  third person?). The drawn wheel turns with `RudderShown` as before.
- **Wind panel** (`WindPanel.cs`): aboard any ship (not asleep), right edge of the screen: a compass card with her
  outline bow-up, north on the rim, the true wind (pale arrow, blowing toward her from where it comes) and the apparent
  wind (orange); under it force, name, m/s and knots, from where; the apparent wind's speed and side; at the helm the point
  of sail and tack ("Close-hauled, starboard tack", "In irons", "Taken aback"), and sternway. **Toggle: Ctrl+U**
  (Controls: "Magelan: wind panel aboard") or `.magelan wind [on|off]`; `ModConfig/magelan-client.json` `WindPanel`.
  The helm's own wind line stays.
- **Her motion** (the user then confirmed the "reversed steering" was at the wheel with no sails, most likely going
  astern under the sweeps, "with no movement direction indicator I could not tell"): the panel's first line and the
  helm's line say "AHEAD / ASTERN x kn (y b/s)" or "DRIFTING to port/starboard" (more sideways than along her keel),
  "Stopped", plus "sweeps astern" (panel) and a boat's "oars pulling/backing"; a green arrow on the card shows her course
  over ground relative to her bow (`HelmMath.Motion`: way + leeway). Offline check added (`-- helm`, 12 ALL OK).
- **Masthead pennants** (`PennantRenderer.cs`): a red streamer with a white stripe at the main truck (4.5 long) and the
  fore (3), from `masts[].y1` in the design, streaming down the apparent wind, fluttering faster as it freshens, hanging
  slack in a calm. `.magelan pennants [on|off]` (`Pennants`).
- **Map** (`ShipMapLayer.cs`, `HelmUiSystem.cs`, `MapPackets.cs`): a vanilla map layer "Ships" (its own tab, on and off
  like the waypoints): every ship as her real-size plan (from her collision voxels, `ShipPlan`: hull, castles, head and
  bowsprit, 44 long over all), filled with a darker edge, turned to her heading, at least 12 px long; the player's own
  ships gold with their names, others pale (named when big enough), rowing boats small and brown; hover: name, heading,
  knots, "last seen here". The wind where the player is: a pale blue arrow blowing toward the player's marker (hover:
  force, m/s, knots, from). Data: the server keeps a record of every ship (where last seen, who has been aboard or bound
  their spawn to her = "yours"; in the save game, dropped when she sinks or is picked up) and while the map is open
  answers every 3 s with the player's own ships anywhere and loaded ships within 2000 blocks. Shows on vanilla's world
  map and minimap and Wmap's minimap; Wmap's atlas (M) draws other mods' layers since Wmap 0.3.4.
- **Map arrow 180° off (also on land): Wmap's bug**, fixed in Wmap (`MapRenderer.DrawPlayer`: heading π − yaw, as
  vanilla's `RotateZ(π − yaw)`; Wmap took a player's view as (−sin, −cos), i.e. pitch 0, but a player's level pitch is
  π, so they look along (sin yaw, cos yaw)). Magelan's seats keep the player's own look in `Pos.Yaw` (vanilla's
  `PushYaw` only adds the ship's turn), so the arrow points where they look on deck, at the helm and in a boat.
- Tests: offline `-- helm` (11 checks: frame, keys, the sign ahead/astern at every heading, east → south with D, the
  boat's oars, the seats facing the bow, the panel's arrows, points of sail and tack, the map's turn, the plan) ALL OK;
  the full run has the same 15 failures as above (the generator's part C), none new; `-- motion` runs.

## The twenty-second client test (2026-10-14, Magelan 0.3.29): chalk notes 234–243 (→ 0.3.30) — PARKED

The user tested 0.3.29 (#14835, #15128) and left notes 234–243 and one comment. Work on 0.3.30 was **parked part-way**
(the user has bigger changes in mind): no zip was built for 0.3.30 and **carrack-m was not generated again** — the
generator (tools/shipgen/carrack.py, planking.py, view.py) has the changes below but `assets/magelan/config/ships|shipmeshes/
carrack-m.json` are still 0.3.29's. The C# changes are in and compile; the offline suite is at the known 3 failures
(861 ok). modinfo.json says 0.3.30 (bumped at the start). Note: an early Release build overwrote `release/magelan-0.3.29.zip`
with work-in-progress code (the 0.3.29 assets); rebuild a zip before anyone tests.

### The user's decisions (through the coordinator)

- **Shot damage** (234): a ball leaves only two marks on a slot (hull panel, deck cell, piece): **cracked** (its damaged
  texture: mend it) or **missing** (the slot empty: lay it again). No hole marks, no scars, no other states. A ball keeps the
  energy model (through the planking, the ceiling, cells and pieces behind) and also damages the slots next to where it
  strikes (cracked or knocked out the same way).
- **Plugging from inside** (the extra comment): one plug from inside seals **both layers** (ceiling and planking behind).
- **No poop side ports** (236), and **drop the placeable ports** (239): fixed lidded ports at the gun places, as before 0.3.28.
- **Gun arcs** (237, the user: "yes it should be [centred on the side board's perpendicular] but it does not work as you have
  done for bow guns"): each gun's train centred on the perpendicular to its side board, each way as far as the port's
  geometry allows, found by sweeping the real geometry.

### The notes

- **234** (shot holes not repairable): **done in C#, not seen.** The "holes" were 0.3.27's scars (ShipScars: a splintered
  decal where a ball went in and came out, on fixed planking too), which nothing mends. Now: no scars (`AddShotScars` is a
  no-op, `EntityShip.NoScars`); a save's scars become cracked slots as she loads (`ScarsToCracks`: the nearest slot to each,
  then the attribute removed). `ShipDamage.Blow` on a slotted design: a shot on her fixed planking (no slot within the hit
  radius) is routed to the nearest slot within `RouteReach` 0.9; a slot's hurt after a shot is at least cracked
  (`ShotStep`: cracked or out, never scraped), no seam wear from shots on panels; **splash**: the slots round the strike within
  `SplashReach` 0.8 take `SplashShare` 0.3 of its energy between them (`Splash`, not lost to the shot), cracked or out the
  same way. The hover/repair texts for cracked ("Mend with …") and out ("Stove in: lay it again …") were already there.
  Old test expectation moved: HullSlotTests "a shot of 20 kJ scrapes it" → cracks it. Tests written: `notes0330`
  `Shots0330` (40 panels × 3 angles × 3 energies, 20 fixed-strip hits, 30 deck cells: every slot sound/cracked/out, nothing
  between, routed, splash), self-test `Notes0330Aboard` (a 9-pounder on a frame's strip, an old scar → cracked). Not run
  yet (they need the regenerated design for the rest of `notes0330`; `Shots0330` itself works on 0.3.29's assets).
- **235** (beam ends "cut at angle as rib outer face projection"): **done in the generator, not generated.** The screenshot
  was the boat gallows' beam: its end was cut square at the post's outer face at the beam's foot, so its top stuck out where
  her side leans in. Now `beam_end_wedge(..., zfun)` puts every corner of a beam's end on her side's face there: the gallows
  beams on the posts' outer face (`hull_hw − 0.03`), every deck beam on the ribs' outer face (`rib_outer`: under a weather
  deck the planking's back, 0.005 short of it, square to her side in plan; over an open deck the bulwark's inside), one rule
  for all four corners of a beam on the line where the weather deck steps. No meshcheck run yet.
- **236** (poop side ports): **done in the generator, not generated.** `CASTLE_GUNS` without the poop's (plain bulwark both
  sides, no lid, no gun place); the stern chasers stay. Saves: their gun places' guns go to the hold (`RescueOrphanedGuns`).
- **237** (gun arcs, 4th time): **partly done.** Root cause, in plain words: 0.3.27 offset the limits by the side angle but
  the gun still pointed square across her and its port's cheeks were cut square across her; **0.3.28 (note 226) then put the
  arc's centre back to square on purpose** ("about square, the shorter way first"), so on the curved bow "square" was 16.5°
  (forecastle-1, x 19) and 13° (forecastle-2, x 17) aft of the side board's perpendicular; 0.3.29 widened the arc to 45° with
  a simplified cheek check (a slab with square-across cheeks: it ignored the carriage, the frames standing inside the
  bulwark and her side) and gave the forward bow gun the bow side first, believing "−" was toward the bow — it is toward the
  stern (the HUD said "33° to the stern"). So forecastle-1 trained −33.25 (50° aft of the perpendicular, through the jamb and
  the frame) .. +24.5 (8° forward of it). Built (generator, not generated): ports square to the side board where it runs
  aslant (`PORT_SQUARE`, `port_phi`, `port_skew`: the inside opening HULL_T·sin φ aft of the outside's; cuts `x0in/x1in` in
  planking.py, the reveals, the voxels), each gun standing along the normal (`out`, `yaw`, `box`, `sel` turned), so train 0 is
  the perpendicular; limits swept against her real surfaces (`GunSweep`, `sweep_gun_limits`: barrel radius and carriage
  boxes against points over every non-moving quad within 2.6 m, 0.02 apart; elevation found over the train range, then the
  train laid anywhere in it; the lid; then neighbours symmetric (no BOW_PAIR priority), rooms, gates, stairs, breeching,
  each recorded in the fixture's `trainBound`); `sweep_verify` at the end of the build; the design gets `gunSweep` for the
  offline test (`GunSweep0330`: clear at every limit, collides 1° past a "hull" limit). **Left**: the sweep is slow (~2 min a
  gun: cache each train's transformed points across the elevation search), then generate, run `notes0330`, the table
  (side angle, before → after) and the renders (`view.py 0330`: `0330-bow-top-aft/-fwd/-square`). First numbers from an
  aborted run: waist gun port-1 −37..37 (its carriage meets the bulwark's inside at 37°, before neighbours). The forecastle
  side angles: forecastle-1 16.5°, forecastle-2 13°, waist gun 5 4°, the rest 0.
- **238** (one lower deck panel won't come out, its neighbours do): **not finished.** Found: frame space 15 (x 6–8) has no
  panels in bands 3 and 4 on **both** sides (and 16 none in band 0): the generator's hull_slots refused them; a print of the
  reasons for every half that looks like a panel but isn't was added to hull_slots (`hull slots: N halves that look like
  panels but aren't`) — the next generation shows why (likely "a fitting on it": something within 0.3 of the ceiling there).
  Test `Panels0330` written (that spot a panel, both sides alike).
- **239** (drop placeable ports): **done in the generator, not generated.** `PORT_COVERS = False`, `GUN_PORT_SLOTS = False`:
  fixed lidded ports at every gun place, no board/port slots; the C# port-slot code is dormant (no design has them). Saves:
  the `port:`/`gunport:` slot states are dropped as she loads (ids gone); a boarded quarterdeck/forecastle port is the fixed
  port again. Old tests guarded (0327 poop guns, 0328 covers, 0329 ports/migration print "superseded" when the design has
  none); self-test's 0.3.29 port step replaced by a check that there are none; the stern chaser step accepts no poop gun in
  its way.
- **240** (binnacle "button"): **done in the generator.** It was the binnacle's little lamp: an iron box with a yellow glass,
  on the cupboard's port side (it lit the card inside the cupboard; the compass has been on the lid since 0.3.12), unlit it
  read as a button. Removed (carrack and carrack-m).
- **241** (hatch textures): **done in C#, not seen.** The hatch leaf was one box with the planks texture stretched over 3 × 1.5
  blocks (ModularRenderer.AddBox maps a whole face to one texture) with iron straps. Now each leaf is laid with the
  trapdoor block's own vanilla mesh (`BlockMesh` of the stack's trapdoor), tiled about a block square (a 3 × 1.5 leaf: 3 × 2
  tiles), scaled to the leaf's thickness; the old boxes stay as the fallback.
- **242** (stretched textures at the bow and stern): **done in the generator (planking.py `skin`), not generated.** Each
  planking quad's corners get u by where they are along their own ring (arclength from a common x, 16 texels a block),
  so sheared quads at the ends (the stem's rake, the ceiling easing in over the hold's floor) show the grain along the planks
  at the right density; a quad spanning more than one texture is cut along its length. Metric (planking quads along strakes
  with texture density off by more than a fifth): 0.3.29 493 of 21186; after: not measured yet
  (scratch `uvmetric.py`; test `Textures0330`). Before view: `texview notes 242`.

  cell about the hinge) is added to an open door's `PickBoxes`, so the crosshair and the server's pick take the open leaf
  as the door and right-click shuts it, as vanilla's. Test `Door0330`.

  planking first, then the ceiling, from the one right-click if the planks go round; on a ceiling that's in with its planking
  out it lays the planking through it (`HullBehind`, `HullLayCount`; client target and hints `hint-hull-lay-both`,
  `hint-hull-lay-behind`); the repair tool's way (`Repair`) does the same; `DamagePartFor` on a sound ceiling with its
  planking hurt gives the planking (mend it from inside); the look-at text adds "Her planking behind it is out: the sea comes
  in here …" (`damage-behind-out`, `-cracked`). Self-test step in `Notes0330Aboard`.

## The twenty-first client test (2026-10-14, Magelan 0.3.28): chalk notes 232–233 (→ 0.3.29)

Two ships: **#12600** (a carrack-m launched from 0.3.27, the user's own companion begun on it) and **#14372** (launched
from 0.3.28). Besides the notes, asked for through the round: step D2.3 (her side in two layers), the castle ports redone
(a board, then a port in it) and the bow guns' full swing. The capstan stays where it is.

### The notes

- **233, with 230** ("#230 was not just on deck. it was next to castle stairs as i made in the save file. but it could not
  enclose it"; "i told before on #230 companion way here with enclosed walls but could not enclose it with partitions"):
  **done**, and the companion there is carrack-m's own now.
  - **Her layout on #12600** (read from a copy of the save): a steep stair (piece 180) with its foot on the lower deck's
    centreline cell x −2..−1 (`f:lower:−2:−1`), up forward (+x) to the waist at x 0 (its box x −2..0, z −0.44..0.44); the
    waist's centreline cells x −3..0 taken up (`main:−3:−1`, `main:−2:−1`, `main:−1:−1`) with the main deck beam's length at
    −2 across them (`beam:main:−2:−1`); a wall to port on z −0.5, x −3..0, two rows (`b:main:−3/−2/−1:−2:s:0/1`); the great
    cabin's old centre door (her front still at −3 there) planked (`b:main:−4:−1:e:0..2`); the quarterdeck stair beside it
    (x −2..0, z 0.56..1.44). The server's audit: before the cells came up she put the starboard walls on the well's own cells
    and took them away again; after, only the port wall.
  - **Why the rest was refused**: the starboard side (z 0.5) lies between the well and the quarterdeck stair. Its places on
    the well's cells were shut once those came up, and their twins on the stair's cells ran into **the quarterdeck stair's
    guard**, which beside a drop (the well) stood solid from the stair's treads up (beside a deck it starts at a man's
    height); the **door at its head** (x 0) met the side walls' ends in its own plane; the **roof boards** lay exactly on the
    walls' tops. Fixed: a stair's guard beside a design well starts at a man's height as beside a deck (`DesignWellBeside`);
    a wall stops at the face of a door across its end (`ModularGeometry.WallEnds`); a hood's board lies 0.002 over the walls.
    Offline with her exact layout (`notes0329`, `CompanionUser0329`): her stair, port wall and planked door, then both rows
    to starboard, the door at the head and the two roof boards: all 18 placed; the stair walks up (3.00) and down, its door
    open; the quarterdeck stair beside it walks too (0.1 off its middle: the new wall is a quarter block into its cell).
  - **The default**: carrack-m's companion is where she laid hers: the well x −3..0 on the centreline and the beam's length
    at −2 start out; the stair from `f:lower:−2:−1` up forward; walled on z −0.5 and z 0.5 (two rows on the waist, three in
    the great cabin), its after end on x −3 in the cabin, a door at its head (`b:main:0:−1:w:0`, swinging out onto the
    waist), two hood boards over the waist's cells (`hh:main:−2:−1`, `hh:main:−1:−1`). The great cabin's front opens into it
    at k 0, the cabin's door a cell to port (k −1). The old stairwell in the cabin's starboard corner is gone: one companion.
  - **Saves** (layout revision 1, docs/modular-pieces.md §14): a ship laid before keeps her deck as she had it (#12600: her
    own well, her old stairwell's well still open; #14372: her deck at the new companion's place laid), and the new entries
    are remembered as laid, not put: nothing of her own build is touched. A new ship has the new companion.
- **232** (quarterdeck, x −3: "here and other side gun cannot be placed as says someone standing"): **done.** Not a person:
  "someone standing" is `gunplace-blocked`. Port 3's working room (its trail's slices) reached forward over the **breastwork
  railings on the quarterdeck's edge** (their solid layer from x −2.0625; the room to −2.0). Fixed both ways: the generator
  keeps every castle gun's room on her deck clear of the breastworks' layer (to x −2.149 now), and a railing counts against
  a gun's carriage only (`GunPlaces.CarriageSlices`, 5 of 8 slices: the crew step round a waist-high rail). Offline: a gun
  on every gun place of a new ship, quarter-3 both sides (`GunsMount0329`); the self-test mounts both quarter-3 guns.

### Asked for through the round

- **D2.3 her side in two layers** (the user: "maybe thick hull is just two panel blocks in thickness placed. in open decks
  just one placed"; "on open decks boards, we can keep hull thickness maybe original and have ribs protruding"; "this also
  would count to hull damage resistance and damage mechanics"): **done.** Under her weather decks (the hold, the lower
  deck, the cabins under the quarterdeck and the poop, the forecastle) her planking (`hull:<p|s>:<station>:<band>:<a|f>`)
  and a **ceiling** inside the ribs (`ceil:...`), each a panel naming the other; the ribs 0.5 between them; the inside
  smooth (no riders or inlays; the knees against the ceiling). Over her open decks (the bulwarks) one thin layer, the ribs
  standing proud inside it as in 0.3.28. Where the weather deck steps (x −9, −2, 14) a face closes the step. 642 panels:
  321 planking, every one with its ceiling. Each axe-removable and laid with planks or boards; one out shows the ribs and
  the other layer's back; both out, a hole. docs/slots.md §13.
  - **Resistance** (damage data `ceilToughness` 40, `ceilBacking` 0.3, `linedLeak` 0.5): a shot on a lined spot cracks it
    at 40 kJ and holes the planking at 80 (its ceiling cracked behind it); a 9-pounder's 145 kJ holes both and goes on;
    with its ceiling out the planking takes it all (100 kJ holes it and goes on). Before 0.3.29: cracked at 28, holed at
    56. A ball from outside meets the planking then the ceiling; from inside the ceiling then the planking (a fix this
    round: a shot breaking a ceiling from inside skipped her planking behind it, `ShipDamage.Pierce`). The bulwarks as
    before.
  - **Leaks**: the planking out behind a sound ceiling half (one half-panel 1.6 deep: 0.63 t/s), both out all (1.26), a
    ceiling out alone nothing; a room opens only through both.
  - **Grounding and scraping** wear the planking first (15 kJ: planking 0.054, ceiling 0); the ceiling takes its share
    only once the planking's cracked.
  - **Saves**: every ceiling in and sound; old hull damage stays on the planking.
- **The castle ports, a board then a port** (the user: "removable side board as with hull and then we place port inside
  it"): **done.** 0.3.28's covers are dropped as such: each castle port's place is a **board** (`port:<gun>`, its hole
  planked over) and a **port** (`gunport:<gun>`, the lidded port). An axe in building mode takes the board out (an
  opening; its planks back) or the port out (its trapdoor back; not with a gun on it or its lid open); **any vanilla
  trapdoor** (no new item) right-clicked into an opening puts the port in; planks or boards board an opening over (not
  with the port in it). A gun needs the port in. The poop's six start boarded over, the quarterdeck's six (port 3
  included) and the forecastle's four with their ports in. Saves: 0.3.28's open cover → a port in, planked → boarded over;
  a gun saved on a port keeps it. docs/slots.md §12. (The ports couldn't be her side's ordinary panels: 10 of the 16 port
  frame spaces are fixed by the castle breaks' sweeps, the poop's moulding and the forecastle's stepped sides.)
- **The bow guns' swing**: the castle guns train up to 45° each way (`GUN_TRAIN_CASTLE`; the waist's stay at 25 less their
  neighbours), as far as the port's cheeks, the lid, the catheads, the neighbours and the breeching allow; a mast, a
  cathead, a shroud rail or a fixture only counts against the carriage, not the crew's room behind it. The forecastle pair
  shares the space between them: the forward gun first toward the bow, the after one at least 9° that way
  (`BOW_PAIR_AFT`). Each side the same (− toward the bow):
  - forecastle-1 (forward): −33.25..24.5 (was −9.5..15.25): toward the bow to its port's cheek (−33.75) and the cathead;
    aft to forecastle-2.
  - forecastle-2 (after): −9..41.75 (was −15.5..24.5): toward the bow to forecastle-1; aft to its own lid.
  - the rest: quarter-1 −17.25..24.5, quarter-2 −17.25..17.25, quarter-3 −7.5..17.25 (the deck's edge), poop-1
    −17.25..40.75, poop-2 −30..17.25, poop-3 −17.25..17.25, the waist as 0.3.28, the stern chasers −17..14.25.
  Views `0329-bow-left`, `-bow-right` (forecastle-1 trained to each limit).
- **Capstan**: on hold, not moved.

## The twentieth client test (2026-10-13, Magelan 0.3.27): chalk notes 223–231 (→ 0.3.28)

One ship, **#12600**, a carrack-m launched from 0.3.27. Besides the notes: step D2 as the user chose it on 0.3.26
(docs/step-d-plan.md §11), and the stern stair that couldn't be walked off. The capstan stays where it is (the user: "hold
with capstan movement").

### The notes

- **223** (the poop, its side ports: "these ports by default are not needed my mind"): **done with D2.4.** Every castle
  side port has a **cover** now (a deck slot of kind "port", docs/slots.md §11): in, the port is planked over (no lid, no gun
  can be mounted); out, it's the lidded port. The poop's three a side start planked over, the quarterdeck's and the
  forecastle's open. An axe in building mode takes the planking out (a port); planks or boards, right-click, plank it over
  again (not with a gun there, not with the lid open). A ship saved with a gun on a poop port keeps it: her port opens.
- **224** (the quarterdeck's side at frames 11–12: "place default ports also here"): **done.** A third port a side on the
  quarterdeck at x −3 (`gun-port-quarter-3`, `gun-starboard-quarter-3`), open, no gun mounted (as the other castle places).
- **225, 227** ("you did not move the default partition wall position with the rib and beam here and at bow"; "this
  partition's default position is with the upper floor's last rib"): **done.** The great cabin's front (its partitions, the
  doors, the window, the third row under the deck) on the quarterdeck's edge beam's line, x −2, and the forecastle's on the
  forecastle deck's, x 14: under the edge beam, at the frame there (were −3 and 15). The rooms (`greatcabin` to −2,
  `forecastle` from 14), the stairwell's inboard wall (now to −2: the landing −4..−2) and the paint zones (the strip −3..−2
  is the cabin's now) with them. Each moved piece keeps the key it had on its old line (`partition@-3,...`), so a ship laid
  before doesn't get a second front (hers stays where it was; a new ship has it under the beam). Views: `0328-cabin-front`,
  `-cabin-front-in`, `-forecastle-front`.
- **226** (the forecastle's forward gun "trained 15° to the bow": "these and other guns still don't have full right left
  arc to swivel"): **done.** Every gun trains up to 25° each way from square (12 before; 0.3.27 centred the bow guns' 12 on
  her side's normal, so that one trained only 4.5..15.25 toward the bow), as far as its room, its neighbours, the gates,
  the gallows, the stairs and its own breeching (clear of its carriage) allow, the shorter way first: the waist guns
  −17..17 (gun 4 −16.75..17; gun 5 and quarter-3 −10.25..17.25, by the stairs), the quarterdeck's −17.25..18.25 and
  −17.25..17.25, the poop's −17.25..19, −20..17.25, −17.25..17.25, the forecastle's −9.5..15.25 (the foremast, a cathead)
  and −15.5..24.5, the stern chasers −17..14.25. The least either way 9.25°.
- **228, 229** (the great cabin, a rib meeting a deck beam: "looks different from the ones holding the boats … some
  triangular hole … beam going over rib, with sloped end and rib cut flush with the beam"; "the sloped support could be just
  a straight triangle and a bit larger"): **done.** Every rib and beam joint (`STRAIGHT_KNEES`): the rider up the wall to the
  beam's underside, cut flush there; the beam over it out to the ceiling, its end sloped to the ceiling where her side leans
  in (`beam_end_wedge`: the gap under the square end was the triangular hole); a straight knee under the beam against the
  rider, 0.6 down it and 0.55 in under the beam (a curved one 0.25 × 0.42 before; the hold's 0.7 in). The gallows' knees the
  same. Views `0328-knee`, `-knee-hold`, `-gallows`.
- **230** ("I want to move the companion way here. Managed to place stairs but cannot enclose it with walls, roof and
  doors"): **done (mod side).** Half the walls round a well were anchored on the well's own (taken up) cells, so they were
  refused: each such border place has a **twin** on the same segment anchored across the line (usable only while the first
  one's cell is out), so a wall round a well stands on the deck beside it and a door at a stair's head swings out onto the
  landing. The waist's top row was cut in half by her low bulwark (and doors refused there): a place a block inboard of her
  side counts as inside her. A **roof**: a hood's place over every open-deck cell (`hh:<deck>:<i>:<k>`), a plank slab on the
  walls' tops (2 over the deck), not tied to the cell under it, so a stair walks under it; offered by aiming at a wall's
  upper part or a laid roof board. The user's well (x −3..0 on the centreline beside the quarterdeck stair) offline: walls,
  a window, a door and the roof placed, the stair walked up and down. (With the great cabin's front now at −2 that well's
  after cell is the cabin's: the hood a block forward, or the well behind the cabin's door.) View `0328-hood` (one forward of
  the mainmast).
- **231** (the quarterdeck, the poop stair: "move default stair location 1 block to right aka centre line, and doors to
  cabin move where are stairs now"): **done** for the poop stair: on the centreline (z −0.5..0.5) up to the poop front, the
  poop cabin's door where it stood (z 0.5..1.5), the poop's railing open over the stair's head. The quarterdeck's stair
  and the forecastle's stay where they are: the user's new companion (230) is on the waist's centreline beside the
  quarterdeck stair. View `0328-poop-stair`. (Ships laid before keep their stair and door: the pieces keep their codes.)

### Step D2

- **D2.1 one-block panels, D2.2 no strips** (`HALF_PANELS`): each frame space is two panels, its after half from the frame's
  middle to the space's (`hull:<p|s>:<station>:<band>:a`) and its fore half (`:f`): 321 panels (206 reaching under her
  waterline), 152 frame spaces with both halves. The planking over a frame is split on its middle, each half its panel's:
  two neighbours out leave only the **rib** (drawn in her side's thickness, 0.22 wide, while a panel beside it is out; its
  collision the voxel column aft of its line, as a deck beam's). Saves: magelanDmgF "v" 4: a 0.3.26–0.3.27 panel's hurt (its
  code is the after half's now; the fore half's has its own bit, `ShipPart.Hull(..., half)`) goes to both halves; the slot
  states follow the hurt. Leaks scale with a panel's area (one half out 1.6 deep: 0.49 t/s).
- **D2.3 the thick wall with the ribs inside: not done this round.** It moves every ceiling 0.25 inboard (every room
  narrower: the pieces' places, the stairs, the gun places, the beds, the hold's stowage, the hydrostatics with it); put off
  to keep 0.3.28 testable.
- **D2.4 placeable gun ports**: the port covers (223 above). Lang: `hint-port-take`, `hint-port-lay`, `port-has-gun`,
  `port-lid-open`, `port-already-open`, `gunplace-port-covered`.
- **D2.5 deck cells and pieces take shots**: every plain deck cell is a damage part (80 kJ, `damage.cellToughness`: cracked
  at 0.35, stove in at 0.7, the shot goes on through it; a cell with a gun, a piece or a fixture on it stops at cracked; a
  cell that's out stays out until laid; beam lengths stay her structure). Partitions 30 kJ, doors 40, windows 15, railings
  20: cracked they're drawn cracked and mended with boards and nails (held right-click); shot away they're gone (splinters;
  a piece something hangs on stops at cracked). A cracked cell: the repair tool (2 boards, 1 nails); stove in: laid again
  with planks blocks or boards. Saves: magelanDmgF "v" 3 (cell codes a new part kind: deck, cell i, k; an older save's
  per-station deck hurt cracks the nearest cell at most, the rest dropped).
- **D2.6 the stern lower-deck stair**: the obstacle where you stepped off (foot near x −12, by the mizzen) was her side's
  curve at the stern quarter (deck +0.25..0.5 at x −12.9..−12.4, z ±3.3), not the tiller: a walked stair's landings are
  checked a man's width now (0.6), so that spot is refused ("flight-no-landing": no room to step off the stair); of the 942
  stairs the offline sweep can place, every one walks.
- **Capstan**: on hold, not moved.

- To see in the client: the poop's sides plain (its ports planked over), an axe in building mode on one opening it, a gun
  on it, planking it over again; the quarterdeck's third port a side; the cabin fronts under the edge beams; a gun's train
  both ways; the joints and their knees in the cabins, the hold and on the gallows; the poop stair on the centreline; a hood
  round a stair well on the waist; panels a block long (a shot, an axe), two out side by side with the rib between them; a
  shot cracking and stoving in a deck cell, and shooting a partition away.

## The nineteenth client test (2026-10-13, Magelan 0.3.26): chalk notes 198–222 (→ 0.3.27)

Two ships: **#446**, a carrack-m launched before 0.3.24 (her saved layout: the long hatches, older pieces and stair
places), and **#10039**, launched from 0.3.26. Each #446 note was checked against a fresh carrack-m; what's only her saved
layout is said so. Besides the notes: the user's question on stairs and beams (stairs need a 3-block opening, the beams are
every 2: "rebuild with 3-block rib spacing, or make partial beam removal possible?"), then "in addition to opening floors we
should add side ribs automatically" and "if a cut is done then the cross beam should go till the next beam"; "the cannon
report is muffled and does not sound like a cannon"; "cannons should allow aiming and fire while aiming", "the starting click
to aim makes it point too low"; and "made holes in ship but cannot repair — does not give that option".

### The notes

- **198** (#446, the forecastle, "would it be ok if we move capstan here?"): **answered, not moved.** Its bars' ring is 2.9
  across (1.02 out from its middle, the crew walking them 1.4): in the forecastle (front at x 15, the foremast at 17.5,
  0.45 thick) there are 2.5 between the front and the mast, so the bars would sweep through the front's door and partitions
  or into the mast. It stays at 10.5 in the waist (all its clearances as before). See the open questions.
- **199** (#446, "placed the wrong floor, can't change its orientation with the wrench as vanilla"): **fixed.** Vanilla's
  wrench, right-click a piece: a wall piece (partition, window, railing, door) goes round its cell to the next side, a floor
  piece (chest, bed, stair, ladder-stair...) faces the next way it fits; contents, paint and id kept (`ShipRotate.cs`).
  (The piece there was a partition on the hold's top row, put while aiming at an opening in the lower deck: an opening is
  now also laid with planks or boards out of building mode, see "mending", and the opening's own target comes first.)
- **200** ("stair railing verticals should go into the stair beams, they hang"): **fixed.** The ship's stairs' balusters
  reach 0.04 down into the stringers (they stopped 0.25 over them).
- **201** (#446, "hatches were the long version, I removed them but couldn't find the item to replace them"): **#446's saved
  layout** (0.3.23's long hatches); fresh ships have the 3 × 3 trapdoor hatches. **Made discoverable**: in building mode with
  anything but planks, boards or an axe in hand, an opening says "a planks block (or 4 boards) lays it again; trapdoors (one a
  cell) make a hatch over it: solid ones a cargo hatch, window trapdoors a glazed one"; the building-mode message says
  trapdoors make hatches. The item is vanilla's trapdoor (`game:trapdoor-solid-*`, `-window-*`), as in 0.3.20.
- **202** ("lanterns on the mast's other sides aren't on the same level, only the original shows its hook fitting"):
  **fixed.** The mod's mast hooks at the design's own mast hook's height over its deck and reach from the mast (the
  mainmast's 2.94 over the main deck, 0.76 out; the fore- and mizzenmasts' 2.56 and 0.66 / 0.6, on the forecastle, the
  poop and the lower deck; none where the mod's ceiling hooks already hang beside the mast, under the castles); a lantern on
  one is drawn with its iron arm (and a band round the mast where the design has none at that height). A lantern saved on one moves up with it.
- **203** (#446, the poop: "ports placed illogically, different distances; most aft ports we don't need; max 3 guns a side
  is ok"): **fixed.** The poop's three a side, one in each bay (x −15, −13, −11, 2 apart); the one by the transom (poop-4,
  −16.75) gone, poop-3 from −13.5 to −13. A gun mounted on poop-4 on a ship saved before goes into her hold. The quarterdeck's
  two a side and the stern chasers stay (no clash pairs left but the stern chasers with poop-1).
- **204** ("reduce how far guns are pulled in when stored and the recoil"): **fixed.** Run back 0.75 when fired and housed
  (1.1 before): the muzzle still 0.14 behind the shut lid; every gun's working room 0.35 shorter.
- **205** ("guns at the bow where the side curves have a limited arc as on flat sides"): **fixed.** A gun's train is centred
  on her side's normal at its port (generator `side_angle`, where it's 3° or more off square): the forecastle's forward gun
  trains 4.5..15.25° toward the bow (−9.5..12 before; the foremast stops its trail beyond), the after one 1..25° (−12..12).
- **206** ("gun port covers open at the wrong angle at the bow"): **fixed.** Each lid hinged along her side's tangent at its
  top (along x where the side runs straight), its hinges' knuckles on that line.
- **207** (#10039, "shooting the other ship but looks like no damage", the great cabin's side): the ball **did** hit her: the
  crosshair named her fixed planking (a strip or band that's no panel) which takes a shot as her structure and had nothing to
  show. **Fixed: every hit leaves scars**: a splintered patch where the ball went in and where it came out, outside and
  inside (`ShipScars.cs`, `ScarRenderer.cs`, textures `dmg-shot-a/b`): rays the clients follow to her drawn surfaces; the
  latest 64 kept and sent; mending the planking there (the repair tool or laying a panel) takes them off; not drawn over a
  panel that's out.
- **208** (#446, the hold, from outside under her: "protruding corner of another element"): **fixed.** A deep beam (the
  hold's, 0.75) ended at her ceiling at its mid height; aft, where her side curves in under it, its lower corner came out
  through her planking. Every beam now ends where her ceiling is narrowest over its depth and breadth.
- **209** ("says planks but actually boards"): **fixed.** The repair texts say boards (vanilla's `plank-*` items) and planks
  blocks (`planks-*`); the plurals right.
- **210** ("damage below the waterline isn't visible from inside"): **fixed.** A scraped panel shows faint splits on its
  ceiling (`dmg-scraped-in`); cracked, leaking, worn and holed already had inside looks.
- **211, 222** (#10039 and #446: "you did not move stairs to avoid breaking beams"; "cannot place stairs perpendicular to the
  ship"): see **Stairs and beams** below.
- **212** ("can we make gun ports placeable: either a side board or a port; here I'd like to try a gun"): **planned for the
  next build** with D2: a castle side's hull panel slot takes planking or a lidded port piece, which makes a gun place there.
- **213, 214, 220** (#446, the boat gallows: "a weird break, not continuous"; "these beams don't need to go thinner, the same
  cross section as ribs"; "they could be the same as the castles' ceiling beams and ribs"): **fixed.** Each post is its frame
  carried on up, straight, in the frame's section (its inner face the frame's line, out to the planking's) from the rail to
  the beam (no break at the rail); the beam a deck beam's section (0.23 × 0.5) across their heads, its ends flush with the
  posts; a curved hanging knee under each end as in the castles.
- **215** ("blocked moving past the stairs where the way looks clear: too wide a collision box?"): **fixed.** A ship's
  stair's solid guard (the quarter block outside its side) starts a man's height up where there's deck beside it, so it no
  longer stands a quarter block into the next cell at walking height; over a drop it's as before. Saved stairs get it.
- **216, 217** ("this beam doesn't align with the grid, the rib spacing or the placements"): **fixed.** The quarterdeck's
  and the forecastle deck's edge beams centred on their frames' lines (−2, 14) as every other beam (they were flush with the
  deck's edge on the castle's side, 0.125 off); 0.115 of each shows past the deck's edge over the waist.
- **218** ("a break in the texture here and in the floor aft", the forecastle deck's underside): **fixed.** The castle decks'
  rows between their fronts' lines and their edges (x −3..−2, 14..15) were fixed planking drawn by the voxel shapes, out of
  line with the cells beside them; they're deck cells now, drawn as every other.
- **219** ("how to combine the side gate with a plank way to a pier? is sideways opening historical?"): **answered and
  built.** The gate swings inboard about its hinge (as it did): historically an entry port had a half door opening inboard,
  or none, and people came aboard over a **brow** from the quay. **The brow**: with her anchored and the gate open, boards in
  hand, right-click the gate: it runs out from the port's sill to the nearest solid footing within 5 blocks of her side (no
  steeper than about 40°); walked off her onto the quay and back aboard (a ladder of kind "brow", the walking animation);
  the same click takes it in; it comes in by itself when she weighs anchor or gets under way, the gate shuts or the footing
  is gone (`ShipBrow.cs`, `BrowRenderer.cs`). She carries it (the boards in hand only say "the plank").
- **221** ("can't take up this deck cell, probably the lantern hook: make hooks so we can remove those fixed"): **fixed.**
  carrack-m has no ceiling hooks of her own any more (their iron eyes fixed the cell over them): her lanterns hang on the
  mod's ceiling hooks (the cell half a block aft, beam-free), each drawn with its eye; a cell with a lantern under it is
  taken up once the lantern's down. A lantern saved on a hook that's gone moves to the nearest free one.

### Stairs and beams (the user's question; notes 211, 222)

The frames stay every 2 blocks. Two things:
1. **A beam's collision** is the one voxel column aft of its frame's line, so the cell forward of a beam is free of it under
   the deck: **a stair across her** goes up through a 3 × 1 well (along z) between two beams, no beam cut (note 222).
2. **Trimmed beams**: a beam under a well is in **lengths a cell wide** (beam segments: deck slots of kind "beam",
   docs/slots.md §10): one comes out only once **both deck cells beside it are open**, at most **two lengths of one beam**,
   next to each other; never a castle deck's edge beam, a beam at a mast's partners, or one with something hung on it or
   standing in it. **It's automatic**: open the deck (an axe in building mode on the cells), then put the stair: a stair or
   ladder-stair through a well that a beam's length is in the way of takes it out with it (its planks back), and the hint
   says so; an axe in building mode on it does it too. The **carlings** (fore and aft timbers, the deck beams' section, along
   the opening's sides, from the whole beam aft to the whole beam forward, the cut beam's ends meeting them) appear by
   themselves while a length is out and go when it's laid back (planks or boards, right-click it; not while a stair passes
   through it, nor into the room a man climbing it needs). Refused stairs say why: "a deck beam is in the way: open the deck on both sides of it and put the stair here
   again: the beam comes out with it", or for a beam with no lengths there "put the stair elsewhere, or across her".
   carrack-m: 201 lengths (main deck 85, lower deck 78, quarterdeck 27, poop 9, forecastle 2), 402 carlings.

### Mending holes (the coordinator, from the user: "made holes in ship but cannot repair")

- An opening (a hull panel out, a deck cell up) is laid **out of building mode too**, with **planks blocks or boards** (4 a
  block, vanilla's recipe): right-click the opening, or hold right-click on it with the repair tool's way (its "lay" job
  takes them from the hand and the inventory). The look-at text: "Stove in: lay it again with N planks blocks or 4N boards".
  The crosshair's pick of an opening checked from inside her, from the water and from her deck at a bulwark's.

### Guns

- **Fire while aiming**: left-click fires the gun being laid while right is held (the press is also watched each frame, in
  case the game doesn't pass a left click on while right is held).
- **Laying is relative**: when you start, the gun stays as it's laid and your view is its zero; moving the view lays it by
  as much (no drop to 5° down when you start looking down at it); past a limit it stops there and comes off it at once. The
  client sends its aim ten times a second; the server lays it there (or, from an older client, relative to its view).
- **The report** (`tools/sounds/cannon_report.py`, cannon1..3.ogg): cut from vanilla's recorded explosions (large, medium,
  small): the 0.29 s of silence cut, a sharp crack at once (the medium's or the small's attack over the large one's body),
  a +3 dB lift over 2.5 kHz, a soft limiter, normalised to about −0.8 dBFS, the rumble faded out by 1.55-1.85 s; no
  low-pass, the distance's dulling milder (1 − 0.6 × far²).

  | file | length | peak | onset | quiet (−60 dB) by |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | cannon1.ogg (large) | 1.850 s | −0.96 dBFS | ≤ 1.8 ms | ~1.73 s |
  | cannon2.ogg (medium) | 1.700 s | −0.80 dBFS | ≤ 1.8 ms | ~1.6 s |
  | cannon3.ogg (small) | 1.600 s | −0.73 dBFS | ≤ 1.8 ms | ~1.49 s |

  Their first 0.1 s about −9 dBFS RMS. The old synthesis is kept behind `--synth`.

### Built (generator and C#) for 0.3.27 (not seen in the client)

- **Generator** (MAGELAN_MODULAR=1 python carrack.py): the beams' collision the voxel column aft of each frame line
  (`beam_coll`); `beam_segments` (deck slots of kind "beam" a cell wide, under every well's cells but the castle decks' edge
  beams and the masts' partners) with their carlings drawn bay by bay (`CARLING_W`, the deck beams' section, ending flush
  on the whole beams' faces); the castle edge beams centred on their frames (−2, 14), their rows −3..−2 and 14..15 deck
  cells (634 cells, 201 beam lengths); every deck beam ending at the ceiling's narrowest over its depth and breadth (note
  208); the poop's ports (poop-4 gone, poop-3 −13); `side_angle` and the guns' trains centred on it, the castle guns'
  limits searched about it; the lids hinged on her side's tangent; recoil 0.75; the boat gallows' posts straight frame
  sections with a hanging knee, a deck beam's section across; her own ceiling hooks dropped for the mod's (`h:c:`), the
  hold's second lantern a bay forward of the mainmast (h:c:hold:4:-1). `shipgen.py`: beam lengths out of the full grid and
  their quads in slot groups; `meshcheck.py`: `--slots wells` / `wells-in` (random wells with their beam lengths out).
- **C#**: `ShipBeams.cs` + `BeamRules` (DeckSlots.cs), `ShipDecor.PlaceTakingBeams` (a stair or ladder-stair takes the
  lengths in its way, their planks back), a beam's length not laid back into a stair's climbing room; `ShipRotate.cs`
  (the wrench); `ShipScars.cs` + `ScarRenderer.cs`; `ShipBrow.cs` + `BrowRenderer.cs`; openings laid out of building mode
  with planks blocks or boards (`ShipSlots.LayNeed`, the repair tool's "lay" job); relative laying and firing while laying
  (`GunLaying.Relative`, `GunLayPacket` aim); mast hooks at the design's height and reach with their iron arms; the
  stairs' balusters into the stringers; the stairs' guards over decks a man's height up; `RescueOrphanedGuns`;
  `RehookLantern`.
- **Offline tests** (tests/MagelanTests, `NoteTests0327.cs`, `notes0327`): ALL OK; the full run 793 ok, 3
  failed (the known three: Sea's stern dry outline, the hole's reveal edges, note 32's panel). `design carrack-m`,
  `slots`, `hull`, `gunplaces`, `modular` ALL OK.
- **meshcheck** carrack-m: 0 conflicts ≥ 0.001 m² as designed, with random wells' beam lengths out (`--slots wells`, seeds
  1 and 2; `wells-in` 3), `--hull random`, `--slots checker` (the largest 0.0007, the hold's old ones).
- **Self-test** (tools/run_selftest.sh): "SELFTEST ALL OK" (365 SELFTEST lines). Its working blocks' reload step now compares
  the bloomery's fire before and after the reload: its ten game hours (~40 s at 30×) can run out while the forge heats.
- **Zip**: release/magelan-0.3.27.zip (Sea 0.3.5 unchanged).
- **Views**: docs/views/carrack-m-0327-*.png (gallows, gallows-across, gallows-rail, quarter-edge-under,

- To see in the client: a stair put through a well across a beam (the beam's length out with it, the carlings beside the

## Magelan 0.3.26 (2026-10-13): step D1 — hull panels as slots

The user said go for step D (docs/step-d-plan.md) on 2026-10-13; D1 is its first test build: carrack-m's hull planking as
slots that take shots. Contract: docs/slots.md §9. What D1 did otherwise than planned: docs/step-d-plan.md §10.

### Built (generator and C#) for 0.3.26 (not seen in the client)

- **Generator** (MAGELAN_MODULAR=1 python carrack.py): carrack-m's strakes below the main deck spaced again between seams on
  quarter-block lines (`HULL_BANDS`, `HULL_FIXED_BANDS`, `seamed_levels`); the wales two strakes 2.75..3.25 (0.3.25's
  2.6..3.2); every planking ring sampled at each frame strip's edges (±0.25 round each frame) and each frame space's middle
  (`planking.xgrid_rings`), so the planking is cut square across there; `hull_slots`: the panels (frame space × band × side,
  only whole ones; the rest fixed, the reasons counted), their quads in the groups `slot:hull`, `slot:hull:bottom`,
  `slot:hull:bottomclad`, `slot:hull:interior` with `slotQuads`, the reveals round each (bare oak, drawn only while it's
  out), their collision voxels (`solids`), `hullSlots` and `hullBands` in her definition; no `hullOut`/`hullIn` for her;
  `hull_slot_checks`. `meshcheck.py --hull filled|empty|checker|random [--seed n]`; `view.py --empty=<id,...|demo>`, views
  `python view.py 0326`.
- **Panels**: 154 (77 a side): 102 reaching under her waterline (74 wholly under it), 24 over her main deck (the great
  cabin's and the poop cabin's sides, the forecastle's, two of the quarterdeck's bulwark), the rest between; by band
  (0 the bilge .. 8) 18, 26, 30, 28, 28, 11, 9, 2, 2; by room the hold 74, the lower deck 56, the great cabin 16. Fixed:
  72 frame spaces at her ends (not whole, or where her lines curve hard: |dz/dx| ≥ 1), 30 cut by an opening (all the
  waist's bulwark: gun ports, the entry port, scuppers; the castles' ports and windows), 18 castle breaks' sweeps, 12 under
  the forecastle's overhang, 8 with their inside in two rooms (at the castle fronts, the companion), 14 with a fitting
  on them (the rope ladders under the entry port, the poop cabin's window timbers). Planks to lay one: 2-6 blocks (3 for
  most). Her mesh 17.1 MB (16.9 before).
- **Mod**: `HullSlotDef`/`HullSlotLayout` (src/Ship/HullSlots.cs: ids, voxels, neighbours, chunks, picking an opening),
  `ISlotLayout` (deck cells and hull panels share `SlotStates` and `SlotMeshPlan`; both families' differences in
  `magelanSlots`), `ShipDefinition.HullSlots`/`Hull`/`FullGrid`; `EntityShip.HullPanels` (src/Ship/ShipHullSlots.cs): the
  state always what the panel's hurt says (cracked at 0.35, out at 0.7), her collision without the panels out, her rooms
  opened by them (`RoomEnclosed`, `SealedAt`), taking out (building mode, axe, 1.5 s; refused under her waterline afloat or
  with something against it) and laying (building mode, planks, right-click the opening), `/magelan hull
  [take|lay|crack|reset] <id>`. Damage: the panels are her hull's parts (part i = panel i), a blow's energy shared among
  them, a shot goes on through a panel it stove in, her fixed planking is structure (30 kJ, the shot goes on); grounding
  with no panel near hurts the nearest within her half beam. The sea: a panel out under her main deck an orifice of
  `damage.openLeak` (0.2 m² a 1.5 m² panel) once the sea is over its head, a weir over its sill while it's between; cracked
  ~1 l/s; over her main deck a downflooding point. Repairs: cracked, the repair tool (2 planks, 1 nails); out, "Stove in:
  lay it again with N planks blocks in building mode". Old saves (magelanDmgF without "v": 2) of carrack-m have their hull
  damage moved onto the panels. Wrecks: a panel out isn't in her wreck; broken up, 2-4 whole panels are stove in.
  Drawing: the hull family in `SlotRenderer` (chunks of a side's two frame spaces, the damage overlays on the panels'
  planking, her paint), drawn after each hull group; shadows; the paint picker and her surfaces know the hull groups.
- **Tuning**: `hullToughness` 80 kJ (28 cracks a panel, 56 stoves it in: a 9-lb ball at ~145 kJ stoves one in and cracks
  the far side's), `openLeak` 0.2: one panel out in the hold 1.6 under the water lets in 0.73 t/s; two at the pumps take
  out 0.40 t/s, so with one panel out she founders in about 34 minutes unless it's laid (664 t to founder); a cracked one
  lets in about a litre a second; one at her waterline with the sea half way up it 0.9 t/s over its sill. To balance
  after a client test (D3).
- **Tests**: `-- hull` (the mechanics), `-- design carrack-m` and `-- slots` run the hull contract too, the full run has
  `-- hull`. Offline (`dotnet run --project tests/MagelanTests -c Debug`): 765 ok, the 3 known failures (Sea stern dry
  outline, hole reveal edges, note 32's panel: the archived carrack's); `-- hull`, `-- design carrack-m`, `-- slots`,
  `-- gunplaces`, `-- modular`, `-- notes0325` ALL OK. The contract: 154 panels, every one's voxels its own, its quads,
  its strips' faces and its neighbours' edges, nothing drawn twice in 50 random states, no reveal drawn all in. Generator:
  `hull_slot_checks` all true; meshcheck 0 visible conflicts of 0.001 m² or more all in (`--hull filled`), and in four
  random states, a checkerboard and all out (`--hull random/checker/empty`). Self-test (`src/SelfTestHull.cs`, at the end
  of the run): 361 lines, SELFTEST ALL OK: a panel under her waterline stove in by 200 kJ (out of her collision, saved, her
  hold open), the sea coming in, laid again (the water stops), cracked by 30 kJ and mended, one under the water refused and
  one over it taken out, kept out over a reload, an old save's hull damage moved onto its panel (v 2), a carrack's gun
  stoving a panel in, eight panels out: she founders and goes down in 57 s, her wreck without them.
- **Views** (all panels in; with panels out: `0326-holes-*`, EMPTY_DEMO's seven panels): docs/views/carrack-m-0326-side,
  -side-dry, -bow-quarter, -stern-quarter, -close, -hold-side, -lowerdeck-side, -section, -holes-out, -holes-close,
  -holes-hold, -holes-lowerdeck, -holes-cabin; the same all-in views of 0.3.25's hull as docs/views/carrack-m-0325-*
  (stern-quarter excepted: that name was 0.3.25's own view). All in she looks as before: 0.02-0.3 % of the pixels differ

  the main deck (spaced again, about 0.3 still) in the close view (1.3 %) and the section (2.3 %); the copper line is at the

- To see in the client: her side and ceiling as before (the planking cut at the frames' strips: no seams should show); a
