## Magelan 0.3.25 (2026-10-13): stern chasers, older carracks archived, crawl handling

The user's answers on 0.3.24: note 179 meant the transom ("at back yes back facing ports"); "We can keep only modular now
and park in archive previous"; crawling at the wheel ("slowmo movement of walking speed with sails down, including turning
… being at wheel and then?"); and "fix also cannon fire sound. it is reverberating and with delay".

### Built (generator and C#) for 0.3.25 (not seen in the client)

- **Stern chasers (note 179)**: carrack-m has two more castle gun places, `gun-port-stern` and `gun-starboard-stern`, on
  the poop deck firing aft through lidded ports cut in the transom 1.875 either side of the centre (sill 0.25, lintel 1.0
  over the deck, the lids hinged under the taffrail, opening out and up by 110° + the transom's rake), empty as the other
  castle places. The nameboard is narrower, between the ports. Generator: `STERN_GUNS`/`STERN_PORTS`, `stern_guns`,
  `stern_port_lid`, `stern_port_clearances`, `castle_obstacles(stern=True)` (her sides limit their train instead of the
  transom); views `python view.py 0325`. Contract: docs/slots.md §7.1 (a gun along x: `port` is z0 y0 z1 y1, `wall` the
  transom's faces' x).
- Mod: `ShipCompartments.AlongX`/`PortBox` read a transom port (sealing with the lid shut, its downflood point if it were
  in a closed room; the waist's downflood check no longer mistakes a stern port's z for an x); `GunLaying.TrainAcross`
  (+ train = to starboard for a stern chaser; HUD "trained 5° to starboard", its limits "to port … to starboard"); the
  mount hint says "stern"; `/magelan fire stern` (port/starboard are the broadsides only; `FireBroadside(±1)` skips the
  chasers); `/magelan gunplaces` marks them `[aft]`. **Guns in each other's way** (new rule): a gun can't be mounted while
  another mounted gun's carriage is in its working room or its carriage in the other's (`GunPlaces.RoomsClash`,
  `gunplace-gun-room`): each stern chaser vs the poop's aftmost broadside guns on its side (poop-4 and poop-1) — one or the
  other, as a gun was shifted aft to chase. No guns laid as designed clash, nor the two chasers.
- **Older carracks archived**: only carrack-m's charter is in the creative inventory (`*-carrack-m`); carrack-m is plainly
  "Carrack" (entity, design name, "Charter of a carrack"). `carrack` and `carrack-a` stay registered (entities, charters,
  definitions, meshes) so saved ships and deeds in inventories keep working; their charters are named "(archived …)". The
  generator still builds them and their tests still run. docs/modular-pieces.md: "Archived designs".
- **Crawl handling (the sweeps)**: at the wheel with the sails furled, **Ctrl** (the sprint key) puts her under the sweeps
  (S at furled does too, as the sweeps astern did); then **hold W** ahead (CrawlSpeed 1.5 b/s), **hold S** astern
  (CrawlAsternSpeed 1.0), up to speed and stopped again in CrawlRampSeconds (2 s), stopping when let go; **A/D** turn her
  directly about her middle at CrawlTurn (6°/s) even standing still; no wind, leeway or sail. Ctrl again (then W) to set
  sail; letting go the anchor (Space) ends it too; Ctrl with sail set says to furl first. The old sweeps-astern state
  (SailSet −1) is folded in (a ship saved with it loads under the sweeps). Synced (`crawl`, `crawlDrive` with her speed and
  turn in "magelan"), the HUD says "Sails: furled, under the sweeps: pulling ahead / backing astern / holding her" and the
  keys. Boats keep their oars. Server config `CrawlSpeed`, `CrawlAsternSpeed`, `CrawlTurn`, `CrawlRampSeconds`. Pure maths
  in `Crawl` (src/Ship/Crawl.cs).
- **The cannon's report**: vanilla's large explosion (0.29 s of silence, then three seconds of rumble at nearly full
  loudness, played by the server) replaced by our own short shots (`tools/sounds/cannon_report.py` →
  `assets/magelan/sounds/effect/cannon1..3.ogg`: a 1 ms crack, a falling boom, a short rumble, gone by 1.4 s, mono), played
  by each client at the muzzle the moment the shot's packet comes (no server sound, so never twice), its pitch varied, no
  reverb, duller (low-pass) and a little lower further off, heard to 200 blocks.
- Tests: `-- notes0325` (also in `-- gunplaces`, `-- modular` and the full run): the stern chasers (symmetric, aft, port
  box, lid, open deck, the clash pairs and the mount rule, no downflood point), the archive (creative inventory, names,
  no recipe), the crawl maths (ahead/astern/holding, the ramp, coasting, the turn's sign and 90° time, when it holds); the
  cannon test checks the three report files. Self-test: the backing-off step is under the sweeps; `CrawlHarbour` in the gun
  pool (a carrack-m in a strong beam wind creeps ahead 4 s, stops, turns 90° to starboard standing, setting sail or
  dropping the anchor takes her off the sweeps); `SternChaserAboard` (mounted, run out, fires aft out of the transom, the
  poop gun in its way refused, taken up again); the archive check in Notes0314.
- Results: offline 734 ok, the 3 known failures (Sea stern dry outline, hole reveal edges, note 32's panel); `-- notes0325`,

### Proposal (note 197): a longer boat, and animals aboard (to discuss with the user)

- **The bigger boat**: reuse vanilla's sailboat shape (its entity shape from the game's assets) as a Magelan boat design ("launch",
  about 6–7 blocks): its hull mesh as her exterior, a grid and decks worked out by the generator as for the skiff, two
  thwarts, oars and its own mast and lugsail (stepped/unstepped like the skiff's), cargo room aft for a chest (an
  `attach` place of kind chest) and a stall amidships for one large animal. It stows on the gallows only if they're
  lengthened (the boat places are 7.6 long now): or it's towed astern on a painter instead of stowed (simpler: no lifting).
- **Animals aboard**: an **animal stall** as a modular piece (a floor piece 1 × 2 or 2 × 2, a rail round it and a hay
  rack; on the main deck or the lower deck). A tamed creature led by a rope (vanilla's lead) onto the ship and to the
  stall is **tethered in it**: mounted on a stall seat like a passenger (the same seat machinery as the crew's deck
  seats, so the engine keeps it with her, teleports and Cylinder World's seam take it along), standing animation, eating
  from the rack (hay/feed as the stall's inventory: hunger stops while fed). Untethered with the lead again. Saved with
  her (the entity's id per stall, as bunks keep sleepers); a wreck lets them go (they swim). Getting them aboard: from a
  dock or the gangway/entry port walking (they walk on her deck as players do, the deck mover for creatures), or lifted
  in by a **tackle on the main yard** (a later piece). The boat's stall works the same, so an elk can be rowed ashore.
- Order: stall piece + tethering on the ship first (testable at a dock), then the launch, then the yard tackle.

## The fifteenth client test (2026-10-04, Magelan 0.3.17, the modular carrack) and the slot plan (→ 0.3.18 and on)

Notes 162–166: stairs cut holes in the castle deck's edge and can't be put back once removed; the window lead looks like
jail bars (bigger grid: 4, then 4 again); an open door against another wall flickers; one old fixed wall left in the
forecastle, and the hearth and oven could be placeable with a chute for the smoke; railing corners flicker. Chat: the
place search is too aggressive (mid-air walls) — search only where the crosshair aims, like vanilla; building with
planks/fences/panes/stairs/slabs only with a saw in the offhand; a dye bucket must paint, not be placed; stairs need a
rethink (steeper, a 2-long well, top and bottom attachments).

**The slot plan** (agreed; memory `ship-slot-architecture`): every changeable part is a design-defined slot (deck cells,
hull panels by frame space × strake band, ports and lids, partitions, railings, stairs, hatches) with a state (filled /
cracked / empty), saved as differences from the design and drawn as baked chunk meshes rebuilt on edit. Fixed: masts,
rigging, steering and the skeleton; the pump may move along the keel; guns become placeable items (recovered from
wrecks; castle guns later); shots damage panels, deck cells and partitions down to a clean opening. Steps in order:
A (0.3.18) the fixes above + placeable hearth/oven/flue → B removable deck cells (hatches and wells as pieces) → C
cannons as items → D hull panels as slots with the unified damage. Later ships (a cog, a caravel) reuse the kit. And building becomes gameplay (step E, later): lay the skeleton (keel, frames) on a
slipway, place the panels, decks and fittings slot by slot; a ship's cost is the sum of her slots' materials; she's
watertight once her underwater panels are all in, then launched.

## Multiplayer crews, laid-up ships, logging out aboard, the watch (2026-10-03, → 0.3.11)

The user's rules (chat, 2026-10-03):
- Crew list with two roles, captains and crew; the only difference is who can add to the list. Full access for the
  crew for now (may change later). The list is a bronze plaque at the main cabin door: write names, see roles.
- A laid-up ship (all her crew offline) is locked against non-crew. She still rocks in the waves and wind, but takes
  no water, damage or wear; sails furled, anchor down. Kept loaded and solid where people are around, so nobody spawns
  a ship on her or sails through her (someone parks in port and logs off; someone else can't take the spot).
- Logging out aboard: if the ship moves meanwhile, you come back aboard at her new position. Crew who weren't aboard
  when she sailed stay at their own place. Spawn on the ship for crew only; ship destroyed (sunk, removed by an admin)
  → spawn back to the previous one (the last on land). Picking up a ship isn't possible (only boats).
- Sleeping at sea: the lashed helm holds her course (no course changes during sleep); the watch wakes sleepers near
  shore, on a Kraken attack and so on. No night-skip of the voyage.

Built (0.3.11; README "Her crew", "Laid up", "Logging out aboard", "The watch"):
- `ShipCrew.cs`, `ShipCrewDialog.cs`, `CrewPacket`; the plaque fixture (`tools/shipgen/carrack.py` PLAQUE: the great
  cabin's front, starboard of its door, at eye height; bronze with a rim and four rivets); deed launcher = first captain;
  guests: doors, gates, hatches, heads only; admins count as captains.
- Laid up: `LaidUpTick`, `FloodTick` skipped, `Damage` refused; config `LayUpWhenCrewOffline`.
- `ShipLogouts.cs`: logout record in the save game, login → aboard her (loaded or from the ships' map record, which now
  keeps Y), gone → spawn; config `RejoinShipOnLogin`. `ShipSpawns.EndPlayerOnShip` (off the list → spawn back).
- `ShipWatch.cs`: lashed-helm message, the watch (land ahead, water, aground, aback/irons, weather, hit, `RaiseAlarm`),
  the reception bell; config `ShipWatch`, `WatchAheadSeconds`. Sea Monsters 0.1.7 calls `RaiseAlarm("kraken")` when a
  Kraken starts circling (needs Magelan 0.3.11).
- Self-test `CrewWorks`.

To check in the client / on a server:
- The plaque shows and reads; signing, adding a second player by name, roles, leaving.
- A second player (not crew) can board and open doors but not steer or open chests; laid up (crew logged out) they're
  put off and can't board.
- Log out aboard, have the other player sail her away, log in: back aboard. Log out ashore: stay ashore.
- Sleep in a bunk under sail toward land: woken before she strikes.

## Built overnight (Magelan 0.3.5)

Five tasks, built by agents one after another while the user slept (2026-09-30 → 10-01). None of it seen in the client.

### 1. The crosshair catches the ship, and a spawn point on her

The user: "player crosshair does not register it, at least regular hull parts. This is important for item placement
and inspection", and "for now we just can use metal plate on the wall near the wheel and no roles are needed, and spawn
can be next to it. Gears we can add later." Built as planned in A and B below. Offline checks 300 → 336, the server
self-test 101 → 117, all pass.

- **The crosshair** (client): the game's selection ray now meets the ship's hull, decks, masts and fixtures, so the
  look-at box shows what you look at and blocks behind her hull are no longer highlighted or placed on.
  - `EntityShip.IntersectsRay`: her collision voxels raycast in her own frame, tilted with her drawn pitch and roll
    (`ShipParts.Ray`, `VoxelGrid.RaycastCell`, which also says which face the ray came in through), or a fixture's
    selection box in front of them (an empty place only while you hold what goes there, as for clicking). It sets the
    hit point and face, returns the real distance, and the part as the selection box index.
  - A client Harmony prefix and postfix on `GameMain.RayTraceForSelection`: ships are left out of vanilla's own pass
    (its chunk walk misses a big ship whose middle is far off, and it compares entities by the square of the distance
    to their centres) and tested after it against the block or entity vanilla found: the nearest hit point wins.
  - `IsInteractable` is true on clients only (arrows and creature AI on the server still ignore her).
  - The look-at box (`HudElementBlockAndEntityInfo`) is rebuilt when you look at another part of her, not only
    another entity (a postfix on its `EntityInView`). `GetInfoText`: her design (when she's named: her name is the
    title), then the part: "Hull, port side, forward, below the waterline (frames 17–18)", "Main deck", "Underside of
    the main deck", "Quarterdeck, in the poop cabin", "Mainmast", "Woodwork (the great cabin)", a fixture's or the
    item's name ("Helm", "Copper plate", "Door"). The damage state line goes under it (task 3).
  - **The part index** (`ShipPart`, `ShipPartMap` in `src/Ship/ShipParts.cs`): kind in the top byte; the hull: side
    (port/starboard), station (the frame space along the keel: 0 between her first two frames, counted from the stern,
    the frames every 1.5 from the design's new `frames`), band (whole blocks up from the keel); a deck (the design's
    new `decks`) with its station and top/underside; a mast (the design's new `masts`); woodwork (structure) like the
    hull; a fixture by its index. A hull part is the planking between two frames by a block of height: task 3's panels
    map one to one (or the band can be split). Which cells are hull: within half a block of her outside to either
    side, the bottom or the ends, where her side (or stern, bow) runs on up from below the main deck without stepping
    inboard (so a stair, a gallows post or the poop cabin's front bulkhead over the rail is woodwork). Zones for the
    text come from the index alone: fifths of her length (at the stern, aft, amidships, forward, at the bow), below
    the waterline / topsides / bulwark / castle side. `dotnet run --project tests/MagelanTests -c Release -- parts`
    prints what the crosshair meets from a few places aboard and round her.
  - What vanilla does with a selected ship, and what was done about it:
    - left-click: vanilla would swing at her like at a creature and send an attack packet (the server drops it). Now a
      left-click on a ship is consumed and does nothing (`OnInWorldAction`).
    - right-click that nothing of ours takes: vanilla first offers it to the held item (eating and drinking work
      looking at the deck), then calls `OnInteract` (now an empty override) and sends an interact packet the server
      drops or passes to `OnInteract`. Harmless.
    - held items with the ship as the entity selection, with the server's anti-abuse on: vanilla's reach check measured
      to her 6 × 4 box round her middle, so eating while looking at her far end would be refused. A server patch on
      `ServerPlayer.isInInteractionRangeOf` measures to her hull.
    - vanilla's red "in reach" crosshair and the attack's reach use the selection box: on clients it's now a speck
      far below her keel (the ray goes by her voxels).
    - the block highlight and placing behind the hull stop by themselves; the old "clicks through the deck" guard
      stays as a fallback.
    - aboard (deck seats, the helm): looking at the deck shows "Main deck"; the helm's wheel is the helm fixture.
    - the rowing boat (`EntityRowBoat`, vanilla's boat) is left to vanilla; a ship's hull nearer than her wins.
    - an animal trap held on her says she's too wild to catch; the creative entity-mover tool can grab her.
- **A spawn point on the ship** (server, config `ShipSpawn`, `src/Ship/ShipSpawns.cs`):
  - **The plate:** an attach place (kind "plate") on the poop cabin's front bulkhead, port of its door, at eye
    height, facing the wheel 3 blocks forward (`carrack.py` `PLATE`; `docs/views/carrack-helm-plate.png`,
    `carrack-plate.png`). Any vanilla metal plate (`game:metalplate-*`) goes there with a right-click; it's drawn
    with the item's own mesh stood up on the wall. Shift + right-click takes it back.
  - **Binding:** right-click the plate with an empty hand: your spawn is bound to her; again: unbound. Said in chat
    each time. Anyone may. The spawn spot is on the quarterdeck right in front of the plate (`spawn` on the fixture).
  - **Mechanics:** the bindings (her entity id, the plate, and the spawn you had before, read from vanilla's player
    data by reflection) are kept in the save game (`magelanShipSpawns`). Vanilla's spawn position
    (`SetSpawnPosition`, unlimited uses) is kept at the spot's block, at her still-water level, checked every 2 s and
    moved when she's more than 2 blocks off (so it follows her across Cylinder World's seam too). After vanilla's
    respawn teleport (which loads her chunks) the player is put aboard at the spot once they're alive and she has
    loaded (tried every 0.25 s for 20 s). If she doesn't turn up, their old spawn comes back and they're taken there.
    A spawn someone sets elsewhere since (a temporal gear) ends the binding.
  - **Ending:** her gone for good (removed, picked up, later sunk: any despawn but a chunk unloading) or the plate
    taken down: everyone bound to her gets their old spawn back, at once if online, else when they next join; said in
    chat. `ShipSpawn` off: the plate binds nothing, and bindings end as players join.
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - Taking the plate down ends every spawn bound to her (anyone may take it down; no roles yet).
  - The plate's place: the poop cabin's front, port of the door, facing the wheel (starboard has the poop stair).
  - A left-click on a ship does nothing (no swing).
  - When she isn't there after a respawn (20 s), the old spawn comes back and the player is taken there.
  - A spawn set elsewhere (a temporal gear) while bound ends the binding instead of being overwritten.
  - The text: "Woodwork" for her own structure that isn't hull, deck or mast; frame numbers counted from the stern.

### 2. The ship's shadow

The user: "Ship shadow is missing." Why: `ShipRenderer` was registered for the Opaque stage only, and the engine puts
into its shadow maps only chunks, entity renderers and renderers registered for the shadow stages; the ships are
entities with the "Dummy" renderer. Offline checks 336 → 346, the server self-test 117 → 121, all pass. The engine
facts are in `docs/engine-notes.md`, "Shadows (sun shadow maps)". The user's `clientsettings.json` has
`shadowMapQuality` 2 (a far and a near map: the near one about 33 blocks round you) and `allowSSBOs` true on an
OpenGL 4.3 card, so the shader that runs is the NoSSBOs build.

- **Casting** (client, `ShipRenderer.RenderShadows`, rules in `src/Client/ShipShadows.cs`): the renderer is also
  registered for `ShadowFar` and `ShadowNear` and draws every ship within the shadow map's box into it.
  - Her pose as drawn: the same model matrix as the normal pass (`SetModel`: place with the heave, heading, pitch,
    roll; relative to your seat when you're aboard). The moving parts are worked out again in the shadow pass (it
    comes before the normal pass), the sails furled and braced as drawn (`SailPlace`), the doors as they stand
    (`PiecePlace`), the boats on the gallows as they lie.
  - The engine's own shadow shader for chunks and held items (`chunkshadowmap`, or its NoSSBOs build when chunks come
    from SSBOs), as vanilla's helve hammer does: `mvpMatrix` = the map's projection × the sun's view × her model
    matrix, `origin` 0, the texture as `tex2d`. It drops see-through texels, so rope and cut-outs cast their shape.
  - What goes in: both maps get the decks, castles and bulwarks (the Exterior shape), her planking outside, the
    bottom (tarred or clad), the masts and standing rigging, the sails, the yards, rudder, wheel, gates, the hatch in
    the waist, the anchor, the doors and the boats on the gallows. The near map also gets the braces, sheets and the
    anchor cable, the figurehead and the small things on deck (the two mast lanterns, the plate). Never: the Interior
    shape and skin group, the tiller and its ropes, the hold hatch, anything in a cabin or the hold, glass, the name.
  - Culled: a sphere round her (her bounds, plus the anchor's travel: radius 40.5 on the carrack) against the engine's
    shadow culler, and the distance she's drawn at.
  - Her image is pushed away from the sun, 0.1 blocks in the near map and 0.3 in the far one, against stripes on her
    sunlit hull; that moves only the stored depth, not where her shadow falls.
  - Defensive: shadows off in the graphics settings means the stages don't run; the shader is checked (there, not
    disposed, has `mvpMatrix` and `tex2d`); the shader in use before is given back with its matrix reset; an
    exception in the shadow pass is logged once and ship shadows stay off for the session.
  - A switch for each player: `ShipShadows` in `magelan-client.json`, `.magelan shadows on|off`.
- **Receiving:** nothing to change. She's drawn with the standard shader, whose `Use()` binds the shadow maps, so the
  world's shadows already fell on her; now her sails, castles and rigging shade her decks too. Inside she's darker by
  up to about a fifth by day (her own decks shade the cabins; the interior's warm block light, which the shader adds
  back in shadow, keeps the rest).
- **The rowing boat** (`EntityRowBoat`) casts its shadow already: vanilla's `EntityShapeRenderer` puts every shaped
  entity into the maps (`SystemRenderEntities.OnRenderFrameShadows`, `Shadowmapentityanimated`), and on the gallows
  it's one of the ship's pieces.
- **Refactor, same behaviour:** the normal pass's model matrix, sail and door placement, and the stowed boats' place
  and parts went into small helpers the shadow pass shares; `ShipDefinition.Indoors` replaces the two room lookups for
  the pieces' interior light.
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - The far map (past about 30 blocks) leaves out the moving ropes, the figurehead and the small things on deck.
  - The depth push: 0.1 (near) / 0.3 (far) blocks. More if her hull shows stripes in the sun, less if her shadow
    seems detached where something stands on her deck.
  - The cabins get a little darker by day now that her decks shade them.
  - Ship shadows on by default for everyone; `.magelan shadows off` per player.

### 3a. Damage, leaks, pumps, repairs and sinking (the mechanics)

The plan below, steps 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8, and maintenance wear. The user: damage shows gradually (the textures and the
look-at text first, real holes at the end); damage and sinking first (grounding, then cannon damage). This is the
mechanics only: the damage textures, the water drawn inside her (Sea's flood level) and real holes are task 3b. Offline
checks 346 → 376, the server self-test 121 → 145, all pass. Config `Damage` (master switch), `GroundingDamage`,
`Leaks`, `Wear` (factors).

- **Her parts** (`src/Ship/ShipDamage.cs`, `DamageLayout`, built per design as `ShipDefinition.DamageParts`):
  - a hull panel per crosshair hull part (task 1's `ShipPart.Hull(side, station, band)`): the planking of one side
    between two frames by a band a block high; 476 on the carrack, 172 below the waterline. Each has its outer face (area,
    middle, outward normal) and that face's box. The lowest band takes in the keel and the flat floor (up to 7 m²).
  - the decks between two frames (92), the rudder (a box the generator writes), each mast, each sail, each mast's rigging
    (new `ShipPartKind`s Rudder, Sail, Rigging: the crosshair doesn't meet them; they're read at the mast and the helm).
  - each part has a hurt (0..1) and the hull panels a seam wear (0..1). The ladder (the worst step reached): sound, worn
    (seams ≥ 0.3), scraped (≥ 0.05), cracked (≥ 0.35), leaking (seams ≥ 0.7), holed (≥ 0.7); sails torn 0.3 / shredded
    0.75; masts sprung 0.4 / broken 0.85; rigging frayed 0.3 / parted 0.75; the rudder damaged 0.3 / wrecked 0.75.
  - the numbers are the ship JSON's `damage` section (`DamageDef`): the generator writes the carrack's toughness, her
    rudder's box and two at the pump's brake; every number has a default in C#.
  - server: floats, saved with her by part code (`Attributes["magelanDmgF"]`, so a changed design keeps what still
    matches); clients: two bytes a part (hurt, wear) in the watched attribute `magelanDmg`, sent only when a byte
    changes; the water, sinkage, list, trim, stage, pumpers and fouling with her sailing state (`magelan`).
- **The damage API** (server, `src/Ship/ShipFlooding.cs`):
  - `EntityShip.Damage(localPoint, localDir, energy kJ, radius, cause, kinds)`: shared among the parts within the radius,
    the nearest most ((1 − d/r)², d to a part's box); each takes at most what it has left (toughness × (1 − hurt)); the
    planking's seams start (wear + 0.8 × the hurt). Toughness: a hull panel 80 kJ, a deck 60, the rudder 60, a mast 400,
    a sail 25, rigging 80.
  - **Penetration** (for cannon balls): a hit on a spot of radius ≤ 0.5 (`PierceRadius`) is a shot. What the parts it
    holed couldn't take goes on along localDir through her voxels to the next thing in its way (the part map's own
    classification of the cell it meets: the far side, a deck, a mast), woodwork inside takes 30 kJ, until it's spent or
    out of her (`DamageResult`: the hits, `Pierced`, `Exit`, `Remaining`). Offline: 400 kJ holes the side and hits
    the mainmast's foot; 20 kJ stays in the planking.
  - `DamagePart(index, amount)`, `DamagePartFor(ShipPart)`, `PartByName`, `PartName`, `StateOfPart`, `RepairAll`, `Flood`.
- **Grounding:** `CheckPose` now says which hull samples touch. A move that `PoseToward` refuses: each place she newly
  touches takes ½·m·v² × 0.06 (v that point's own speed, her way and her turning; m her displacement and the water in
  her), spread over 1.25 blocks, hull and rudder only; a broad touch shares it. About 1 b/s scrapes, 2.5 cracks, 4
  holes (offline). At most once in 0.4 s: driven on with sails set she bumps every few ticks and keeps wearing. Lying on
  the ground in a seaway she grinds where she touched lately and where her bottom would be in the troughs (3 kJ a
  second per block of wave height). The self-test's run into the wall at 5.3 b/s holed her bow (10 panels, nothing
  aft). A flooded ship floats deeper, so she grounds sooner (`FloatY`).
- **Ship on ship** (`HitsShip` now says which ship and where): both take ½·μ·v² × 0.06 where they meet (μ the reduced
  mass, v how fast they closed there).
- **Leaks:** a panel's effective hole: worn seams 5·10⁻⁶ m², cracked 3·10⁻⁴, leaking seams 8·10⁻⁴, holed 0.03 up to
  0.2 stove in. Flow = 2.66 × area × √(head outside − head inside) (out again when the water inside stands higher).
  The head outside is Sea's shared waves at the panel with her true pitch, roll, heel and list (the server keeps her
  level for walking, not for this), so panels near the waterline leak as she rolls and heels. At 2 deep: worn 0.02 l/s,
  cracked 1.1, leaking 3.0, holed 113 up to 750. One space (no bulkheads).
- **What the water does:**
  - she floats deeper: her hull's section layer by layer (the dry mask) gives how deep a weight of water puts her. The
    carrack displaces 905 t; her reserve buoyancy (waterline to the main deck's edge) is 642 t; the hold holds 1133 t.
    Pos.Y is the still-water level − the sinkage + the heave, on the server and on clients (clients ease the synced
    sinkage).
  - the water runs to the low side: its free surface (i/∇) eats her metacentric height (1.26, from her roll period); she
    lists toward her heel, or the side it came in: 2° at 100 t, 12° at 300 t, lolling past that, at most 35°. Her roll is
    slower and more damped (only the roll: the server's heave and the clients' still agree).
  - she's slower and less handy: speed and turning ÷ (1 + 2 × water / displacement); 300 t: 0.6.
  - no trim afloat; going down she's down by the head.
- **Downflooding:** 43 places: along the waist both sides, the deck's edge (through the scuppers, a fifth as freely)
  and the rail, a point each frame space; the main deck's hatch and the gates while they stand open. Once one is under
  the local wave surface the sea pours in (1.7 × length × depth^1.5, half of it below): she's foundering.
- **Sinking** (`SinkStage`: afloat → foundering → sinking → sunk):
  - foundering: the sea coming in over her side, or 80% of her reserve buoyancy gone; a warning to everyone aboard and
    on the helm's readout. Back to afloat after 10 s without either.
  - her reserve gone, she settles (0.3 blocks/s) till her deck's edge is at the water, then goes down: everyone aboard
    is unmounted into the water (and not put back aboard at a relog); the boats on her gallows are spawned beside her on
    the water as the entities of their items (a rowing boat, a raft); spawns bound to her end ("sunk"); Sea's dry
    cut-out, her dry places and the keep-out for swimmers stop; boarding and every fixture but her chests and cargo are
    refused. She goes down faster and faster (up to 1.5 blocks/s), over to 30° and 6° by the head, the waves letting go
    of her.
  - on the bottom (her hull samples meet it; or the world's floor, or sea that isn't loaded): `ShipWreck.OnSunk(ship)`,
    the hook for task 5. Until then she lies there frozen: no motion, sailing or waves, her storages kept; a diver can
    open her chests and cargo.
  - **shallow water:** she settles onto the bottom first (`OnBottom`, aground), sits upright on her flat floor, and
    counts as sunk only if her waist's deck edge is then under the still water. Otherwise she stays foundered there;
    pumped out and mended she floats off. (The self-test pool, 5 deep, is this case.)
- **Pumps:** the bilge pump is a fixture now (`pump`, `carrack.py`), its brake a moving part (`pump-brake`, source
  `pump:pump`, rocking ±12° while anyone pumps; `docs/views/carrack-pump.png`, `-pump-stroke.png`). Held right-click:
  the client sends a stroke a second, 10 litres a stroke (a vanilla bucket), two at the brake at most, pumps add up.
  Two hold 4 leaking and 3 cracked panels (17 l/s) but not a hole (156 l/s). Vanilla's `sounds/block/pump`.
- **Repairs** (`ShipRepairs`; held right-click 4 s on the same part, then the server checks and takes the items, the
  hand first, then the hotbar and backpack; nothing in creative). What's held picks the job:
  - planking (hull, decks): scraped 1 plank; cracked 2 planks + 1 nails and strips; holed 4 + 2 (`game:plank-*`,
    `game:metalnailsandstrips-*`)
  - seams: worn 1 flax fibres + 1 resin; leaking 2 + 1 (`game:flaxfibers`, `game:resin`)
  - a sail (at its mast): torn 1 `game:sail` or 4 `game:cloth-*` or 4 `game:linen-*`; shredded 2 sails
  - rigging (at its mast): frayed 2 `game:rope`, parted 4
  - a sprung mast: fished with 4 planks + 2 rope (left at hurt 0.3: not as good as new); a broken one needs a port
  - the rudder (at the helm): damaged 2 planks + 1 nails; wrecked 4 + 2
  - below the water: from inside her (aboard), or a diver outside with his eyes under water.
- **Wear** (game time while she's loaded and afloat, at most an hour at a time): seams wear out in 60 days (worn at
  18, leaking at 42), more in a seaway, each panel at its own rate (0.7..1.3); an unsheathed bottom fouls in 90 days and
  makes her up to 15% slower (sheathing keeps it clean; nailing it on cleans it); sails set in winds over 0.5 tear
  (0.3 an hour at full sail × (wind − 0.5)²). Torn sails, a sprung mast or parted rigging drive her less; a damaged
  rudder answers less. Careening and dry docks later.
- **Inspection and HUD** (client, unverified): the look-at box under the part's name: its state, roughly how many litres
  a second it lets in (in still water), what mends it; at a mast also its hurt sails and rigging, at the helm the
  rudder, at the pump the water. Mending and pumping show their progress above the hotbar. The helm's readout: the
  water ("Water in the hold: 12.0 t (0.8 deep), rising (4 l/s) · 2 at the pumps"), the foundering warning, hurt sails,
  masts, rigging and rudder (the box is 96 high now, for four lines). Everyone else aboard gets the foundering warning.
- **Commands** (creative or admins): `/magelan damage look [energy]` (a shot where you look; at a mast or the helm that
  part, half wrecked), `part <name> [0..1]` (hull-p-12-2, hull-s-12-2, deck-main-10, rudder, mast-main,
  sail-main-course, rigging-main), `at <x> <y> <z> [energy] [radius]`, `age <days>`, `list`; `/magelan repairall`,
  `/magelan flood <tonnes>`; `/magelan info` has a damage line. Offline: `dotnet run -c Release -- damage` prints her
  parts, hydrostatics and a table of what water does to her.
- **Hooks for the next tasks:**
  - 3b (visuals): `ShipDefinition.DamageParts` (`Parts[i]`: Kind, Part, Min/Max (the outer face's box), Center, Normal,
    Area; `Find(ShipPart)`, `PartAt(point, kinds)` to give each hull quad its panel); `EntityShip.PartDamage` (Hurt[],
    Wear[]), `StateOfPart(i)`, `DamageVersion` (bumped on every change: rebuild); `Water`, `FloodLevel` (ship-local y,
    level in her frame), `Flooded`, `Stage`/`GoneDown`, `Sinkage`, `ListAngle`, `Trim`. `ShipDrySpaces.IsDry` already
    counts below `FloodLevel` as wet and a gone-down ship as not dry; `ShipRenderer.AddWaterClips` (skips a gone-down
    ship) is where Sea's flood level per clip goes.
  - 4 (cannons): `EntityShip.RayHit(origin, dir, range, null, out ShipRayHit hit)` (world ray → `hit.Local`),
    `WorldDirToLocal`, then `EntityShip.Damage(hit.Local, localDir, energy, radius ≤ 0.5, "cannon")` →
    `DamageResult` (`Hits`, `Pierced`, `Exit` ship-local, `Remaining` kJ for what flies on); `DamageKinds` to limit it
    (chain shot: Sail | Rigging); pure `ShipDamage.Apply` for tests.
  - 5 (wrecks): `ShipWreck.OnSunk(ship)` is called once when she rests on the bottom; `ShipWreck.Sunk` (an
    `Action<EntityShip>`) to hang the wreck on. She still has her `Storages`, `Attached` (chests, cargo, the plate),
    `PartDamage` (what's holed), `Pos`, `ListAngle`, `Trim`. Note: `Die(Removed)` drops her storages and attached items
    at her middle (`OnEntityDespawn`): move them into the wreck first. `ReleasedBoats` has the boats that floated free.
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - the numbers: toughness, the ladder's steps, the leak areas (a fresh hole ~110 l/s at 2 deep: an hour or so to sink
    her alone; several holes minutes), the pump (10 l/s, two at a brake), 60 days for seams, 90 for fouling, sails in a
    gale
  - a panel is a band a block high (the lowest band takes in the keel and flat floor)
  - a mast's sails and rigging are read and mended at the mast; the rudder at the helm
  - a broken mast needs a port (not mended at sea yet); a fished mast isn't as good as new
  - shallow water: sunk only when her waist's deck edge is under water; else foundered on the bottom, and salvageable
  - going down she's always down by the head (6°) and lists to the side the water came in (30°)
  - water that comes over her side isn't kept on deck (half of it goes below); decks don't leak
  - the list: toward her heel, else the side the water came in, else starboard; on the bottom she sits upright
  - a sunk ship's hull no longer keeps swimmers out (divers go in to her chests); only chests and cargo can be opened
  - creative players mend without items
  - flooding doesn't change her heave (so the server's and the clients' heave stay the same)
  - the SeaMotion setting also scales her list and trim on that player's screen

### 3b. Damage and flooding drawn (the textures, the water in her hold, real holes)

The plan's steps 7, 5 and 10 below. The user: damage shows gradually, first in the textures and the look-at text (3a),
at the end real holes, "smooth edges so not to overcomplicate". All client drawing; none of it seen in the client.
Offline checks 376 → 389, the server self-test 145 → 148; Sea 0.3.3: its offline tests 73 → 77, the GPU shader check
24 → 26 (on the user's RX 6600), its self-test 19, all pass. No new config.

- **The planking by panel** (`HullDamageSkin`, `src/Ship/ShipDamageSkin.cs`, pure; the client builds it off the main
  thread with the mesh):
  - the generator marks the hull's planking in the mesh file: `hullOut` (outside: the strakes, transom, castle breaks,
    and the wales and rail caps over them) and `hullIn` (the ceiling), per group as index ranges, and `hullThickness`
    (`planking.py`, `shipgen.py`; carrack.py and boat.py regenerated: only the two mesh files changed).
  - each marked quad belongs to the hull panel its middle is in (the crosshair's rule: side, frame space, band; the
    ceiling's: the panel outside it): 16445 of the carrack's 16619, 474 of her 476 panels have planking outside.
  - chunks: one side, three frame spaces, per mesh group (exterior, bottom, bottomclad, interior): 76 on the carrack, 8
    on the launch. A change rebuilds only the chunks whose panels changed look, or that a hole reaches.
- **Overlays** (backlog step 7): each panel's state picks an overlay composited in the atlas on its quads' own texture
  (tarred oak, copper, plain oak inside): `base++0~magelan:block/dmg-<state>-a|b` (two of each, so neighbouring strakes
  differ; `tools/shipgen/textures.py`, `docs/views/damage-textures.png`):
  - worn: subtle, the pitch greying in faint streaks and pale oakum showing in the seams
  - scraped: long gouges along the planks down to lighter bare oak, a thin shadow under each
  - cracked: dark splits along the grain, the split's lip lifted pale (so they show on tar)
  - leaking: oakum working out of the seams in pale strands, dark wet runs under them; inside, dark wet stains
  - holed: in every plank a dark break with pale splinters along the grain (round the real hole)
  - the ceiling shows only cracks, wet and holes (not worn or scraped).
- **Real holes** (step 10), cut on the client when a chunk is built:
  - an ellipse on the panel's outer face, along the planks, cut square to it through the planking outside and the
    ceiling (and a wale or rail cap over it): from 0.66 × 0.44 just holed to 1.2 × 0.8 stove in (four steps), off the
    panel's middle by a fixed pick, well inside the panel.
  - the edge is traced on a 0.07 grid over each quad the hole reaches (marching squares, interpolated), so it's smooth;
    quads all inside are dropped. Offline the cut area is the ellipse's (0.542 m² for 0.545), nothing is left inside it.
  - its reveal: 28 short quads of raw oak (`planks/oak1`) round the edge, from the planking's outside to the ceiling (or
    its thickness in), with the topsides' planking (else the bottom's, tarred and clad).
  - **the collision grid stays whole**: you can't climb or swim through a hole (not trivial: the dry mask, the walking
    and the hull keep-out all rest on it).
- **On the client** (`ShipRenderer`): every half second at most, when her `DamageVersion` changed, each panel's look is
  worked out again; the chunks that changed are queued and built six a frame into her own meshes. The design's hull
  groups are split once (what isn't planking, and each chunk as sound) the first time one of her ships is damaged; a
  ship with nothing to show draws the design's meshes as before. The shadow pass draws the same meshes (light through a
  hole). The overlay textures go into the atlas the first time they're asked for. An exception there is logged once and
  damage isn't drawn for the session.
- **Sails** (torn, shredded): a square sail's shape is tesselated again with some cloth pieces on a linen texture with
  holes cut out (`++8~` OverlayCutout, `sail-hole-*`, a darker frayed edge `sail-fray-*`): torn about a fifth of the
  pieces, shredded over half with big holes and one in eight gone; the lateen's quads the same. Drawn alpha-tested; the
  shadow shader drops the holes too. The holes are mirrored left to right: a sail piece is a thin box whose two faces show
  the texture mirrored, so a hole must be on both.
- **The water in her hold** (step 5; `ShipWater`, `src/Ship/ShipWater.cs`, pure):
  - a surface level in the world while she rolls and pitches, through her middle (`FloodX`, her waterline's middle) at
    her flood level, eased as it rises and falls; out to the inside of her hull at that height (the generator's exact
    outline, `waterClip.dryOutline`, less half the planking's thickness), every half block along her, found closely at
    her ends; points kept inside her where her section isn't round (by the sternpost, rolled hard). Offline: 20 to 1000 t,
    pitched and rolled up to 25°, level to 1e-6, inside her, its edges on her planking.
  - drawn after the glass, blended, from both sides, in the interior's light: vanilla's `block/liquid/saltwater` as its
    own repeating texture, tinted murky (0.36, 0.5, 0.52, 0.85), drifting slowly; its middle bobs with vanilla's liquid
    warp (vertex wind mode 12, with "waving stuff" on), its edges stay on the planking. Sloshing: it tilts a little (at
    most 0.06) toward the side she rolls to, lagging her roll. One mesh per flooded ship, updated every frame.
- **Sea 0.3.3** (the dry cut-out's flood level per clip): `WaterClipBox.Flooded` and `FloodY` (world y, level); the
  shader skips a dry clip where the water pixel is under it (`seaClipB.y`, unused before: the flood's height over the
  clip's water y), before anything else, so it's right whatever the hull's pitch and roll (SlopeAlong / SlopeAcross) and
  with the detailed outlines and the raked transom. The C# twin (`InDryHull`) compares the same, in single precision.
  Offline and on the GPU: flooded level and flooded pitched, rolled, detailed and raked. Magelan's dependency: sea 0.3.3.
- **Magelan tells Sea** (`ShipWater.Clip`, from `ShipRenderer.AddWaterClips`): her flood's level as it's drawn, 0.05
  under it (so the two surfaces don't flicker where they meet).
- **Wet under it:** `ShipDrySpaces.IsDry` now tests the water's level in the world (`EntityShip.FloodWorldY`: her flood
  level through her middle, with her drawn pitch and roll; on the server she's level, as before), the same as it's drawn.
  `FloodLevel` is worked out again only when the water changes (it's asked every tick). Sea's postfixes (checked): the
  camera's eyes in water and breathing only overrule vanilla where the world has water at the eyes, and are skipped
  where it's not dry, so under the water in her hold vanilla's under-water view and drowning stand; splashes are
  skipped only where it's dry (players aboard are mounted, so vanilla never splashes them anyway).
- **Views** (`docs/views/`): `dotnet run --project tests/MagelanTests -c Release -- damage-views` writes her hull groups as
  the client builds them (a row of panels amidships, port side: sound, worn, scraped, cracked, leaking, holed, stove in)
  and `python tools/shipgen/texview.py damage` renders them with the game's textures: `carrack-damage-topsides`,
  `-bottom`, `-clad`, `-hold`, `-hole`, `-hole-inside`, and the water in her hold, rolled 10° and down 2° by the head:
  `carrack-flooded-hold`, `-cutaway`.
- **Visual oddities to expect** (not seen):
  - a hole below the waterline before the hold has flooded: the dry cut hides the sea inside her, so through the hole you
    see into the dry hold; no water pours in (it's only the numbers). Once there's water in her it's drawn, and under it
    Sea shows the sea: where the waves inside her outline dip below the water drawn, a second water surface shows under
    it through the translucent one.
  - the wales stand 0.1 proud of the hull: where a hole cuts one, its cut edge isn't closed (a thin gap).
  - where a cut quad meets its uncut neighbour there are T-junctions: maybe a sparkle of a pixel along them.
  - water in her hold higher than the sea outside (poured in over her side): drawn, but no under-water view or drowning
    there (the world has no water at the eyes).
  - the drawn water's level is eased and sloshes; the wet level (breath, view) isn't: up to about 0.1 apart.
- **Hooks for the next tasks:**
  - 4 (cannons): a shot through her side (`EntityShip.Damage` with a radius ≤ 0.5) holes panels as before; the hole is
    drawn once the hurt byte reaches the client (≤ 0.5 s later), sized by how stove in the panel is (`HullLook.HoleStep`).
    Nothing to call. A muzzle flash or smoke isn't there. The deck parts' damage isn't drawn yet (decks are voxel shapes),
    so a ball through a deck shows nothing there. `HullDamageSkin.HoleOf(panel, step)` gives where a hole is drawn, if
    splinters or smoke should come out of it.
  - 5 (wrecks): a gone-down ship draws no water in her hold and no dry cut (Sea sees the sea in her, as before); her
    holes and overlays stay drawn from her `PartDamage` while she lies on the bottom. A wreck built from world blocks
    won't have these meshes: the wreck's generator can read `PartDamage` / `HullLook.Of` to break the planking where she
    was holed.
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - the overlays' look (subtle worn; scraped bare oak; cracks with a pale lip; oakum out of leaking seams outside, wet
    stains inside; splintered breaks for holed), two of each; the ceiling shows only cracks, wet and holes
  - the hole: an ellipse along the planks, 0.66 × 0.44 to 1.2 × 0.8 as she's stove in, raw oak reveal; the collision
    stays whole
  - torn sails: a fifth of the pieces holed, shredded over half with some pieces gone; the holes mirrored (see above)
  - the water in her hold: saltwater tinted murky and see-through, bobbing in the middle with "waving stuff", sloshing a
    little; Sea shows the sea under it (0.05 under)
  - damage is looked at every half second and built six chunks a frame

### 4. Cannons

The user: "Cannons - for now just model and fire on click or button - later loading etc. But recoil animation would be
cool. So we can test sinking the ship." Item 16's waist guns; the long waist was chosen partly for them. Offline checks
389 → 406 (the 17 cannon checks alone with `-- cannons`), the server self-test 148 → 161, all pass. Config
`Cannons` (on), `CannonsBreakBlocks` (off). None of it seen in the client.

- **The gun** (`tools/shipgen/gun.py`; `docs/views/carrack-gun-close.png`, `-gun-recoil`, `-guns-out`, `-guns-deck`,
  `-guns-port`, `-guns-port-recoil`): a cast bronze muzzle-loader of about 1500–1520 after the Mary Rose's culverins and
  demi-culverins: a 2.75-block barrel (the real ones ~3 m, 3.75 at the carrack's scale: too long for her beam), a 0.14
  bore, a lathe profile with a base ring, two reinforces with their rings, a chase girdle, a muzzle swell, the bore dark
  inside, trunnions with rimbases, a cascabel; on a truck carriage: stepped cheeks with iron cap squares, a transom, a
  bed and a quoin, two axletrees and four solid trucks; the breeching from two ring bolts beside the port round the
  cascabel. Vanilla textures: `metal/plate/tinbronze`, debarked oak, oak planks, iron plate.
- **Placement** (`carrack.py`, `guns`): three a side at x 2.875, 4.375 and 7.375, each between two frames: forward of the
  main shrouds' channels and the gallows' after posts, either side of the entry ports (clear of the gates swung open),
  aft of the forecastle's break; run back they clear the companionway, the waist hatch and the capstan's cable. Ports
  0.75 wide, from 0.25 to 0.75 over the deck (the rail cap over them), cut through the planking outside and in and the
  collision's shell, lined with oak; the scuppers under them are gone. The muzzles stand a quarter block out of her side.
  Each gun is a fixture (`gun`: `muzzle`, `out`, `elevation` 1°, `material` "bronze", `bore`, `shot` 4.1 kg, `box`: solid
  where it stands run out) and a moving part of its own. No port lids and no swivel guns yet. The sea coming in over
  her side still goes by 3a's places along the rail (none is in a port): an open port doesn't let it in sooner yet (with
  lids it could, as it did the Mary Rose).
- **Firing** (server, `EntityShip.FireGun`, `src/Ship/ShipGuns.cs`): right-click a gun, or `/magelan fire
  [gun|port|starboard|all] [elevation]`. Ready 8 s after it fired (the look-at box and the hint say "ready" or "reloading
  (5 s)"); the loading steps will go in `GunState` (commented there). The muzzle and the direction come from the fixture
  through her frame as she really lies (her yaw, and the waves' pitch and roll with her heel, list and trim: the
  server's own true pose, as the leaks use), so a roll lays a broadside down or up; her own way is added to the shot's.
- **The shot** (`Ballistics` pure in `src/Ship/Cannons.cs`, `CannonShots` on the server; numbers in `CannonDef`, a design
  may override them in `cannon`): a 9-lb iron ball at 60 b/s (a fifth of a real gun's, to be seen), real gravity, a v²
  drag (0.0006 a block), fixed 1/60 s steps everywhere. Range over the water from her waist: level 42, at her 1° 49, 10°
  131, 20° 221, 45° 316 blocks. Energy ½·m·v² × 20 (a real ~270 m/s shot's): 148 kJ at the muzzle.
  - **Ships:** each step's stretch against every ship's collision as she really lies; not the ship that fired it in its
    first 6 blocks. `EntityShip.Damage(point, dir, energy, 0.12, "cannon")`: the plank it strikes takes up to its 80 kJ
    (holed); if it went through, it strikes whoever's in its way inside her, and if it came out (`Exit`) flies on from
    there with what's left. Close up a ball holes her side and cracks or holes the far side; it doesn't come out of a
    carrack (148 < 2 × 80).
  - **Creatures and players:** vanilla blunt damage, 0.5 HP a kJ (a close hit ~70), `CauseEntity` the one who fired
    (vanilla refuses it for players without PvP), the attack privileges checked like vanilla's arrows; it goes on 25 kJ
    poorer.
  - **Water:** the column's top plus Sea's waves. A splash (vanilla's water splash particles, spray, the large splash
    sound). Under 6° it skips (from a ship's waist a level shot comes down at about 7°, so rarely); else it goes in at
    0.9 of its speed and the water's drag (0.3 a block) leaves under half its energy after a block and spends it within
    about 7; then it sinks. So a shot into her side just under the water still holes her.
  - **Blocks:** it stops in a puff of the block's own dust. With `CannonsBreakBlocks` on, up to three soft blocks (no
    stone, ore, metal, brick; resistance 4.5 at most; 15 kJ a point of resistance) break and drop, where the one who
    fired may build (land claims; a shot fired by no one breaks none in a claim).
  - **Sinking her:** at 65 kJ a shot (through half a block to a block of water), 10 shots below her waterline sink her in
    3.7 minutes, 20 in 1.9 (offline, `-- cannons` prints the table; at 88 kJ, stove in, 1.7 and 0.9).
- **Effects:** the boom (vanilla's `mediumexplosion`, heard 256 blocks off), a spurt of flame, two puffs of white-grey
  smoke blown out along the barrel that grow to a few blocks and drift off with Sea's wind (0.8 of its speed), fading
  over 5–7 s; splinters and dust where it strikes a ship; all vanilla particles from the server. On clients: a point light
  at the muzzle for a seventh of a second, and the gun running back (below).
- **The recoil** (`PartMotion.Recoil`, source `fire:<gun>`): the gun slides 1.1 blocks inboard in 0.18 s (easing out),
  stands 0.6 s with the breeching taut, and is run out over 3.5 s; its trucks turn as it rolls (their parts ride on the
  gun: `MovingPartDef.Parent`, new, applied in `ShipRenderer.ComputeParts`, so the shadow pass has it too); the breeching
  stretches (follow ropes). Clients start it when the shot's packet comes (`GunFiredAt`); one who joins mid-recoil sees
  it run out.
- **Clients** (`src/Client/CannonClient.cs`, `ShotPacket`: fired, going on from here, stopped; to players within 320
  blocks): the ball flown in the same steps between packets, drawn with the design's "shot" mesh (a cast iron ball,
  `game:block/metal/tarnished/iron`) by `ShipRenderer.DrawShots`. A click fires once (not again while right-click is held,
  nor while the client knows it's reloading).
- **Commands:** `/magelan fire` (above); `/magelan target [distance] [port|starboard]`: a carrack abeam of your ship
  heading the same way (60 blocks off by default: her level shot meets the target's waterline there; else the side you
  look to), or in front of you with no ship.
- **The gun as a block** (for the wreck): `magelan:gun-bronze-<north|east|south|west>` (`blocktypes/gun.json`, the shape
  `shapes/block/gun-bronze.json` written by `gun.py`: the same gun and carriage in 61 boxes, octagons for the round
  parts), placed facing the way you look, drops itself, in the creative inventory. No use yet.
- **For the wreck (task 5):** every gun is a fixture of type "gun" with `Material` "bronze" (`def.Fixtures.Where(f =>
  f.Type == "gun")`), its place (`Pos`, on the deck under its trunnions) and the way it points (`Out`, ship-local). A
  wreck can put a `magelan:gun-bronze-<side>` block on the seabed (or in the wreck's blocks) for each: `<side>` the
  compass side its muzzle faces (Out turned by her yaw; the block's shape points north at "north"), so a diver finds it
  and breaks it to take it home. Iron guns (to come) would give bits instead. Nothing about the guns is saved per ship
  but the cooldown, which isn't (it's in memory).
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - three guns a side at those places, the ports' size and height, no scuppers under them; the gun's size (2.75
    barrel, 0.14 bore, 9-lb shot)
  - 60 b/s, real gravity, the energy factor 20 (a close hit holes a carrack's side and goes on across her)
  - the fixed 1° elevation, an 8 s cooldown, anyone may fire (no crew roles yet), right-click only (no key)
  - the recoil (1.1 blocks, 0.18 s back, 0.6 s, 3.5 s out); the guns are solid where they stand run out (walk round
    them); running back pushes no one
  - creatures 0.5 HP a kJ; players only with PvP; blocks don't break by default; skipping off the water under 6°
  - `/magelan target` 60 blocks off
  - the gun block in the creative inventory, placeable, doing nothing

### 5. Wrecks

The user: "Wreck my mind was game blocks, but should not drop chiselled blocks but partial raw materials. Metal parts
could be recovered mostly or bits. Like bronze cannons could be recoverable." and "Sunken ships would create wreck and
treasures (place cargo hold chest and vessels on sea floor or inside the wreck)." Step 9 of the plan below. Offline
checks 406 → 436 (the 30 wreck checks alone with `-- wreck`), the server self-test 161 → 175, all pass. Config `Wrecks`
(on). None of it seen in the client: the wreck blocks' looks are only rendered (`docs/views/carrack-wreck-*.png`).

- **The wreck block** (`magelan:wreck`, `blocktypes/wreck.json`, `src/Ship/BlockWreck.cs`; its cells, faces and drops in
  `src/Ship/WreckCells.cs`): our own block, not a microblock. Its block entity keeps a 4 × 4 × 4 grid of 0.25 cells (her
  collision grid's size) as layers, a material and a 64-bit mask each (one ulong for a block of one material), and the
  wreck's id. Five materials: weathered oak (vanilla's aged planks), tarred oak (very aged planks, near black), pine
  (aged debarked wood), copper (tarnished copper, verdigris), iron (rusty iron).
  - collision and selection: its cells merged into boxes (exactly its cells, offline)
  - drawn by its block entity (client, unverified): each cell's faces that no cell is against (in the block, or in a
    wreck block beside it) merged into rectangles of one material, handed to the game with vanilla's own
    `ModelCubeUtilExt.AddFace` (as the shape tesselator and microblocks do), so the chunk lights it and culls its faces
    against solid neighbours like a JSON block's; each face shows the part of the texture its place on the block would
    on a vanilla cube, so neighbouring faces join up. Offline: the mesh's corners, atlas pieces and normals.
  - not chiselable (`canChisel: false`; the chisel only works on vanilla's own chiselled blocks and plain cubes), not in
    the creative inventory or the handbook; middle-click gives a plank; wood to the tools (an axe is quickest), metal
    where it's mostly copper or iron.
  - **breaking it** gives part of its raw materials (`WreckDrops`, a table in C#; also in the README): weathered oak 3 ± 1
    oak planks a full block, 15% a firewood, 20% 1–2 sticks; tarred oak 2.5 ± 1 planks, 10% firewood, 15% a stick, 12% a
    resin, 8% nails and strips; pine 3 ± 1 pine planks (and the same firewood and sticks); copper sheathing 0.35 ± 0.15
    copper plates and 2 ± 1 copper bits a full block; iron 2 ± 1 nails and strips and 5 ± 2 bits; a block of fewer
    cells that share (the fraction is the chance of one more). The whole carrack: about 1100 oak planks, 70 pine
    planks, 110 sticks, 60 firewood, 25 of her 48 copper plates and 150 copper bits, a few resin and nails.
- **The builder** (`WreckBuilder.Build(WreckInput, IWreckSite)` → `WreckPlan`, `src/Ship/Wreck.cs`, pure):
  - her design's cells: up to her castles (the masts over them, the tops and the bowsprit go); the outermost cell of
    her planking tarred (new `ShipPartMap.IsOuterSkin`), copper under her waterline + 0.75 if she was clad; the rest
    weathered oak; her masts pine up to a stump 0.5–1.5 over the main deck.
  - broken up, all from a seed (her entity id): planks gone by an evened-out noise (coarse along her length, so they go
    in runs) 3% anywhere, 13% her woodwork inside, over the main deck 17% rising 7% a block (her bulwarks about a third
    gone, the poop more); one castle fallen in (the stern castle or the forecastle: 85% from its deck up, a third of the
    walls under it); holes where `PartDamage` says she was holed (an ellipse on the panel's outer face, 0.5 × 0.35 to
    0.95 × 0.65 as she's stove in, ragged, through her planking, ceiling and frames), small ones in 40% of her cracked
    panels, holes in holed decks, and 2–4 panels of her sides stove in at random.
  - laid in the world: each world cell (0.25) takes her design cell its middle falls in, through her pose as she lies
    (her yaw, list and trim, as she went down), settled 0.3 into the seabed. Offline her cells are kept exactly at right
    angles, within 2% at others. Nothing in a ground block (rock, soil, sand, gravel, anyone's blocks, another wreck:
    anything with collision that isn't a plant) and nothing under the seabed's surface (the top of the first ground block
    down that column); plants give way; her own blocks from a run before are free; water stays (wreck blocks don't push
    it out). Lone cells go.
  - her masts above the stumps: two or three level pine timbers each (at most 24 blocks of a mast, tapering) on the
    seabed, from her side outward to the side she lies over to, across her give or take 40°, a gap and a kink between
    them. Her anchor (shank, stock, arms, flukes) in iron on the seabed outboard of her cathead (she always drops it
    going down).
  - places: each container where it stood (its block in her has at most 24 of her cells, which go; not buried), else
    the nearest free block on the seabed beside her; each gun on her deck if there's room for it and its 3-block barrel,
    facing out of its port, else tumbled onto the seabed 2.5 outboard of its port, facing anyhow; the extra chests on the
    seabed beside her middle on her low side; sand (the seabed's own block, only on sand or gravel) at up to 24 free
    blocks against her hull's lowest blocks, outside her.
- **On the server** (`ShipWrecks`, `src/Ship/ShipWrecks.cs`; `MagelanMod.Wrecks`):
  - `ShipWreck.Sunk` starts it; a look every second finds sunk ships that aren't wrecks yet (after a reload, or with
    `Wrecks` switched on since).
  - she's lowered till her hull samples meet the bottom (in case she stopped in sea that wasn't loaded); her wreck is
    built with the world as its site, which stops at an unloaded chunk (tried again 2 s later): so only with every chunk
    it touches loaded.
  - anyone (creature or player) whose box would be in her cells is moved up out of them.
  - her blocks go down bottom up, 400 a tick (the carrack's ~2100 in about half a second): `SetBlock` with a stack whose
    attributes carry the cells.
  - then in one tick: the registry entry (`magelanWrecks` in the save game: never two wrecks from one ship), her cargo
    taken out of her and she's removed (empty, nothing drops), her containers (a chest or storage vessel as itself with
    its contents; a trunk or crate: an aged chest for its contents, the trunk itself with the loose things), the loose
    things (the hold's big storage, lanterns, beds, the plate, copper plates of an unfinished sheathing) in aged chests,
    16 stacks a chest, her guns as `magelan:gun-bronze-<side>`, the sand; anything left (no room, or a failure) dropped
    beside her. A save in between finds her with her cargo and some of her blocks; begun again, the same seed and input
    lay the same wreck over them.
  - `/magelan wreck` (creative): the nearest sunk ship's wreck now; `/magelan wreck here [seed]`: a made-up carrack's
    wreck (holed along one side, heeled 8–28°, down by the head, sometimes clad, no cargo) on the seabed 24 blocks ahead.
- **Later, the Earth map's wrecks:** the builder needs no live ship. Make a `WreckInput` (a design from
  `MagelanMod.GetShip`, her origin at about `seabed − Grid.OY` so her keel rests on it, a yaw, list and trim, a hurt per
  damage part, a seed, the cargo fixtures to fill) and a loot list (`WreckLoot`: a fixture's container with its contents,
  or loose things), and call `ShipWrecks.Place` when the chunks there are loaded (it waits for them). Inside worldgen
  itself: `WreckBuilder.Build` with a site over the generating chunks, and the blocks set with the worldgen accessor and
  `BlockEntityWreck.Stack` (the block entity reads `stackForWorldgen` when it's initialised); the containers and guns as
  `ShipWrecks.Finish` places them.
- **Views** (`docs/views/carrack-wreck-overview`, `-port`, `-starboard`, `-stern`, `-deck`, `-hold`): `dotnet run
  --project tests/MagelanTests -c Release -- wreck-views` writes a wreck's faces as the client builds them (heeled 19° to
  starboard, 5° by the head, holed along her port side, on sand), `python tools/shipgen/texview.py wreck` renders them
  with the game's textures, her guns, chests and sand as plain boxes.
- **Choices for the user to confirm:**
  - she lies as she went down (heeled up to 30° and 6° by the head), not upright; settled 0.3 into the seabed
  - the decay: about a quarter of her design gone (3% anywhere, more inside and up high), one castle fallen in, 2–4 random
    breaches; the masts as 2–3 timbers (at most 24 blocks of each), fallen to her low side
  - the materials and their textures (aged planks inside, very aged planks for the tar, verdigris copper, rusty iron);
    the decks plain weathered oak (not the caulked look)
  - the drop numbers above (copper: about half the plates back and bits); the yield doesn't depend on the tool (an axe is
    only quicker)
  - "the seabed's surface" is the first ground block down each column, sand and gravel included (she never replaces
    them: cells under it are left out); only plants give way. Sand heaped against her only where the seabed is sand or
    gravel
  - chests and vessels go down as themselves; a trunk or crate's contents go into an aged chest and the trunk or crate
    with the loose things; the loose things in aged (`normal-aged`) chests; copper plates of an unfinished sheathing come
    back as plates (a finished sheathing is copper cells)
  - her guns mostly tumble onto the seabed (on a heeled wreck there's rarely room on her deck); a tumbled gun faces
    anyhow
  - the anchor always lies by her bow (she drops it going down)
  - the wreck is made about a second after she's on the bottom, even with players watching (her entity vanishes as her
    blocks appear)
  - `/magelan wreck here` for looking at wrecks without sinking a ship

## Damage, sinking and wrecks (discussed 2026-09-30; steps 1–10 built overnight in 0.3.5: 3a, 3b and 5 above)

The user asked whether a damage model is feasible: cannons, sea monsters beaten with them, sunken ships leaving a wreck
with the hold's chests and vessels as treasure, and maintenance and repairs that matter. Decided by the user:
- **Order:** damage and sinking first (grounding, then cannon damage), cannons (item 16) next, sea monsters last.
- **Damage shows gradually:** first in the textures and in what you read when you look at a spot, at the end real holes
  (smooth edges are fine).
- **Wrecks are world blocks.** Breaking them gives part of the raw materials back, not chiselled blocks: timber as
  planks or firewood, metal (nails, fittings, copper sheathing, the anchor) mostly whole or as bits. Bronze guns can be
  recovered whole.

Two engine issues come first; both were read in the 1.22.7 source.

**A. The crosshair doesn't catch the hull** (the user: important for placing things and for inspecting). **Built
overnight (0.3.5), see above.** Why:
- `EntityShip.IsInteractable` is false, so the client's selection ray trace skips her.
- Even when true, vanilla's `Entity.IntersectsRay` tests one box, `SelectionBox` (6 × 4 about the centre).
- `GameMain.RayTraceForSelection` gets its candidates from `GetEntitiesAround(eye, range, range)`, which walks only the
  chunks within reach of the eye and keeps entities whose centre is in range (`InRangeOf`). A carrack's centre is often
  10–15 blocks away, and may be in the next chunk.
- The look-at box (`HudElementBlockAndEntityInfo.EntityInView`) is rebuilt only when the entity changes, not when you
  look at another part of her.
- Server: vanilla's entity-interaction packet looks for the entity within PickingRange + 10 of the player and checks
  `IsInInteractionRangeOf`; from her ends a big ship fails that. So clicks keep going through our own packets.

Fix:
- Override `EntityShip.IntersectsRay`: raycast the voxel grid in her frame (tilted on clients), set the tester's
  `hitPosition` and `hitOnBlockFace`, return the real distance, and the panel under the crosshair as
  `selectionBoxIndex`.
- `IsInteractable` true on the client only. On the server it would let arrows hit her centre box
  (`EntityProjectile`) and creature AI see her.
- A client postfix on `GameMain.RayTraceForSelection(IWorldIntersectionSupplier, Ray, …)` adds the ships the chunk walk
  missed.
- The look-at box is rebuilt when the panel changes (a postfix on `EntityInView`), and `GetInfoText` says what's there.
- Vanilla itself then stops selecting blocks behind the hull. The "clicks through the deck" guard in `OnInWorldAction`
  stays as a fallback.
- Placing things aboard (under "Later") needs this too.

**B. A spawn point on the ship** (the user wants it). **Built overnight (0.3.5) with the plate by the helm and no
roles, see above; the ship's log, crew roles and temporal gears are still to come.** Vanilla:
- Only a temporal gear (`ItemTemporalGear`) sets a player's spawn: `IServerPlayer.SetSpawnPosition` with the block the
  player stands in, `temporalGearRespawnUses` uses. Beds don't set spawn.
- On respawn the server's own handler teleports to `GetSpawnPosition` first (`server.EventManager`). Mods'
  `PlayerRespawn` handlers run after it (`modEventManager`).
- Used aboard now, the spawn stays at a world spot the ship sails away from, so you'd respawn in the sea.

Plan:
- Bind the spawn to the ship (her entity id and a bunk), kept in the player's mod data together with the spawn they
  had before.
- Keep vanilla's spawn position following her bunk, updated every few seconds while she moves. Then the respawn
  teleport loads the chunks where she is.
- After that teleport, the mod's `PlayerRespawn` handler puts the player aboard at the bunk.
- If she's gone (sunk, picked up), the old spawn comes back and the player is told.

The user's ideas (2026-09-30): "temporal gear on the player's bed", or "placing a temporal gear in the ship's log to
register passengers bound to the ship". The user needs a way to set the crew anyway, for multiplayer and access.
Proposed, not confirmed yet:
- **The ship's log:** a fixture, vanilla's big open book (`clutter/book-big-open`) on the great cabin's table. Right-click
  opens a dialog with her name, her captain, and the roster: each player's role and berth.
- **The captain** is whoever launched her from the charter (stored at launch). Ships already afloat get their first
  captain when someone first opens the log. The captain can hand her over.
- **Signing on:** a temporal gear used on a bed aboard (held right-click, as vanilla uses the gear) is spent. The bed
  becomes that player's berth and their spawn, and it signs them on as a passenger in the log. The user's other idea,
  the gear put into the log (spawn at a general spot, or at a berth the captain gives), is the alternative.
- **Roles:**
  - captain: everything, including the roster, the name, handing her over and picking her up
  - officer: steer, set sail, anchor, the hold, and signing on crew
  - crew: sails, anchor, capstan, pumps, repairs, guns, the hold
  - passenger: board, their own berth
  - stranger: board only if the captain leaves her open, and touch nothing
- A vanilla player group can be named as the crew, reusing `/group`.
- **Checked on the server:** every fixture packet (helm, sails, hatches, storage, attach, pickup, name board, copper)
  goes through one access check. Admins pass, and in singleplayer everything is open.
- **Sinking:** the log goes into the wreck.

**The damage model, in steps:**
1. **Panels and inspection.** Built overnight (0.3.5): 3a above.
   - The unit is a hull panel: the planking between two frames (1.5) × a band of strakes (about 1 block), each side.
     The deck, the rudder, each mast, yard and sail, and the rigging are parts of their own.
   - The carrack has roughly 250 hull panels: one byte each (sound, worn, scraped, cracked, leaking, holed), synced as
     a watched attribute.
   - Looking at a panel tells its state.
   - A test command, `/magelan damage`, hurts a spot.
2. **Grounding and collisions.** (Built overnight, 0.3.5: 3a above.) The speed at impact and the hull points that touched decide which bottom panels get
   scraped or cracked. Ramming another ship damages both at the contact.
3. **Leaks and the water inside.** Built overnight (0.3.5): 3a above.
   - Cracked and holed panels below the waterline let water in (area × √depth). Seams leak a little more as the hull
     ages (maintenance).
   - The water sits her deeper (volume ÷ waterplane area), runs to the low side (a list, and she rolls sluggishly),
     and slows her.
   - Carracks had no watertight bulkheads: one bilge.
   - When the main deck's edge or an open hatch goes under a wave, water pours in and she sinks.
   - The helm's HUD shows the water in the hold.
4. **Pumps.** (Built overnight, 0.3.5, on the pump already there: 3a above.) A pump by the mainmast (a wooden tube and a brake handle, a simple mesh of our own), worked with held
   right-click. More pumps and more players pump faster.
5. **The water shows inside (Sea + Magelan).** Built overnight (0.3.5, Sea 0.3.3): 3b above.
   - Sea's dry cut stops at the flood level (`FloodY` per clip).
   - Magelan draws a water surface inside the hull that stays level while she rolls.
   - Below it `IsDry` is false (breath, underwater view).
6. **Repairs** with vanilla items, at the panel (built overnight, 0.3.5: 3a above):
   - planks with nails and strips for a cracked or holed panel
   - flax fibres with resin to caulk seams, which stops leaks
   - sail or cloth for sails
   - rope for the rigging
   - Holes below the water are mended from inside.
7. **Textures.** Built overnight (0.3.5): 3b above.
   - Panels are drawn with a worn, scraped or cracked overlay (a composite like the tar).
   - The client rebuilds the hull mesh from its quads when a panel changes; each quad belongs to the panel its centre is
     in.
8. **Sinking.** (Built overnight, 0.3.5: 3a above; the wreck's hook is `ShipWreck.OnSunk`.) She goes down over some tens of seconds, settling and listing. Passengers come off and swim, and the
   rowing boats on the gallows float free.
9. **The wreck: world blocks on the seabed.** Built overnight (0.3.5): 5 above.
   - Our own wreck block: it looks like the chiselled hull (built from the design's grid, broken up), drops raw
     materials when broken, and can't be chiselled.
   - The hold's cargo goes into vanilla chests and storage vessels in the wreck, with some spilled around it.
   - Guns: bronze ones lie there to be recovered whole, iron ones come back mostly as bits.
   - The same generator can later place wrecks with loot on the Earth map.
10. **Real holes.** Built overnight (0.3.5): 3b above (the collision stays whole).
    - shipgen splits the planking at the panels.
    - A holed panel's quads are cut out with a smooth-edged opening and its reveal (the planks' thickness), like the
      window cuts now.
    - The collision grid stays whole, so you can't climb through a hole, unless the user wants that.

Then the cannons (item 16; built overnight in 0.3.5, 4 above) and, last, the sea monsters.

## Build

- **Done 2026-09-29.** Release zips carry the version from modinfo.json (`release/magelan-0.2.0.zip`,
  `sea-0.2.0.zip`, `cylinderworld-0.11.0.zip`, `earthmap-0.8.0.zip`); a build removes the older zips from `release/`.
  The READMEs, the self-test scripts, Cylinder World's polar smoke test and Wmap's smoke runner use the new names.
